Now a title matching `^(?P<head>.+? vs\.? .+?) - (?P<seg>[A-Za-z ]+)$` whose segment is one of the feed's three attested family segments — `Exact Score`, `Halftime Result`, `More Markets`, a CLOSED list (`_EVENT_TITLE_SEGMENTS`) — keys the HEAD alone (the same `a vs b@date` / `b vs a@date` keys the plain title yields, byte for byte — the plain title's keys are unchanged) and `_yn_event_sides` reads the same head; the segment builds no key, a title naming one club builds no matchup key, and no bare-date key exists (the `league_alias_probe` rule stands). The list is closed because `_yn_event_sides` is a WITNESS (the alias arm, the draw, C5 decide on it): the review's CRITICAL found that a regex admitting any trailing word let `Arsenal FC vs. Chelsea FC - Women` / `- Reserves` / `- Aggregate` witness the men's full-game rows (`premap_alias wsl->epl`), the wrong-event class the bridge's scope belt exists for; any other suffix now stays in the title, keys nothing and witnesses nothing, exactly as before (`tests/test_m1_review_pins.py` §A). His exact-score market title `Exact Score: <A> X - Y <B>?` (the feed's one shape) keys the two clubs as the matchup, never the score. The exact-score and halftime rows therefore now reach the venue's rows and refuse by the family name (`unknown_market_type:family_not_listed`, `exact:family-absent` / `halftime:family-absent`) — the lanes are separate builds; nothing maps here.

**The empty prefix filter (`resolve_explain` only; `resolve` untouched).** `type_prefix_filter_emptied` was documented as a fast-lane latency symptom; `gap_prefix_filter.md` proved a second class: leagues the venue lists ONLY moneylines for (bra, lpa, ekst, scp, srb, swsl, sld — 6 `atc` rows per game, zero `tsc`/`asc`/`astatc` rows anywhere in the table; ~$82k/24 h of his totals and spreads counted as a mapper gap, recoverable by no rule). When `kept` empties on a C3 family (behind the switch, like every C3 reading), `_kind_absent_probe` names it from the venue's own rows, never a table: the league code(s) the board's identifiers carry for this game (`_C3_IDENT_RE` `lg`), then one read-only probe per code — `SELECT 1 FROM us_premap WHERE identifier LIKE '<kind>-<lg>-%' LIMIT 1` — a row is `<family>:kind-absent:league-listed` (the venue lists the family in that league, not yet on this game: the latency case), none is `<family>:kind-absent:league-unlisted` (structural, unmappable at this venue today). Names: `total:kind-absent:league-unlisted`, `total:kind-absent:league-listed`, `spread:kind-absent:…`, `btts:kind-absent:…`; sibling of `total:segment-absent` (which needs a `tsc` row on another segment — a first-half total on an `atc`-only board is `kind-absent`). The step name stays (the census buckets on it; `explain_unmapped` prints `type_prefix_filter_emptied:total:kind-absent:league-unlisted`); the probe runs only on the refusal, like `league_alias_probe`, and is recorded in `kind_probe` (`kind`, `league_codes`, `asked`, `listed`; a failed probe or an unreadable board leaves the bare `<family>:kind-absent` with `error`). The refusal returns before `c3_same_segment` / `c3_pick` can see the rows, so the probe widens nothing (pinned: `resolve` is None in every case; the counterfactual `tsc-lpa-rac-cat-2026-09-06-2pt5` row makes the same slug `resolves` with no code change).

Every C3 hit is source `premap` (`QUARANTINE_RESUME_SRC`'s own class; the copy lane's premap step answers with the same rows, so the copy lane's spreads/btts/fh totals now map at premap before its exact lane — the copy lane's `prop` block still refuses a first-half total there). `resolve_explain` carries `family`, `c3` (the family trace), `matched_by`, and the family refusal as the step's `split`, so MIRRORUNMAP prints `no_side_match:spread:line-absent` etc.; the not-listed families keep `unknown_market_type:family_not_listed` (pinned) with `refusal` naming the family. Left out: exact score / halftime result / team totals (not on the venue: refused by name, an orientation rule is unwritten until a soccer `es-` row is observed); cfb spreads whose venue row names nicknames (identity cannot read them — the 1h/1q rows of washst-wash name the schools but a full-game slug never crosses a segment); segmented spreads and second-half families are supported by grammar but unattested in his feed; `_norm`, `side_intent`, `match_side`'s branches and `market_type_of` are unchanged; the live worker's family gate reads `market_type_of` as before (a first-half total is admitted as `prop`, which `MIRROR_FAMILIES` carries).


## 10. C4 (2026-09-06 22:2xZ) — college football spreads whose venue rows name mascots; the multi-word school code

Owner 19:33Z: "I need more trades firing in the mirror sleeve!" Read at 22:22Z (`his-recent_2222.log`): in the two hours to 22:21Z RN1 filled 198 markets / $460k; the C3 families map (soccer totals, draws, the cfb totals `tsc-cfb-washst-wash-…-total-34pt5` $42k on a book). Still refused: `cfb-washst-wash-2026-09-06-spread-home-<L>` (outcome "Washington State", title "Spread: Washington (-L)") $7.5k + $3.8k + $1.3k + $0.3k + $0.1k as `no_side_match:spread:names-unreadable`, and the moneyline `cfb-scarst-flam-2026-09-06` (outcome "South Carolina State") $1.0k as `side_code_unmatched`. Patch C4 against 566094a; tests in `backend/tests/test_c4_cfb_spreads.py` (the dumps' rows verbatim). Identity only: no nickname dictionary, no similarity, no home/away, and no positional inference beyond what the venue's own rows certify.

### 10.1 What the venue's rows say (`c3_rows_2038.log`)

The SAME game is named by mascots on some `asc-` rows and by schools on others, per row: `asc-cfb-washst-wash-…-neg-24pt5` "Will the Cougars cover -24.5 vs the Huskies in Cougars vs. Huskies?" beside `asc-cfb-washst-wash-…-1h-pos-3pt5` "Will the Washington State cover 3.5 vs the Washington in Washington State vs. Washington?"; `asc-cfb-scarst-flam-…-neg-21pt5` "Bulldogs … Rattlers" beside `…-neg-22pt5` "South Carolina State … Florida A&M". The moneyline rows list the mascots as sides: `aec-cfb-washst-wash-…` cougars (BUY_LONG) / huskies (BUY_SHORT), `aec-cfb-scarst-flam-…` bulldogs / rattlers. No row on either event names a mascot AND its school; the venue's per-side contracts (`atc-cfb-…-<code>`) list none for these two games (the dumps carry only the `atc-cfb-washst-wash-…-dstd/fg/rec/…` prop rows), and where the probes do list one (`atc-cfb-bayl-aubrn-2026-09-05-winner-2h-bayl` "Will Baylor win the second half?") its question names the school. So a mascot is tied to a slug position ONLY by the aec side order — which is exactly the C1 grammar class's claim (§7.2: "what remains unproven at mapping time — that marketSides[i] is the team at slug position i") and exactly what its certification checks per market (§7.7 step 1: the venue's per-side contract for code i must name the aec side description).

### 10.2 The chain (`premap._c4_subject_by_code`, `_c4_subject_certified`, `map_lane.code_reads`)

Runs inside `_c3_pick_spread` ONLY when C3's name step reads nothing (his outcome name-matches neither venue name) AND neither venue name names a code (a school-named row that does not match his outcome keeps C3's `spread:names-unreadable`). Everything before it (line byte-equal, whole-number, segment, title shape/shear) and after it (the sign/complement table, the intent, the sign shear) is C3 unchanged.

1. **Codes.** `cfb-washst-wash` on both feeds: a = washst, b = wash (the C3 key: league, a, b, date, segment, line).
2. **His team and his title's team, by the code** (`map_lane.code_reads`): the C1 whole-word/first-word rule, the C3 stem rule ("Washington State" → washst by `wash`+`st`, "Washington" → wash), and — only where those name NEITHER code — the C4 split rule (10.4). Exactly one code, else `spread:code-unmatched` (trace `code_hits`); the title's team the same way, else `spread:title-unreadable` (trace `title_hits`). **The pair witness (review fold, 10.7):** the two must be the two DIFFERENT positions — two different names on one code is `spread:code-collision`, a title naming his outcome's own team (one name, read twice) is `spread:pair-unwitnessed`; both refused before any certification is read.
3. **The subject.** C3's grammar has a in the subject slot; on a mascot row the subject's identity is a POSITION, certified only by the venue's own rows on this event: the moneyline row `aec-<lg>-<a>-<b>-<date>` must be on the board with exactly the question's two names as its sides (a set — its ORDER is never read), and the C1 grammar class's state (`ingestion_state.mirror_grammar_echo`, read by `premap.grammar_cert` in `resolve`/`resolve_explain`, spreads only) must be readable, not tripped, and hold a VENUE-TRUTH record for that aec market — `certified[aec slug] = {outcome_desc, side_index, his_slug}`, written by `mirror_live._grammar_admission` when `map_lane.grammar_truth` says ok (the venue's per-side contract for the code named that mascot; kept after the fill echo pops `pending`, bounded at 200). The record's mascot must be one of the question's names, and its slot in the question (0 subject, 1 opponent) must equal its certified code position: a record placing the subject's mascot at b (or the opponent's at a) is `spread:subject-conflict` — the grammar never has b as the subject, so that is a contradiction, never a map. Either side's record certifies the pair (Huskies at wash puts Cougars at washst by elimination: two teams). Anything short of this — aec row absent (`aec-absent`), other names (`aec-names`), state unreadable (`grammar-unreadable`), tripped or `mismatch > 0` (`grammar-tripped`), no record for this event (`grammar-uncertified`), a record of another shape or another event (`record-shape`) — is `spread:subject-uncertified`, the reason in the trace's `subject`.
4. C3's table: his team a with −L → `neg-L` yes BUY_LONG; a with +L → `pos-L` yes; b with −L → `pos-L` no BUY_SHORT; b with +L → `neg-L` no. matched_by `premap_spread_code` (trace `subject_via: code`); source `premap`; behind `PREMAP_YN_IDENTITY` with the rest of C3.

**What the certification says, and whether it is on tonight.** The C1 class does not certify a convention ("side 0 is slug team a for cfb"); it certifies ONE aec market at a time, against venue-only truth, before a grammar book opens (§7.7). At 22:22Z `mirror_grammar_echo` reads ok=0, mismatch=0, `grammar_echo_ok`=0, `grammar_probation`=0: no grammar book has opened, nothing is certified, and for these two games the venue lists no per-side contract in `us_premap` (the live fallback — his own code's `atc-cfb-washst-wash-…-washst` as the one candidate the venue must confirm — is a live read only the worker makes). So tonight every one of the five washst-wash spreads refuses `spread:subject-uncertified` (`grammar-uncertified`); they map the tick after the live worker certifies `aec-cfb-washst-wash-2026-09-06` (venue truth ok on its moneyline), and stay refused while nothing does. That is the fail-closed reading of the brief's step 3: the mascot→position tie is trusted only where the venue's own contract has confirmed it for this very market.

### 10.3 Refusal names (census: `no_side_match:<split>`)

`spread:code-unmatched` (his outcome names neither or both codes), `spread:title-unreadable` (existing name; now also his title's team naming neither/both codes on a mascot row), `spread:code-collision` and `spread:pair-unwitnessed` (10.7, the pair witness), `spread:subject-uncertified` (10.2 step 3, with `c3.subject` naming which leg), `spread:subject-conflict`. Every C3 spread refusal is unchanged; `spread:names-unreadable` now stands only where a venue name names a code (the school-named rows) or his title/outcome name the mascot but not the school (C3's own name path).

### 10.4 The multi-word school code (`map_lane.code_words`, folded into `code_reads`)

`code_hits("scarst", "South Carolina State")` is False (first word "south" neither starts with nor prefixes "scarst"), so the moneyline refused `side_code_unmatched`. The rule: the code is the concatenation of NON-EMPTY PREFIXES of CONSECUTIVE words of the outcome, from its first word, EVERY word used in order, the split UNIQUE, and the outcome at least two words; "&" inside a word is dropped before splitting ("A&M" → "am"). `scarst` = s|car|st, `flam` = fl|am, `washst` = wash|st, `michst` = mich|st, `ohiost` = ohio|st, `tam` = t|am. Refused: a split that skips a word (`tex` over Texas|Tech leaves Tech unused — pinned as the NEGATIVE; `code_hits("tex", "Texas Tech")` keeps its C1 verdict, a first-word hit), two splits (`abab` over Aba|Bab), a single word (the first-word rule owns it: `aubrn` never reaches Auburn), codes under three letters. `code_reads(code, outcome, sibling)` is `code_names` wherever `code_names` names EITHER of the slug's codes, and the split rule only where it names neither — so `mist` on "Michigan State" beside `mich` stays unnamed (mich is named first; C1's pin), every C1/C3 verdict stands, and the split rule turns a "names neither" into a hit and nothing else. `aec_code_side`'s hits, its venue-side and title-order checks, and `pair_agrees` read `code_reads`. `cfb-scarst-flam-2026-09-06` outcome "South Carolina State" (index 0) now maps to `aec-cfb-scarst-flam-2026-09-06` side 0 Bulldogs BUY_LONG as `grammar`; "Florida A&M" (index 1) is Rattlers BUY_SHORT; the pair agrees both ways; live it answers to the grammar class's certification as every grammar book does (the venue lists no per-side contract for this game in the dumps: `grammar_echo_unverified`).

### 10.5 Expectation table (the six rows of `his-recent_2222.log`, offline against the 2038 dump's rows, `PREMAP_YN_IDENTITY=on`)

### 10.6 What this does NOT do (left out, by design)

- No dictionary, no fuzzy step, no price evidence in code: `neg-24pt5` "Cougars cover -24.5" trading LONG 0.01 while the title says Washington −23.5 corroborates Cougars = Washington State to a reader, and the code never reads it.
- The same-event school-named rows (the 1h/1q rows put "Washington State" in the subject slot of the same identifier head) are not used to certify the full-game rows' subject: that would be a positional inference across rows (that the venue keeps the same team in the subject slot on every segment), which no venue row certifies.
- No offline read of the per-side contract rows: where `us_premap` lists `atc-cfb-<a>-<b>-<date>-<code>`, its question names the SCHOOL (the probes' `-winner-2h-bayl` "Will Baylor win the second half?"), not the mascot, so it cannot tie a mascot to a code from the table; the mascot field lives only in the live contract payload `grammar_truth` reads. The C1 class's own record is therefore the one certification, and it is per market.
- The class-level counters (`ok >= 1` on some other game) never certify this game: the C1 class opens no grammar book without per-market venue truth, and the spread inherits exactly that bar, not a weaker one.
- `live_executor._side_echo_verify` (the copy lane's post-fill echo; entry OFF by owner order, §9.8) re-derives a spread through `c3_pick` on the bought market's live rows alone — no aec row, no state — so a `premap_spread_code` fill reads `no unique live match` → `unverified`, never a false mismatch. Wiring the board and the record into that echo is the same named follow-up as §9.8's.
- The C3 pin `test_nicknames_on_the_venue_row_are_unreadable` asserted the pre-C4 refusal name on exactly these rows; its `None` result and `venue_names` assertion stand, its expected split is now `spread:subject-uncertified` (`aec-absent` on that board). One C1 round-3 pin moved with the fold (10.7, the certification fix); no other C1/C2/C3 pin moved.

### 10.7 Review fold (2026-09-06 23:3xZ) — the pair witness; the per-side contract's suffix

**The collision rule (HIGH-1, 2026-09-06 23:3xZ).** The chain resolved his outcome and his title's team independently by `code_reads`, and the code rules read each name ALONE: a first-word prefix names one code for two schools — "Ole Miss" (whole word) and "Mississippi State" (first word) both read `miss`, and `msst` is named by nothing; likewise `mich` for "Michigan"/"Michigan State", `ala` for "Alabama"/"Alabama A&M", `tex` for "Texas"/"Texas A&M", `ark` for "Arkansas"/"Arkansas State" (a one-letter residual is never the stem rule's), `flor` for "Florida"/"Florida A&M" (the split rule for `flam` is never reached once the first-word rule names the other code), and the whole-word rule collides too ("Southern Miss" reads `miss` beside `smiss`). Both names landed on position 0, the title's sign was not flipped, and "Spread: Ole Miss (-7.5)" / "Mississippi State" mapped to `neg-7pt5` yes — the Rebels' side of a Mississippi State fill. The fix is the same bar C1 holds on the moneyline (§7.8: an unnamed sibling corroborates nothing): `_c4_subject_by_code` requires a PAIR WITNESS — his outcome and his title's team must resolve to the two DIFFERENT positions — before anything else is read. Two different names on one position is `spread:code-collision` (every case above, pinned in `TestThePairWitness`); a title naming his outcome's own team ("Spread: Washington State (-23.5)" / "Washington State", "Spread: Washington (-2.5)" / "Washington") carries no second name and is `spread:pair-unwitnessed` — no single name can exclude the collision ("Mississippi State" alone reads `miss` and would buy the Rebels' side), so the four same-name rows of C3's sign table and the two small "Washington" rows of 10.5 now refuse, on every game, including washst-wash where the stem rule happens to read one name right. The four rows where his title names the OTHER team (the $7.5k / $3.8k / $1.3k / $0.3k / $0.1k rows are all of that shape) map exactly as 10.5 says; two names on two positions map on every game the rules can read (scarst/flam on the mascot rows, mich/ohiost). Both refusals are by name, before the state (an unreadable state changes nothing), and carry `code_hits` / `title_hits` in the trace. Not folded: a second name from his event title ("Washington State vs. Washington") could witness the same-name rows the way C2's yes/no branch reads `_yn_event_sides`; his event title is not in any spread row this session logged (his-recent prints no such column), so it stays a named follow-up rather than a rule built on an unobserved field.

**The deviation's real coverage cost (v3, 2026-09-07).** A fill on the FAVOURITE's token has outcome == the title's team by construction ("Spread: South Carolina State (-19.5)" / "South Carolina State", $35k at 20:28Z, is that shape), so on a mascot row every favourite-side fill refuses `spread:pair-unwitnessed`; only underdog-side fills (title names the favourite, outcome the dog: the five washst-wash rows) map through this chain. Tonight's direct cost is the two "Washington" rows, $128 (the S.C. State row was `spread:line-absent` regardless: 19.5 is not listed). School-named venue rows are untouched by this (C3's name path). **A known limit (LOW-1, v2 review).** The witness assumes his outcome and his title render a school the same way. Mixed renderings in one row — "Louisiana Tech" / "LA Tech", "UNC" / "North Carolina", "LSU" / "Louisiana State", "UCF" / "Central Florida", "Miami (OH)" / "Miami Ohio" — can read two DIFFERENT positions for one school ("Louisiana Tech" reads the opponent's `lou` by its first word, "LA Tech" reads `latech` by the split rule) and pass the witness with his team at the wrong position. A single feed market renders a team one way, so this is a feed-shape assumption, not an observed path; the event-title witness above (two names of his own, both read against the two codes) is the named follow-up that would close it, and until then the census key to watch is `premap_spread_code` fills whose `code_hits` and `title_hits` name a school the feed also spells another way.

**The certification fix (HIGH-2, 2026-09-06 23:3xZ; inherited from C1 round 3, load-bearing since C4).** `mirror_live._contract_candidates` admitted a per-side contract by its QUESTION alone: with only `atc-cfb-washst-wash-2026-09-06-wash` "Will the Cougars win?" in `us_premap`, `_contract_candidates(…, i=0, "Cougars", "Huskies")` returned that row (suffix `wash`, the OTHER code), `grammar_truth` read the same contract naming "Cougars" and said ok, and the class would have written `certified[aec] = {Cougars, 0}` — every washst-wash spread would then place the Cougars at washst, and the C1 moneyline would buy side 0 for a Washington State fill, on the venue's row for wash. Now `by_question` also requires that the suffix does NOT fit the other side: a suffix that IS the other code, or prefixes it (whether or not it prefixes ours too, `bo` for boise/bost), is never ours by its question. Pinned with exactly that case (`TestTheContractSuffixIsNeverTheOtherSides`): the candidates are `[]`, `_grammar_admission` answers `grammar_echo_unverified` with ok=0 and nothing certified; and for Huskies at wash (i=1) the wash row is the code's own contract by code, its name "Cougars" is the OTHER side's, and the truth check trips — the venue's own contradiction, caught before a dollar moves. The by-code fits (his code, a prefix of his code and of no other, the venue's short code `bu`/`h` by its question) are unchanged. One C1 round-3 pin moved: `test_a_question_naming_both_sides_is_not_our_contract` asserted that, without the other side's description, BOTH `…-bu` "Will the Bears win against the Tigers?" and `…-au` "Will the Tigers win against the Bears?" fit Baylor's side by question (`len == 2`); the `au` row's suffix prefixes `aubrn` — it is the other side's contract, the hole in miniature — so the line now reads `== [base + "bu"]`; every other assertion of that pin, and every other C1/C2/C3 pin, is unchanged. Two smaller items from the same review: the class-level counters never certify a market (mutant c: `_state(ok=5)` with no record is `spread:subject-uncertified` / `grammar-uncertified`, pinned in `test_the_class_counters_certify_nothing`), and `certified` is written through `_grammar_certify`, which pops a re-certified key before re-inserting it so tonight's certification is the newest record and the oldest is evicted (pinned in `test_a_market_certified_again_is_the_newest_record` and the live-worker record test).

**v3 (2026-09-07), the re-review's LOWs.** LOW-2, the wrong-side guard on a re-certification: `_grammar_admission` reads the record it already holds for the aec market against tonight's venue-truth reading (`_grammar_contradiction`): the SAME mascot at the OTHER position, or ANOTHER mascot at THIS position (Huskies at washst after Cougars at washst), is the venue contradicting itself — a wrong side one of the two times — and is a `mismatch` like any other: the class trips, `side_echo_mismatch`, the old record is kept, `last.detail` names both readings; it replaced the record silently before. The same reading is a refresh, and the complementary side (Huskies at wash after Cougars at washst) is the pair itself, consistent — the reviewer's literal rule (any change of `(outcome_desc, side_index)`) would trip on that, so the rule is the contradiction, not the change (pinned both ways in `TestAContradictingRecertificationTrips`). LOW-3, `fits_other` is symmetric: a suffix that EXTENDS the other code (`texas` beside `tex`) is not ours by its question either; beside HIS code the same suffix is the venue's longer rendering and fits by its question (pinned in `test_the_other_codes_contract_never_fits_by_its_question`).


## 11. C5 (2026-09-07) — a differing TEAM code is translated by the venue's own question, per game

Owner standing orders: "map more of his trades", "mirror a larger percentage of RN1's positions"; identity-only mapping, fail closed, never map on a guess. The gap, read from the venue's own rows (render-ops `epl-rows`, 2026-09-07 00:05Z, `scratchpad/hard2/epl_rows_0004.log`; the investigation in `epl_unmapped.md`):

| his feed (`trades`) | the venue (`us_premap`) |
|---|---|
| `epl-eve-mun-2026-09-06-mun` — "Will Manchester United FC win on 2026-09-06?" ($91k in 6 h) | `atc-epl-eve-mnu-2026-09-06-mnu` yes/no, LONG/SHORT, line empty — "Will Manchester United FC win against Everton FC in the Premier League match scheduled for Sep 6, 2026?" |
| `epl-eve-mun-2026-09-06-eve` — "Will Everton FC win on 2026-09-06?" | `atc-epl-eve-mnu-2026-09-06-eve` — "Will Everton FC win against Manchester United FC in the Premier League match scheduled for Sep 6, 2026?" |
| `…-draw`, `…-total-2pt5`, `…-btts`, `…-spread-away-1pt5` | `atc-epl-eve-mnu-…-draw` "Will the Premier League match Everton FC vs Manchester United FC scheduled for Sep 6, 2026 end in a draw?"; `tsc-epl-eve-mnu-2026-09-06-2pt5` "Will the total in EVE vs MNU be more than 2.5?"; `astatc-epl-eve-mnu-…-btts` "Will both teams score in the match between Everton FC and Manchester United FC on 2026-09-06 9:00AM ET?"; `asc-epl-eve-mnu-…-pos-1pt5` "Will the Everton FC cover 1.5 vs the Manchester United FC in EVE vs MNU?"; exact scores "Will EVE vs MNU finish EVE wins 2-1?" |
| `epl-ars-che-2026-09-06-che` / `-ars` ($118k) | `atc-epl-ars-cfc-2026-09-06-cfc` "Will Chelsea FC win against Arsenal FC in the Premier League match scheduled for Sep 6, 2026?" / `-ars`; `tsc-epl-ars-cfc-…-0pt5` … |

League code equal (`epl`), date equal, ONE team code equal in the same position (`eve`, `ars`), the OTHER differs per club (`mun`/`mnu`, `che`/`cfc`). The identity arm (§8) reads `yn:no-row` on every family of both events because `atc-` + his slug names nothing; C3's identifier builders (`_c3_code`) read `btts:no-row` / `spread:line-absent` / `total:line-absent` for the same reason; only the full-game total mapped, through the wording arm (his title's matchup keys fetch the event, the line is unique on the segment). Serie A (`bol`/`sas`) and Ligue 1 (`fl1->lg1`) had matched: this is a per-club code difference, not a league one. Patch C5 against 43b453c (the branch HEAD; 885a8c6 + two render-ops presets); tests in `backend/tests/test_c5_code_translation.py` (the two games' rows embedded verbatim).

### 11.1 The rule (`premap.c5_certify`, `premap.c5_resolve`) — a translation certified by the venue's question, per event, never a table

1. **Trigger.** His slug parses as `<lg>-<a>-<b>-<date>-<rest>` (any family: the moneyline's `rest` is a team code, the draw's `draw`, C3's `total-2pt5` / `btts` / `spread-away-1pt5` / `first-half-total-1pt5`). Among the venue's FULL-GAME per-team moneyline rows on his date (`atc-<LG>-<va>-<vb>-<date>-<t>`, `t ∈ {va, vb}`; half rows, draws, exact scores never count) no event carries BOTH his codes — his own event on the board, mapped or refused, belongs to the identity/alias arms and is never translated around — and EXACTLY ONE event shares exactly one of his codes in the SAME position (home stays home) under his league code; with none under his code, under exactly one other code (the C2 alias, then witnessed on both clubs). Two candidate events (a same-day twin listing) or two league codes: `yn:code-translate:ambiguous`. Zero: today's reason stands, no C5 trace. A lone per-team row (the other never named it): today's reason.
2. **Witness.** Both the shared code's row and the unshared code's row must be the venue's per-team contract — a yes row and a no row with the venue's own intents, one question, no line, no sign (`_c5_sided`) — and each question must fullmatch the measured P4 template on his date through the reading C2 uses (`_yn_team_question`, factored out of `_yn_team_row` unchanged: fold, `pmus._YN_Q_PATTERNS`, `_yn_date_ok`, the club scope screen on subject and opponent, the league rule on the league slot). The two rows must NAME EACH OTHER (`pmus._yn_name_match` both ways). Then HIS OWN WORDS must name the unshared club: his title when this market is that club's moneyline (`_bridge_title_subject`, C2's title witness — the same normalisation: case, `pmus.fold_latin`, the pinned `GENERIC_CLUB_TOKENS`); else his moneyline title on that club as `trades.market_title` / `markets.title` store it for the slug `<lg>-<a>-<b>-<date>-<code>` (one bounded read, `_C5_TITLES_SQL`: trades through `trades_ts_idx` with a 3-day bound, markets through `markets_event_idx` on his event slug — no new index, see §11.3; `LIMIT 9`, and nine distinct rows back is `yn:code-translate:titles-overflow` — a title past the bound could be the one that disagrees; two stored titles naming different clubs witness nothing); else the side of his event title (`markets.event_title` — `trades` stores no event title, `001_init.sql`) that is not the venue's shared club. A total's, a btts's or a draw's matchup title is NOT read as a code witness (the brief's rule: a title that names no one club binds no code). Nothing readable: `yn:code-translate:unwitnessed` (with `why`: `no-event-title`, `event-title-shape`, `event-title-other-side`, `titles-disagree`, `venue-lined`, `venue-intent`, `venue-sides`, `venue-question`, `shared:yn:…`, `unshared:yn:…`). A name that is not the venue's: `yn:code-translate:name-mismatch` (`his_club`, `venue_club`; `why: rows-disagree` when the venue's two rows disagree with each other). Under an alias the shared club must be his words too (his event title's other side, or his stored moneyline title on it).
3. **Rewrite, then the lane as today.** His slug is rewritten to the venue's codes (`epl-eve-mun-…-mun` → `epl-eve-mnu-…-mnu`; the tail is replaced only when it IS the translated code) and `resolve`'s own calls run on the same prefix- and segment-kept rows: `match_side(…, yn_identity=True)` (the yes/no identity arm, the draw's event/title witness) and `c3_pick` (totals by segment, btts, spreads). No family gets a side rule here; a lane refusal on the rewritten slug is named by the lane (`total:line-absent`, `yn:subj` …) with `yn_c5.certified = true`. The hit keeps the lane's own `matched_by` (`premap_identity`, `premap_draw_title`, `premap_fh_total`, `premap_btts`, `premap_spread`) and source `premap`, and carries `code_translated: {"mun": "mnu"}` and `witness: atc-epl-eve-mnu-2026-09-06-mnu` (the venue row that named his club) — `resolve` returns both, `resolve_explain` carries them with `yn_c5` (`label: yn:code-translated`, `witness_src: title|trades|event`, `event`, `shared`, `pair`, `slug`, `his_club`, `venue_club`). The translation lives in the one call: nothing is stored, so another date or opponent derives its own or none (pinned: the 2026-09-13 return fixture certifies from its own rows; 2026-09-07 certifies nothing).

Consulted only after every other arm found nothing (wording, identity/alias/draw, C3, the named lane), inside `PREMAP_YN_IDENTITY` like all of C2/C3: off, every row answers exactly as before. NEVER a static club-code table, never a prefix/similarity match on codes (`mun`/`mnu`, `che`/`cfc` look alike — that is not evidence; pinned: a code with nothing in common, `xyz`, is certified by the question alone, and the near-twin `mnu` naming Manchester City refuses). The one new read (`trades`/`markets` titles) happens only once a single candidate event exists and the market's own title names no club; an unreadable store is unwitnessed.

### 11.3 The census, and what is left out

`resolve_explain`'s `split` carries the three refusals, so `explain_unmapped` prints `no_side_match:yn:code-translate:…` and MIRRORUNMAP buckets them; a mapped translation is `resolves` with `code_translated` / `witness` / `matched_by` and `yn_c5.label = yn:code-translated`. Two reporting-only changes from the investigation: (i) the one current path that left a yes/no label BARE — picked by `_yn_pick`, vetoed by `match_side`'s line/identity guard (`epl_unmapped.md` §1.3) — is now `no_side_match:yn:picked-vetoed`, so a bare `no_side_match` on a yes/no row can only mean "judged by older code"; (ii) `MIRRORUNMAPMKT` in `engine-diagnostic.yml` prints `at=` (when the `.latest` row was written; `mirror_report.summarize` keeps it), because the shadow re-judges only the 20 newest-touched markets a tick and a market whose fills stopped keeps its last verdict — the stale EPL labels of 2026-09-06 were that (§5.2 of the investigation). Nothing here re-judges a stale row; the rows above are re-judged when the shadow next reads them (or the mapgap endpoint re-runs `resolve_explain`).

**Review fold v2 (2026-09-07).** MEDIUM-1: the club-name EQUALITY rule (`pmus._yn_name_match`, token-set equality, no containment) is pinned in both prefix directions — his "Will Manchester win on 2026-09-06?", an event title "Everton FC vs. Manchester", a stored "Will Manchester win …" and a venue naming "Manchester United FC Salford" all refuse `yn:code-translate:name-mismatch` (`his_club: manchester`); a `startswith` reading either way would have certified. LOW-2: the stored-title read is bounded fail-closed (`LIMIT 9`, nine rows → `titles-overflow`, never eight read as agreement) and both legs read through indexes that exist: `trades` by `trades_ts_idx` (`ts >= now() - interval '3 days'` — his fills on a dated game; an older market falls to the event title), `markets` by `markets_event_idx` (`event_slug = <lg>-<a>-<b>-<date>`, the kindless form the feed stores; a different stored form finds nothing and falls to the event title). No index migration: `scripts/migrate.py` runs every file inside `conn.transaction()`, so `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` cannot be used (migrations/053's header), and a plain `CREATE INDEX` on `trades` would hold a SHARE lock against every ingestion INSERT for the build, on the API's boot path — not safe live on Render; `markets(slug)` likewise left out. Reading `markets` by `condition_id` does not apply: the witness is ANOTHER market's title (the unshared club's moneyline), not the market being resolved. LOW-3: the shared row alone, the unshared row alone, or neither: no witness, today's reason, no C5 trace (pinned per case). Observation B (the venue's two questions swapped against their identifiers, still naming each other): where his words bind the code — his own title on that moneyline, or his stored moneyline title — the refusal is `name-mismatch` with `why: rows-swapped` (his club is the SHARED row's subject); where only his event title witnesses, nothing of his binds a code to a name, so the pair certifies on the names the rows carry and the record carries `his_club` = `venue_club` (the club the witness matched, the unshared row's subject) beside `shared_club` (the shared row's subject), so the swap is readable in the plan; the family lanes read names on their own rows, so no side is wrong (the moneylines refuse `yn:subj` / `name-mismatch`; btts, spread and draw land on the event's own rows — pinned).

Left out, by design: no club-code table and no similarity of codes; no witness from a total's, a btts's or a draw's own matchup title (per the brief — the event title or a stored moneyline title is required; a moneyline without a stored event title never reaches the rows anyway); no re-judging of stale shadow rows (§5.4 of the investigation, a report-side change); the live worker's memo (`mirror_live`) unchanged; `_norm`, `side_norm`, `match_side`'s branches, `c3_pick`'s side tables and `_yn_team_row`'s refusal names unchanged (`_yn_team_question` is a factoring, byte-identical outcomes); the copy lane's own yes/no lane (`pmus.resolve_team_yesno_exact`) untouched; no separate switch (one switch for C2/C3/C5, as the C3 review settled). The venue's `event_title` for the two EPL events was not printed by `epl-rows`; the fixture assumes the naming its questions carry (the lookup key only — every decision reads the question).

## 12. E2 (2026-09-06) — the latency half: what the census now carries

Owner, ~22:50Z: "I want this firing as frequently as his. Make the latency as low as possible." The build is described in `docs/mirror-to-a-tee-program.md` §5a (E2). The census keys it adds or reads, all in `mirror_live.CENSUS_KEYS` past the served 40-key prefix (raw heartbeat and this module's tests; `integ` is at its ceiling):

- `venue_calls` — every venue REQUEST the tick made, as events: paced reads (quotes, order status, open orders, recent trades, the flatten's bid), cancels, placements (a BUY is a preview and a create: two; a SELL one), closes, every page of the tick's positions walk (`ms.account_positions_pages`) and of the flatten's own reading of the venue's positions (`ml._pm_held`, paced through this lane's gate), the grammar class's market reads and echo, the mapping lane's resolver calls (`map_venue_read`, one for one). Since the review every WRITE of this lane also claims its gaps on the process-wide pacer, so the rate the venue sees is the pacer's (one request per 0.35 s from this process); this counter measures what a tick spends of it.
- `venue_calls_capped` — the soft guard: at `rules.MIRROR_VENUE_CALLS_PER_TICK` (default 80 since review round 2 — 40 candidate reads plus 20 BUYs at two requests each; `capped_env`) the CANDIDATE walk stops for the tick (`capped_tick` beside it). The guard counts the tick's WRITES and its CANDIDATE reads alone — never an open book's read (the tick's fixed cost) — so 40 candidate reads are reachable at any book count; no exit is bounded by it. A candidate a memo or a book already skips is not counted as cut.
- `rate_limited` — every site that read a venue 429 (E2 review round 2; EVERY placement site since round 4): a placement — the post-only rest's create (the adapter's 4xx refusal, `status_code` 429), and since round 4 a BUY's PREVIEW and an IOC take's create too, the two requests the adapter does not wrap (they RAISE the SDK's `RateLimitError` through `_guarded`; read by class/status before the lost-response path, the row is refused `place_refused:rate_limited` with a receipt naming the raise, the room goes back, nothing is frozen and no open-orders search is made: a 429 is the venue refusing the request before it processed it, so nothing was sent and nothing is lost) — the tick abandons `rate_limited` at all three, and skips the backoff while the circuit holds; since round 5 the flatten's two placements too — the sole-holder `close_position` whose adapter caught the SDK's `RateLimitError` into a `close_failed` raw naming it, and the co-held IOC's create raising it — are the same named refusal on their row (never the lost-close freeze, never the open-orders search; a socket reset on either is still lost); a quote read (`_bbo`, never counted as `no_quote` and never a step or a reset of the outage streak); a cancel whose error names 429/RateLimit (each attempt); the tick's positions walk (abandons `positions_unreadable`, and since round 4 skips the 60 s backoff too while the circuit holds). Each trips the process-wide pacer's circuit: the gap is ×2 for 600 s for every lane (`venue_pace.penalize`, `penalty_left`). The match is the SDK's `RateLimitError` class, a `status_code` of 429, or an error text that starts with the name / '429' / 'Too Many Requests' — never '429' inside an id, a slug or a price (round 3); at the placement sites the refusal raw is read by its NAMED fields — `status_code`, `error_type` (the adapter now carries the exception's class name on the 4xx refusal, as `close_position` does), `error`'s head — never as a substring of the whole raw (round 4: a `preview_mismatch` whose `expected_cost` is 429.0 or whose quantity is 429 is a mismatch, and a 400 saying "would cross at 0.429" arms the take and abandons nothing). An int `status_code` is authoritative (round 5): 429 is a rate limit and any other int is not whatever the text's head says — a 400 whose message begins "429 contracts exceeds the maximum order size" is a size refusal; `error_type` and the text are read only when no int status is there (the `close_failed` raw, a cancel's error string).
- `place_refused` — a placement the adapter refused without minting an order (`place_refused:<status>` on the process census: `preview_mismatch`, `preview_unreadable`, the short gate's names, and since round 4 `rate_limited` for a 429 raised by the preview or an IOC create; since round 5 the flatten's sole close and co-held IOC too). The row is `rejected` with the adapter's raw (or, for the raised 429, `{error, error_type, status_code}`) as its receipt.
- `walk_error` — the walk-order streak judge raised after a book (logged, counted; the game's walk goes on).
- `backoff_skipped_circuit` — a 429 abandon — a placement's (`rate_limited`) or the positions walk's (`positions_unreadable`) — that skipped the 60 s backoff because the pacer's circuit already holds (review round 3; the walk since round 4); the next tick runs at the doubled gap. The skip is an explicit flag the 429 sites pass to `_abandon` (`rate_limited=True`, always after `_rate_limited` tripped the circuit), never the reason's name. An outage abandon still backs off, a circuit from an earlier 429 notwithstanding.
- `requotes` (existing) — since round 3 a TTL/replace cancel and its re-rest are ONE op (`ops` counts the pair once); `venue_calls` still counts every request.
- `abandoned_in_flight` — a decided order (an entry or an exit) refused because another book abandoned the tick while this one was in flight under the parallel walk; NO plan is written for that book, so its updated_at stays and E1's walk reads it as unreached and walks its game first on the next tick (`tick_abandoned` counts the abandon itself, once).
- `take_at_his_level` — the bounded take's wait (`MIRROR_TAKE_AFTER_S`, default 20 s since E2, env lengthens only) has run AND the book is at or through his level: one IOC at the same wire follows (`take_placed`, `placed_take` count the placement itself).
- `take_refused_price` — the wait has run and the book is NOT at his level: no IOC, the rest stands (`resting_above_level` beside it on the rest path; on the armed path the arm is cleared `market_away` as before).
- `take_capped` / `replace_capped` — read them knowing an EXIT is never behind them since the review: a reduce, a flatten or a plan on the other side of the rest cancels and goes out whatever the hour's count (`_exit_or_flip`); the two names now count entry churn alone. `take_arm_stale` fires past max(2 × wait, 60 s), never sooner.
- `ops_capped` — read it against the new budget of 20 (was 6); a placement whose refusal came before the venue write no longer spends an op (`_op_slot`).
- `map_reads_capped` — unchanged in meaning; `MIRROR_MAX_MARKETS` is 40 (was 20), so compare the before/after census on the same hour of his flow.
- the candidate read budget the live lane reads is its own: `mirror_live.MAX_MARKETS_PER_TICK` (env `MIRROR_LIVE_MAX_MARKETS`, 40); the shadow's `ms.MAX_MARKETS_PER_TICK` is back at 20.
- `tick_s` — the wall time; the parallel walk (`MIRROR_BOOK_CONCURRENCY`, default 6) takes it toward the pacer's floor (~0.35 s a paced read), not under 10 s on 46 books, which is why `POLL_S` stays 30 s.

## 13. E4 (2026-09-06) — exits at his price, within one cent; entries take first

Owner, ~23:24Z: "we should exit when he exits at his price or within 1c variance (tolerance)"; the addendum, ~23:38Z: "Remove the 20 second wait on entires too". The build is described in `docs/mirror-to-a-tee-program.md` §5a (E4). The census keys it adds, all in `mirror_live.CENSUS_KEYS` past the served 40-key prefix (raw heartbeat and this module's tests), and the plan fields beside them:

- `exit_take` — an exit's IOC SENT (counted at the placement, beside `take_placed`; review LOW-4): the bid at or through the take cent (the lowest cent at or above his exit price less `rules.MIRROR_EXIT_TOL`, 0.01, env tightens only, read at call time), on the tick it is there, a standing rest cancelled first. `take_at_his_level` / `take_first` are the ENTRY's names and never an exit's. A priced short cover's close carries `exit_close_bips` / `exit_close_ask` on the plan: the slippage the ceiling leaves above the ask read just before the close (1 at the ceiling; review MEDIUM-2).
- `exit_out_of_tol` — an exit held outside the cent: a long book's SELL with the bid under the take cent (the rest stands at his cent, ceil(his price), nothing chases), or a short book's cover with the ask over the ceiling (his buy-back plus the tolerance; no close_position, no position read, no freeze, read again next tick). The plan carries `exit_out_of_tol` {bid, ask, floor | ceiling, at}.
- `requote_same_wire` — an exit rest past `MIRROR_REST_TTL_S` at the same side, cent and quantity, left standing: the TTL re-quote it would have been is a no-op (book 29's fourteen). Counted on every tick the rest stands past its TTL; `requotes` does not move. An exit rest at another cent or quantity is replaced as before, exempt from the replace budget (`replace_capped` / `take_capped` never name an exit).
- `take_first` — an ENTRY's IOC sent before any rest (the addendum): the ask at or through HIS CENT (`rules.buy_wire(his)`, the IOC's limit; review HIGH-1 -- the rest's wire sits under the bid and is never "at or through" in a normal book) on the tick the increase is planned, `MIRROR_TAKE_AFTER_S` being 0. A long book's entries only; a short book's add rests as before. The unfilled remainder rests post-only at the same wire (`rest_placed` beside it); the IOC cancels nothing, so it is never a replace. Under a lengthened wait (env) E2's rest-first take runs: a take off a standing rest counts `take_at_his_level` alone, and the armed take with no rest standing (the arm's age is the wait) still counts here, since nothing was cancelled before it.
- plan fields — `exit_px` (his exit price, unrounded), `exit_px_src` (`his_fill`, or `none` for a snapshot-driven reduce, an unpriced vanish or the admin flatten, whose prices are the old ones), `exit_floor` / `exit_rest` / `exit_take` on a long book, `exit_ceiling` / `exit_cover` on a short, `requote_same_wire: true` while a rest stands past its TTL, `take_qty` / `take_filled` on the tick an IOC went out.
- reading a held book — `exit_out_of_tol` rising tick after tick on one book with `requote_same_wire` beside it is the rule working: he exited, the market is more than a cent past his price, and the book waits at his cent. It is not a stuck book; a stuck book names `open_order_pending` with neither.
- meeting E2's re-quote credit (§12; E2 review round 3, LOW-6 — a TTL or replace cancel and the rest that follows it on the same book are ONE op, `_Tick.requote_credit`) — the credit is granted by a TTL or replace cancel alone and spent by a non-IOC placement alone, and a `_Tick` is built per tick, so no credit outlives one. On the same-wire path no cancel is sent, so no credit is ever granted there (nothing to clear, none to go stale). An IOC never spends it: an exit's take after a TTL cancel of an entry rest is the cancel's op plus its own (`ops` 2); the take-first after a TTL cancel is the cancel's op, the IOC's own and the remainder's rest on the credit (`ops` 2); an IOC the budget refused (`ops_capped`) with the credit standing rests the plannable quantity on the credit instead (`_entry_take`), so a TTL cohort's crossing book is not left bare for the tick.
- `ops_capped` never names an exit (E4 review round 3, 2026-09-07, M-1) — EXITS ARE EXEMPT FROM THE PER-TICK OPS BUDGET: every cancel, IOC, rest and close on an exit's path (a reduce, the paired and the vanish flatten, the sign-flip flatten, a short book's cover, the TTL re-quote of a reduce rest) takes its slot whatever the count (`_op_slot(exit=True)`; `_place` reads it off the side's leg action) and is still counted in `ops`, on `venue_calls` and the soft guard; only entries are refused `ops_capped`, exactly as before (E2's take-first fallback on the credit included). Before the fold the exit's take at the budget's last op cancelled the rest and had its IOC refused, leaving the book with no exit order for the tick. Read `ops` above the budget as exits going out.
- `cancel_refused:<reason>` (plan reason, round 3 L-4) — a take's or a replace's cancel of the standing rest was refused before it went out (`ops_capped`: an entry's take or replace at the cap; an exit cannot reach it): the rest stands, nothing was sent, and the plan says so — never `take`. A cancel that went out and left the order unknown reads `cancel_pending`, the freeze's name, on the take paths as on the replace path.
- the TTL of a reduce rest (round 3 D-1/D-2) — step O's TTL clause skips only a PRICED exit rest (the book's last plan says `exit_px_src: 'his_fill'`: the rest is at his cent and `requote_same_wire` keeps it; with no plan on file, a long book's rest whose wire is the cent of its own recorded `his_level`); an UNPRICED reduce rest (`exit_px_src: 'none'`; with no plan on file, a rest the ask lifted or one with no level of his) keeps its `ttl` re-quote — reason `ttl`, `requotes` 1, never `replace`, never counted by `_SQL_REPLACES` against the book's entry budget — and is TTL'd through an abandoned tick as before E4.

## 14. S4 (2026-09-07) — the short cover as a priced order; the read-back proof

Every short close on 2026-09-07 failed within a second. With 4a5da1f's logging the venue's reason reads `pmus: close_position aec-itfwo-wuszhe-jiaqwan-2026-09-07 failed: BadRequestError: Price is required for limit order` (03:30:06Z, 03:50:54Z; the same for aec-itfme-yuatak-kazido at 04:29:56Z and 04:51:16Z and aec-itfme-kosuz-chisat at 04:51:52Z): 11 CLOSE rows, 0 executed. The venue refuses `POST /v1/order/close-position` as an unpriced limit order, so the short cover is a REAL order through the placement machinery the long side uses — a BUY of the long token with the closing intent (`ORDER_INTENT_SELL_SHORT` on the wire: the row's side is BUY_LONG, its intent SELL_SHORT, exactly the CLOSE row's labels) — never `close_position`. The rule is E4's mirrored for a short book (`rules.exit_terms(BUY)`): the cover REST post-only at floor(his buy-back price) to the cent (never above him; under the ask whenever the take did not fire), ONE IOC at the ceiling cent (his price + `MIRROR_EXIT_TOL`, buy_wire'd) whenever the ask is at or under it, on the same tick, no wait, never twice per rest, a partial IOC leaving no remainder resting; outside the tolerance the rest stands (`requote_same_wire`), held and re-checked every tick, no freeze. Exits stay exempt from the ops budget, the replace budget and the loss stop. The census keys:

- `s4_probe_placed` — the 1-share read-back probe went out: on the first tick that holds a LIVE short book with a two-sided quote (bid < ask, the market OPEN) while `mirror_s4_proof` is not proved — ONE post-only BUY of the long token with the closing intent at max(0.01, floor(bid − 0.05)) to the cent (off-market, cannot fill; the bid is the book's own `_bbo` read, the LONG token's space the cover's cents are priced in — S4 v2, F2), read back through `open_orders` on the slug (price, quantity, the venue's OWN side and the `intent` as the venue reports them), then cancelled. No mirror_orders row; its two writes are exit ops and counted venue calls, paced. At most once per hour while unproven, never on a frozen book, never twice in a tick, never on a locked or one-sided book, never when the key is unreadable (a malformed key is unreadable: nothing is placed on a key we cannot record into), never on a book with an order standing (S4 v3: a probe that fills needs the book's open-order slot for its row), and never while the record holds a probe order the venue KEPT (S4 v3, GAP 2, below). A 429 on the probe's placement (S4 v2, F3: the create's post_only_rejected raw with `status_code` 429, or the SDK's RateLimitError raised) is E2's rate-limit path — `rate_limited`, the pacer's circuit, the tick abandoned `rate_limited` with the backoff skipped while the circuit holds — and (S4 v3, GAP 3) writes `{"proved": false, "why": "rate_limited", "at": now, "echo": {raised, error}}` so the hour's hold applies: one abandon, not one per tick for as long as the venue limits creates (v2 wrote no record and the probe was the first write of every tick); the `recent` row says `s4_probe: rate_limited`, the plan `s4: {unproven: rate_limited}`. A 429 is still no verdict about the order: the record is the hold.
- `s4_proved` — the read-back matched: price, quantity 1, the slug, the venue's own side `ORDER_SIDE_BUY` (`ml.S4_VENUE_BUY`: the SDK's Order.side, carried verbatim by `pmus._norm_order` as `venue_side` beside the desk's derived `side` — S4 v2, F1), `intent` `ORDER_INTENT_SELL_SHORT`, the order's `state` one of the venue's STANDING states (`ml.S4_RESTING_STATES`: `new`, `pending_new`, `pending_risk` — the SDK's OrderState enum with the prefix dropped; a filled, cancelled, rejected, expired or replaced echo, or one in no state, proves nothing about a standing cover — S4 v3, INFO 5), and the cancel accepted. The key records `{"proved": true, "at", "slug", "order_id", "price", "bid", "ask", "echo": {order_id, us_market_slug, side, venue_side, intent, price, quantity, state, tif}, "cancel"}` (`bid`/`ask`: the quote the probe was placed against); the cover goes on the same tick and every tick after.
- `s4_unproven` — a flatten cover (paired, vanished, the sign flip) refused because the proof has not passed: the key absent, unreadable/malformed, or a recorded mismatch (`why`: `wrong_price`, `wrong_quantity`, `wrong_slug`, `wrong_side`, `side_missing`, `wrong_intent`, `intent_missing`, `not_found`, `read_back_raised:<Type>`, `place_refused:<status>`, `place_raised:<Type>`, `filled_at_create`, `cancel_failed`, `record_failed:<Type>`, and since S4 v3 `wrong_state`, `state_missing`, `rate_limited`; the echo beside it). A `cancel_failed` record KEEPS the probe's `order_id` with `cancel: {ok: false, error, retries, at}` (S4 v3, GAP 2): the venue kept a 1-share order of ours, so every tick after — before the walk, the tick's first venue call (`_s4_cancel_retry`, paced through `_guarded`, an exit op) — the cancel is retried and NO second probe goes out beside it; the venue accepting the cancel, or a refused cancel followed by the order's own status reading cancelled / expired / rejected / replaced or no record of the id, clears the id (`cancel: {ok: true, order, gone, retries}`) and restarts the hourly clock from then (`at`); a status reading FILLED is a share of ours (`cancel.filled`), booked on the book's next walk as a filled probe (`s4_probe_filled`) and then cleared; a 429 on the cancel is E2's path for a cancel — `rate_limited`, the circuit — the id kept and retried next tick, the tick not abandoned. The `recent` row `s4_probe_cancel` says which (`refused` / `rate_limited` / `filled` / `gone`). `wrong_side` is the venue listing the probe as ORDER_SIDE_SELL — an order in another price space (a venue reading a SELL_SHORT limit in the short token's space rests the probe on a high-priced book with our price and intent, and every cover after it would rest where it can never fill); only the venue's own side tells it apart. The book is held live, nothing sent, no freeze; the plan says `s4: {unproven: <why>}`. A PARTIAL short reduce (his partial buy-back) is refused under the pre-S4 name `short_reduce_unproven` until the proof has passed in production — S4 unlocks it: proved, the partial reduce is the same priced cover for the reduce quantity.
- `s4_probe_filled` (S4 v3, GAP 1) — the probe EXECUTED: the post-only latch tripped and the venue filled our 1-share off-market cover at create (the 23:16Z shape), or a probe the venue kept filled before its cancel got through. The share is ours, so it is BOOKED exactly as a cover fill: an ordinary mirror_orders row of kind `s4_probe` (side BUY, intent SELL_SHORT, qty 1, GTC post-only as sent, wire = the probe price, the fill's price, `taker_at_placement`, maker false, state filled) and the ledger through `_book_delta` — one share toward zero, the realized on it, the standing row's shares — so `_cover_qty` sizes the next cover off the ledger the row moved (a filled probe on a 300 short leaves 299 to cover, never 300 against a venue short of 299). The record says `why: filled_at_create` with `booked: {row, booked, ledger}`; the cover stays gated (`s4_unproven`) for the hour. A row that cannot be written leaves the share on the venue and not on the ledger — inside the one-share tolerance the venue/ledger read never names — so the book is frozen `s4_probe_unbooked` by name with the order and the error (`booked: {row: null, error}`).
- `short_cover_rest` — the cover's rest at floor(his) went out (GTC, post-only, no good-till; the row kind is the plan's: `reduce`, `flatten_paired`, `flatten_vanished`).
- `short_cover_take` — the cover's IOC went out (kind `take` at the ceiling cent; or the unpriced vanish's bounded IOC under the flatten kind). `short_flatten_close` now counts a short book's leg covered to flat by any cover order (the name `close_position`'s fill used to carry).
- `short_cover_out_of_tol` — the cover held outside the cent (beside `exit_out_of_tol`; the plan's `exit_out_of_tol` carries bid/ask/ceiling).
- no price of his (`exit_px_src: 'none'`: a snapshot-driven reduce, a vanish with no fill of his, the admin flatten) — the cover is HELD and named `no_price` with `cover: unpriced_held` on the plan, never rested at the ask; the ONE exception is the confirmed vanish / the sign flip with his position on our side gone: one IOC at the ask bounded by `le.buy_limit_price` (the ask + `rules.MIRROR_FLATTEN_SLIP` 0.02, `sell_limit_price` mirrored) for our quantity, `cover: {ioc_at_ask, limit, why}`. (A sign flip is priced by construction: the fills that carry his net across zero are the cover-side fills.)
- the sole/co-held question is gone from the short side: a cover is a clamped order of OUR quantity; `_flatten_send` (the long slippage leg) refuses a short at its head (`book_error`). `_reconcile_lost_close` stays for the legacy CLOSE rows on file; new covers are ordinary rows (tif GTC / IOC) that `_reconcile_placing` / `_find_lost_placement` cover — the fingerprint matches INTENT to intent whenever the row (the 050 column) and the venue row both name one (S4 v2, F4: the cover's SELL_SHORT, a short add's BUY_SHORT; the plan side BUY and the venue row's derived side 'SELL' never agreed on a cover, so a lost cover response was never adopted), side to side when either lacks one. A lost cover at our cent, our quantity, our intent, inside the window, is adopted in `_lost_response` and in step O; one the search cannot find freezes the book `placement_lost` like any rest (never silently live); a cover adopted on a frozen book is cancelled under the freeze's name, the book thawed and the cover rested again. Plan fields: `exit_px`, `exit_ceiling`, `exit_cover`, `exit_rest`, `exit_px_src`, `s4`, `cover`.

## 15. W1 (2026-09-07) — the two lanes stop spending their ticks on finished markets; the shadow names the venue's word

Investigation `gap_book_unreadable.md` (13:50Z): of the 64 markets / $388k whose LATEST shadow verdict read 'no mark: book unreadable', 99.3% of the rows inside his own trading window read the venue OPEN with a two-sided book; the first terminal read (MARKET_STATE_EXPIRED) lands 2-14 min AFTER his last fill on 60/60; 0 halts, 0 raised reads in 8,740 rows. The label was the census reading the NEWEST row of a market the shadow kept re-judging for LOOKBACK_H after the venue expired it. On the live lane the same shape cost the tick: at 13:44Z 26 of 27 book reads were on EXPIRED markets whose `markets` row still read closed=false (two paced calls each, >= 18 s of a 20.4 s tick). Four changes, measurement first, one live change, no order path touched:

- R1 `mirror_shadow.no_mark_reason` — the shadow's `no mark: book unreadable` is split by the reading the row already holds, the venue's string as read and never normalised: `no mark: venue state <STATE>` (any non-OPEN state, terminal or a halt), `no mark: read failed <Exc>` (bbo_error), `no mark: no state, empty` (state None, no error), `no mark: bid only <px>` (OPEN, a bid, no ask — a decided market's 0.99), `no mark: quote off ladder <bid>/<ask>` (OPEN, a quote the mark rule refused), `no mark: empty open book` (OPEN, neither). Nothing else on the row moves (target 0, would_side None, the exit leg's `exit_reason` carries the same string). Every consumer reads the PREFIX `no mark:` (`ms.NO_MARK_PREFIX`; render-ops `unread-rows` reads `LIKE 'no mark:%'`, so the older rows still match). `mirror_report.summarize` gains `no_mark_by` — per class (`mirror_report.no_mark_class`: the figured classes `bid only` / `quote off ladder` collapse to the class name), the markets whose newest row carries it and the rows in the window. Pinned on the six verbatim readings of `hard2/unread_repro.py` (`test_no_mark_names_the_venue_state`). Not in scope: the shadow still marks a quoted non-OPEN book (CLOSED with stale rests, bid 0.01 / ask 0.20) off its quotes — only the live lane's `_bbo` refuses that quote.
- R2 `mirror_shadow._terminal_until` — the shadow's own D1: a market whose read carried a state in `STATE_TERMINAL` is remembered per (whale, condition_id) for `UNMAPPED_TTL_S` and skipped in the tick loop beside `_unmapped_until`, counted `skipped_terminal` on the heartbeat. One `no mark: venue state …` row, then silence, so the newest row per market is the last reading that could plan and the 20-slot tick stops spending slots on finished matches. NEVER for HALTED / SUSPENDED / PREOPEN (they reopen), never when the state was unread; the miss-streak rule is unchanged. Pinned: `test_a_terminal_read_leaves_the_read_set_for_the_ttl`, `test_a_halted_read_never_leaves_the_read_set`, `test_an_unread_state_never_leaves_the_read_set`.
- R3 render-ops `coverage-gap` — `sh` is now, per market, the newest shadow row written at or before his last fill in the window whose `detail->>'state'` is not one of the four terminal strings, falling back to the newest row; the new `judged` column prints when that row was written. Read-only; the second statement stays self-contained.
- R4 `mirror_live._terminal_book_until` (+ `_terminal_book_state`, the state string for the plan) — the live lane's one change. In `_tick_book`, AFTER step M (the markets row and `_maybe_close_episode`, which still run every tick) and before the quote read: a book inside the memo skips the quote read AND the per-market position read and writes the plan `{"kind": "no_plan", "venue_terminal": <state>, "at": now}` under the existing `no_mark` name — census `no_mark` as today plus the new `book_terminal_skipped` (inserted before the pinned last key `cand_terminal_skipped`; past the served prefix, read off the raw heartbeat). The memo's ONE writer is `_memo_terminal_book`, off the book's own read (`_bbo`, book=True): a state in `ms.STATE_TERMINAL`, and nothing open on the book (`t.open_by_book` / `t.nonterminal` — the cancel the first terminal read sends under `no_mark` must have landed; the memo is written on the next terminal read). NEVER on HALTED / SUSPENDED / PREOPEN (read every tick as before), never on an unread state, never from a candidate read (that stays D1's `_terminal_until`). Fail-closed: a terminal market accepts no order; a book with a resting order is read until the rest is gone; the close lands the tick the row reads closed, inside the memo, with no read spent. Effect at 13:44Z's shape: 26 x 2 paced reads leave the tick. Pinned: `test_an_expired_book_is_read_once_per_ttl_and_still_closes_on_the_markets_row`, `test_a_halted_book_is_read_every_tick`, `test_a_book_with_an_open_order_is_never_memo_skipped`, `test_the_book_memo_never_sets_from_a_candidate_read` (which also re-runs `test_the_shadow_never_touches_an_order`).
- Reading the census after W1: `no_mark` on the live heartbeat now counts memo-skipped books too (one per book per tick, as before the read was spent); `book_terminal_skipped` beside it says how many spent no read; `snap_market_unreadable` stops counting expired markets. On the shadow, `skipped_terminal` beside `skipped_unmapped`; `no_mark_by` on the report. The R5 halt cadence and the R6 loss stop of the investigation are unchanged (owner decisions, not rules).
## 16. R1 (2026-09-07) — the league-stripped pair key; the census names an unlisted league

Owner standing orders as §11. The gap, read from the venue's own rows (render-ops `league-census` / `league-rows` 13:51Z, the 19:09Z dump; `scratchpad/hard2/gap_league_codes.md`): his feed and the venue code the same game under DIFFERENT league codes with the team codes and the date byte-identical — `nor-kbk-tro-2026-09-06-kbk` against `atc-els-kbk-tro-2026-09-06-kbk` ("Will Kristiansund BK win against Tromso IL in the Eliteserien match scheduled for Sep 6, 2026?"), itsb/srb, den/sld, sui/swsl likewise — so the slug keys (`nor-kbk-tro-…` vs `els-kbk-tro-…`) never met and the ONLY bridge was the matchup key from `markets.event_title`, absent on a moneyline judged before the title is stored (production `no_key_intersection`, keys=2) and unequal wherever the venue spells a club otherwise. On the 09-06 read every LISTED same-code game's keys meet once the event title is stored (the production labels were stale, §0.1 of the investigation), so R1 recovers little on that day and is kept MINIMAL: a lookup, and the census that reads it.

### 15.1 The key (`premap.event_keys_for`, `_pair_key`; the sweep through `keys_for_row`)

Both sides emit `<a>-<b>-<date>` beside the slug key: his `nor-kbk-tro-2026-09-06-kbk` also keys `kbk-tro-2026-09-06`; the venue's `atc-els-kbk-tro-2026-09-06` likewise. It ends in the date, so `_dated_admissible` admits it by the rule it already had (a pair key without a date, `kbk-tro`, is never admitted). EXACTLY three tokens must stand before the date (`<lg>-<a>-<b>`): a two-token slug has no league to strip, a four-token one is not the grammar, and a bare date is never a key — the `league_alias_probe`'s own lesson (`gvs-2026-08-25` strips to nothing). Keys are a lookup, never a decision: the rows it fetches answer to the arms exactly as before — the identity arm, the alias arm's single-code and opponent witness (`yn:alias-unwitnessed` with no event title, `yn:league-ambiguous` on two codes carrying the pair), the draw witness, C3's `_c3_code`, C5. Rows re-key at the next sweep: the UPSERT rewrites `event_keys` on conflict, the full sweep rewrites every live row inside 30 minutes and the fast lane the imminent window inside 3, so no backfill statement is needed (rows the sweep no longer sees age out at `PRUNE_HOURS` and are not tradeable anyway).

| his slug (event title) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `nor-kbk-tro-…-kbk` "Will Kristiansund BK win on 2026-09-06?" (none) | `no_key_intersection` keys 2 rows 0 | rows 6, `no_side_match:yn:alias-unwitnessed` — the witness rule survives the lookup |
| the same, "Kristiansund BK vs. Tromsø IL" | `atc-els-kbk-tro-…-kbk` LONG `premap_alias nor->els` (the matchup key) | the same, reached through the pair key too |
| the same, the venue's title spelled "Kristiansund vs. Tromsø" | `no_key_intersection` | `atc-els-kbk-tro-…-kbk` `premap_alias nor->els`; the draw likewise |
| `els` and `xx` both carrying `kbk-tro-2026-09-06` | — | `no_side_match:yn:league-ambiguous` `[els, xx]` |
| `nor-kbk-tro-…-first-half-total-1pt5` "…: 1st Half O/U 1.5", the venue's title otherwise | `no_key_intersection` | `tsc-els-kbk-tro-…-fh-1pt5` `premap_fh_total nor->els` (C3's `_c3_code`, unchanged) |
| `nor-sar-vif-2026-09-07-vif` against the venue's `els-sar-vif-2026-09-06` | `no_key_intersection` | the same: the pair key carries HIS date |
| `fl1-olm-pfc-…-spread-home-1pt5` with no event title (the C3 pin) | `no_key_intersection` | `asc-lg1-olm-pfc-…-neg-1pt5` `premap_spread fl1->lg1` |

Tests: `backend/tests/test_r1_pair_key.py` (the els rows verbatim from the 19:09Z dump). Three pins moved with the lookup, each to the class the key recovers: `test_c2_yesno_alias.test_the_alias_needs_his_event_title_as_the_witness` (`no_key_intersection` → `no_side_match:yn:alias-unwitnessed`), `test_c3_derivatives.test_the_feeds_own_rows` (the spread without an event title maps), `test_premap_key_bridge.test_a_slug_with_no_kind_prefix_is_unchanged` (the key set names the pair key).

### 15.2 The census (`resolve_explain`, R3): `venue:league-unlisted`

The `league_alias_probe` stripped the league from HIS slug key only and the venue never emitted the stripped key, so it read `would_have_hit: false` on every row for two weeks and measured this class as zero. With the pair key in the fetch that question has no answer left, so the probe is RE-POINTED: on `no_key_intersection`, for a slug that parses `<lg>-<a>-<b>-<date>…`, it asks whether ANY atc row on his date carries either of his club codes in its position (`identifier ~ '^atc-[a-z0-9]+-(<a>-[a-z0-9]+|[a-z0-9]+-<b>)-<date>-'`, LIMIT 25, the diagnostic path only — never `resolve`); his club NAMES (§16's `his_name_keys`) were in the fetch already and met nothing. Nothing: the venue does not list his league that day — `split = venue:league-unlisted` (`explain_unmapped` prints `no_key_intersection:venue:league-unlisted`; gre1, bel1, ere, bra3 on 2026-09-06, $33,868) and the row leaves the mapper's bucket. Something: listed, keys unmet — `would_have_hit`, counted by `api_unmapped_census` as before (`alias_hits`, `alias_examples`). A slug outside the grammar (tennis, an undated future) asks nothing. The probe's fields: `name_keys`, `code_pattern`, `rows_it_would_find`, `would_have_hit`, `sample`.

## 17. C6-N (2026-09-07) — the names-witnessed per-team row when NO code is shared

Owner standing orders as §11. The gap, read from the venue's own rows (render-ops `esports-chi-rows` 13:52Z, `scratchpad/hard2/gap_esports_chi.md` §1.1-1.2; the col1 rows in `gap_league_codes.md` §a): his league code AND both club codes differ from the venue's —

| his feed (`trades` / `markets`) | the venue (`us_premap`) |
|---|---|
| `chi1-cdh-csc-2026-09-06-csc` "Will CSD Colo-Colo win on 2026-09-06?", event "CD Huachipato vs. CSD Colo-Colo" (No $24,372 / Yes $1,604; his +30,419 win, `no_side_match:yn:no-row` at 22:58Z) | `atc-pdc-hua-col-2026-09-06-col` yes/LONG no/SHORT, line empty — "Will CSD Colo-Colo win against CD Huachipato in the Primera Chile match scheduled for Sep 6, 2026?" |
| `…-cdh` "Will CD Huachipato win on 2026-09-06?"; `…-draw` "Will CD Huachipato vs. CSD Colo-Colo end in a draw?" | `…-hua` "Will CD Huachipato win against CSD Colo-Colo in the Primera Chile match …"; `…-draw` "Will the Primera Chile match CD Huachipato vs CSD Colo-Colo scheduled for Sep 6, 2026 end in a draw?" |
| `chi1-cdp-cu1-…-cu1` "Will CD Universidad de Concepción win on 2026-09-06?" | `atc-pdc-pal-uco-…-uco` "Will CD Universidad de Concepcion win against CD Palestino in the Primera Chile match …" |
| `col1-dep-mif-…-mif` "Will Millonarios FC win on 2026-09-06?", `markets.event_title` NULL ($24,576, `no_key_intersection`); `…-dep`, `…-draw` | `atc-lco-per-mil-…-mil` "Will Millonarios FC win against Deportivo Pereira in the Liga Colombia match scheduled for Sep 6, 2026?", `-per`, `-draw` |
| `col1-cle-gdr-…-gdr` "Will Águilas Doradas Rionegro win on 2026-09-06?", `…-cle`, `…-draw` (event title NULL) | `atc-lco-leq-agu-…-agu` / `-leq` "Will Internacional de Bogota win against Aguilas Doradas Rionegro in the Liga Colombia match …" / `-draw` |

The alias arm (§8) refuses `yn:no-row` (no row under any league code carries his `<a>-<b>-<date>-<t>`) and C5 (§11) has no candidate (it needs exactly one shared code): $27k chi1 + $41k col1 in 24 h. The venue's own per-team question names his club as his title states it and his opponent as his event title states it, on his date — C2's alias witness (§8.1 step iii) minus the suffix equality — and that, and nothing else, admits the row.

### 16.1 The rule (`premap._yn_names_pick`, `_yn_names_draw_pick`; `names_resolve` for the stored-title witness)

Consulted inside `_yn_pick` only after the alias arm's `yn:no-row`, and only where C5 has no candidate (`_c5_owns`: his own event on the board under some code, or an event sharing exactly one of his codes in position, is C5's shape — the names arm refuses `yn:no-row`, today's reason, and C5 runs after as before). Then, ALL of:

1. **His title is his dated win question** (`_bridge_title_subject`: the anchor X; the C2 gate `_yn_pick` already passed).
2. **His event title names exactly two sides** (`_yn_event_sides`), X matches exactly ONE (`yn:event-shear`), and that side's POSITION is t's position in his slug (t == a → 0) else `yn:names-shear` (a title asking about the other club, a reversed event title). With no event title stored, his moneyline title on the OTHER code as `trades.market_title` / `markets.title` store it (`names_resolve`: the C5 read `_C5_TITLES_SQL`, bounded — `LIMIT 9`, nine rows `titles-overflow`, two anchors `titles-disagree`, none `no-stored-title`) names the opponent; read only once the pure arm said `yn:names-unwitnessed`, and the identity lane's yes/no call then runs again with that one name as `names_witness`. Nothing: `yn:names-unwitnessed` (`yn_c6.why`).
3. **Among the fetched rows, the FULL-GAME per-team atc rows on his date whose suffix is the code at the SAME position** (`_yn_names_rows`; never fh/sh/draw/exact) whose P4 question passes `_yn_team_row(d, anchor=X, opp_witness=other)` — subject X, opponent the other side, his date, the club scope screens, the league-slot rule (a women's twin, 'Primera Chile Femenina', is screened here and is no candidate) — form EXACTLY ONE identifier. Two (a same-day twin under `pdcx` with the same league phrase and clubs): `yn:names-ambiguous` (`candidates`). None, but a row naming X against another club, his opponent against another club, or — the other position's row read in mirror — his opponent as subject against a club that is not his: `yn:names-mismatch` (`venue_rows`): 'Gil Vicente Barcelos' is not 'Gil Vicente FC', 'Universidad Catolica' is not 'Universidad Católica de Chile', 'Junior FC' is not 'CDP Junior FC' under token-set equality — refused by design. None at all: `yn:no-row` stands (a wrong date, a terse question).
4. **`_yn_gate`**: the venue's own side and intent for his Yes/No (`yn:side`, `yn:intent`); then `match_side`'s `_yn_line_ok` and identity veto as for every arm (a lined venue row: `yn:picked-vetoed`).

**The draw** (`_yn_draw_pick` → `_yn_names_draw_pick` when no `-draw` row under any code carries his codes): his sides from his event title or, with none, his draw title (`_yn_draw_title_sides`, §9.3); the per-team rows naming sides[0] at position 0 against sides[1] (or sides[1] at position 1 against sides[0]) witness the event — exactly one event; a pair read only in the swapped positions is `yn:names-shear`, two events `yn:names-ambiguous`, a per-team row naming one of his sides at its position against another club `yn:names-mismatch` (as for the moneyline); then that event's `-draw` row through `_yn_draw_row` on his sides.

The hit is `matched_by premap_names`, source `premap` (the class the quarantine admits), and carries `code_pair {"cdh": "hua", "csc": "col"}`, `witness` (the row that named him), `league_alias chi1->pdc` where the league code differs; `resolve` returns `code_pair` / `witness`, `resolve_explain` carries them with `yn_c2` (the pure arm's trace) and `yn_c6` (`witness_src: trades`, `other`, the refusal with `why`). Read in this one call: never stored, never reused for another date or opponent (the 2026-09-13 return certifies from its own rows), never a table (pinned: no code or club literal in the arm's source). Behind `PREMAP_YN_IDENTITY` like all of C2/C3/C5: off, every row answers as before (`yn:would-resolve` in the dark census).

### 16.2 The lookup: the per-club dated name key (`venue_name_keys`, `keys_for_row`, `his_name_keys`)

R1's pair key does not fetch these rows (both codes differ), and the matchup key fails whenever `markets.event_title` is NULL (`col1-dep-mif-…-mif`) or the venue spells the event otherwise. So the sweep keys each FULL-GAME per-team atc row by the P4 subject its own question names (`<club>@<date>`, folded and normalized as the arms read names; the question's date must be the identifier's) and the draw row by the two clubs its draw template names; his side keys his title's anchor, his event title's two sides and his draw title's two sides. The sweep writes `keys_for_row(event_keys, r)` on every row (both paths); rows gain the keys at their next sweep exactly as §15.1. A lookup only: what the fetched rows answer to is the witness above. Consequence for §11: a moneyline with no event title now reaches the venue's row for HIS club alone through his anchor's key; C5 then sees a lone per-team row and witnesses nothing (`yn:no-row`, today's reason — before, `no_key_intersection`); with the event titles meeting, C5 reads as before.

'Rio Ave FC' and 'Académico de Viseu FC' alone pass and a pick needs both sides: por ($80k/24 h) is NOT mappable identity-only on today's rows | | | |

## 18. E1 (2026-09-07) — the esports event-title key: his cs2 series meet the venue's board

Read 13:40–14:10Z (`gap_esports_chi.md` §1.3/§3.3; the venue rows `esports_chi_rows_1352.txt` Q4, his rows Q1, verbatim). The venue lists every cs2 series he traded as an `aec-cs2-<a>-<b>-<date>` moneyline whose SIDES are the team names (`fokus` LONG / `nemiga` SHORT, `ex rustec` / `fortress`, `1win` / `astral` …), the question `Who will win in the upcoming esports event <A> vs <B> scheduled for <date> at <clock> UTC?` (the clock stamps line `00`/`30`, erased as a clock artifact) and the event title `<A> vs. <B>`. With EQUAL codes the wording arm already maps a series by the venue's own side description EQUALLING his outcome (`match_side`'s named branch, unique; `cs2-fnc-nip-…` [NIP] → `aec-cs2-fnc-nip-…` nip BUY_SHORT, book 39). Five series refused `no_key_intersection` for the keys alone: his feed digit-suffixes or shortens a code (`fokus-nemi1`, `k271-sin2`, `bbl1-sin2`, `rustec-for10`, `fokus-ast`, `1win-ast` against the venue's `fokus-nemi`, `k27-sin`, `bbc-sin`, `erx-for`, `fokus-astr`, `1win-astr`), so the slug keys never meet, and his event title `Counter-Strike: <A> vs <B> (BO<n>) - <tournament>` keyed `fokus vs nemiga stake ranked episode 4@…` where the venue's title keys `fokus vs nemiga@…`. Patch M4 against f1f2fb2; tests `backend/tests/test_m4_esports.py` (beside `test_c3_derivatives.py`'s key-builder pins; a separate file so another builder's edit of `event_keys_for` merges).

**The rule (`premap._esports_matchup`, one branch of `event_keys_for`; a lookup, never a decision).** His title is read through exactly the template `^counter-strike: (?P<a>.+?) vs (?P<b>.+?) \(bo\d\)(?: - .*)?$` — on the raw title, case-folded, because `_key_norm` erases the very punctuation the template names (the C3 draw template reads the raw title the same way) — down to `<a> vs <b>`, and that matchup then keys exactly as any title does: `<a> vs <b>@date` and `<b> vs <a>@date` (`fokus vs nemiga@2026-09-06`, `nemiga vs fokus@…`; `ex rustec vs fortress@2026-09-07`; `1win vs astral@…`), which the venue's `FOKUS vs. Nemiga` already keys. A title outside the template (`… - Map 1 Winner`, `Map Handicap: …`, a draw title, another game) keys as it always did. The decision is unchanged: prefix {aec, atc}, then `match_side`'s named branch — the venue's own side description must EQUAL his outcome after `_norm`, unique; anything else refuses `no_side_match` (a venue side `nemiga` beside his outcome `Nemiga Gaming` refuses, pinned). The same keys carry his `-game<N>` map markets to the venue's `astatc-` rows (§17).
