Left out, by design: no ±1 day tolerance anywhere (the instant, never the date, bridges the venue's 09-05); no partial or containment match on a club name ('Casa Pia Lisbon' is not 'Casa Pia AC'); a reversed identifier or event title refuses; no key on a date alone; the draw row's `sportsMarketType` is unread (the appendix prints none) and is not decided on; the markets fallback keys one market at a time (review L3: its rows carry the subject's key alone, C7 reads a `partial` event and refuses); nothing stored, no separate switch. NOT built (review L1): a league-code identity for the venue's code — his `por` equals no venue code, and the only thing separating a same-phrase `ligporw` twin from `ligpor` at the same minute with the same clubs is the code string itself, which no stated fact of either feed binds to his league; refusing it identity-only would refuse every foreign league code, i.e. all of Liga Portugal. What stands: two such listings at the instant refuse `kick:ambiguous`, a women's phrase is screened at the league slot, the record's `team_league` must be its identifier's. Review L2: the census's `at_instant` read compares to the minute, as the arm does. Review L4: the alias-refusal guard in `_yn_pick` and the two-names-on-one-market check are reached only if an earlier check is removed — kept as belt and braces.

## 25. E5 (2026-09-07) — frozen books follow his exits; the operator register; a lost close marked lost once

Owner question 16:5xZ ("when he wins we win, when he loses we lose?"). The 16:42Z heartbeat held 8 frozen books (`frozen_reasons {placement_lost: 7}`, one `venue_ledger_disagree`: book 77 Shelton/Tsitsipas, venue 1,128 / ledger 0 / manual 0 since 2026-09-06), and a frozen book cancelled its open orders, wrote a plan of kind `frozen` and returned — it never followed his exit: he sells, we hold to settlement, a direct P&L divergence on markets mapped correctly. Three parts, all in `mirror_live.py` (the live lane; the shadow is untouched), every rail of the build rules kept: identity only (the book's own slug / asset / condition, the venue's own position), fail closed, every refusal named, no knob raises a rail.

- P1 **the operator register** (`mirror_registered_positions`, migration `056_mirror_registered_positions.sql`: whale, us_market_slug, condition_id, asset, shares, side, registered_at, registered_by, note, source). A row is the owner's dated, named statement that `shares` on the slug are outside the book. Written ONLY by the render-ops `mirror-register` preset (`need_confirm`, confirm=DO; arg `<book_id>=<shares> <note>`, shares signed like the ledger, negative = the short side, a note required): the slug, asset and condition come from the book's own row by id — never typed — and ONE statement prints the venue's reading the worker last wrote (`mirror_books.venue_net`), the ledger, the desk's `manual` shares (the `_SQL_MANUAL_SHARES` shape) and the register so far, then INSERTs only when the figure equals venue − ledger − manual EXACTLY, is nonzero with the book's own leg's sign (never a negative on a long book: the thawed plan would sell shares the venue does not hold), on a frozen book read inside 10 minutes with no row on the slug yet; the `verdict` column says `registered #id …` or `REFUSED: …`. The worker reads the signed sum per slug beside the manual shares (`_SQL_REGISTERED_SHARES`, one statement of the same shape, `_registered_shares`), counts only a sum whose sign is the book's leg's (`registered_sign_refused` otherwise, the figure on the plan), and treats it exactly as `manual`: `explained = ledger + manual + registered` in the freeze comparison, `venue − manual − registered` in `mi.plan`'s venue seat, `registered == 0` beside `manual == 0` in the position-sign proof. The worker NEVER writes the register and NEVER adopts a fill into it (pinned by source). Census `registered_books` (a book whose slug carries a row, once per book per tick), `registered_unreadable` (the table absent until 056 lands: logged once per process, explains nothing); the plan row carries `registered` beside `manual`, and the freeze detail too. The v1 consequence (a registered book thawed and planned from its own ledger) was overturned by the review — see E5 v2 below: a registered book never thaws, and the frozen exit sells the registered shares toward his net. Pinned: `test_e5_a_registered_book_never_thaws_it_exits_the_registered_shares_toward_his_net_and_never_buys`, `…_a_register_row_against_the_books_leg_is_not_read…`, `…_a_register_that_explains_only_part…`, `…_a_live_book_with_a_register_row_reads_it_as_manual_in_its_seat_and_never_increases`, `…_an_unreadable_register_explains_nothing…`, `…_the_worker_never_writes_the_register…`, `…_056_exists…`, `…_the_register_preset_needs_confirm_reads_the_books_own_row_and_refuses_a_typed_slug`.
- P2 **frozen books follow his exits** (`_frozen_exit`, called from both frozen branches of `_tick_book`: the disagreement branch after the freeze, and the agreement branch that a non-terminal row keeps frozen). ONLY under `frozen_reason ∈ {placement_lost, venue_ledger_disagree}` (`FROZEN_EXIT_REASONS`; any other freeze reads and cancels as before, the plan says `reason_not_eligible`), ONLY with the venue's position walked this tick AND his per-market read fresh for this market (`frozen_venue_unread`, `why` on the plan: `venue_positions` / `his_market_read` / `coheld_unreadable`), never on a slug a live non-mirror, non-manual row holds (`_SQL_SLUG_COHELD`, `frozen_coheld`: the venue's number is not the book's alone). The seat is the venue's OWN position less the desk's manual and the register's shares (`_frozen_venue_own`; `frozen_venue_flat` when nothing of the book's is on the venue), never the ledger, never the plan's target. His net not under it: `frozen_no_his_exit`, read only. Otherwise `mi.plan(target, venue_own, venue_own, …)` — the same dead band and hysteresis a live book reads — and `_act` with the venue in the ledger's seat (`_plan_seat`; `_sell_qty` / `_cover_qty` clamp on `_frozen_venue`): E4's exit at his price (the rest at his cent post-only with no good-till, the ONE IOC at the take cent the tick the bid is there, `requote_same_wire` past the TTL), a short book's cover through the S4 gate exactly as a live one (`short_reduce_unproven` / `s4_unproven` hold it; `_short_open_refusal` is never on a cover's path). A plan that is not a reduce is `frozen_reduce_only`, never sent (belt and braces: the sign flip already flattens the target to 0). Never an increase, never a flip, never a new episode; `books_live` does not count it. The row carries the marker `frozen_reduce` in `reason` (kept through adoption, the terminal write and an 'unknown' cancel: `_adopt_reason`): step O lets such a rest STAND on the frozen book (`_frozen_reduce_stands`; every other rest is cancelled under the freeze's name as before, `_cancel_frozen_open`), and the fill path names a fill past the ledger `frozen_excess_sold` on the row's receipt (`{frozen_excess: {shares, usd, px}}`, cumulative) instead of the overfill trip — the row was sized inside the venue's own reading and its fill is inside the row's quantity (`_frozen_excess`), so the sale cannot pass zero on the venue; a venue reporting MORE than the row asked for is the overfill it always was. The ledger books what it held (`book_sell`, `_book_mirror_sell`, unchanged). At venue 0 the next agreement read thaws the book and it closes as any flat book does. `frozen_reduce` counts a placement. Knob: `MIRROR_FROZEN_EXITS` (rules, default ON; the environment may only turn it OFF — `frozen_exits_off`, the frozen book reads and cancels as before). The loss stop and every increase refusal gate increases ONLY (`_tick_book` reads `inc_refusal` on the add action alone; `_frozen_exit` never reads it): pinned. Pinned: `test_e5_a_frozen_long_book_sells_toward_his_exit_sized_on_the_venue_and_names_the_excess`, `…_book_77_shape…`, `…_the_reduce_is_sized_on_the_venue_not_the_ledger…`, `…_never_places_a_buy_that_increases…`, `…_read_only_when_the_venue_or_his_market_read_is_missing`, `…_a_co_held_slug_refuses…`, `…_the_knob_only_lowers_the_rail…`, `…_only_placement_lost_and_venue_ledger_disagree_follow_his_exit`, `…_the_loss_stop_never_blocks_a_frozen_exit…`, `…_the_frozen_reduce_rest_stands_across_ticks…`, `…_any_other_rest_on_a_frozen_book_is_still_cancelled…`, `…_adopted_after_a_lost_response_keeps_its_marker`, `…_a_frozen_short_books_cover_goes_through_s4…`, `…_refused_unproven_exactly_as_a_live_one`, `…_never_adds_to_the_short…`.
- P3 **a lost close marked lost once** (`_reconcile_lost_close`, the `unattributed` branch): the seven books' CLOSE rows sat `placing` for good — read, searched in the trade log and re-frozen `close: unattributed` every ~6 s. Past `le._LOST_FILL_WINDOW_S` (20 min, the nothing_sold branch's own rule: inside it the log may still name the seller) the row is marked `lost` ONCE (`_mark_lost`, `order_lost`) with the venue's own reading on the receipt (`{close, sold, sellers, held, qty, venue, at}`, `_SQL_ORDER_RECEIPT`), the book's order slot is freed, and the next tick reads no trade log and re-emits nothing; the book stays frozen by name for a human (venue ≠ ledger), and the frozen exit sells what the venue still holds when he is gone. Nothing else on the path changes. Pinned: `test_e5_an_unattributed_lost_close_is_marked_lost_once_past_the_window_with_the_venues_reading`.
- Render-ops: `mirror-register` (DO) and the read-only `mirror-frozen` (every frozen book: venue / ledger / manual / registered, his net, the target, frozen since, the last plan's `frozen_exit` verdict, the last `frozen_reduce` order; then the register rows). The `sql: arg must be one of …` line is regenerated from the case labels in order. Census keys inserted before the pinned last key: `frozen_reduce`, `frozen_exits_off`, `frozen_venue_unread`, `frozen_coheld`, `frozen_venue_flat`, `frozen_no_his_exit`, `frozen_reduce_only`, `frozen_excess_sold`, `registered_books`, `registered_sign_refused`, `registered_unreadable`.
- **E5 v2 — the review's folds (hard2/E5_review.md).** F1 (critical): step R walks the account before step O books fills, so `r.venue` is stale by any fill the tick books on the book; the frozen exit never runs on the TRANSITION tick (`was_frozen`, captured when the book's tick begins — a one-tick disagreement is not evidence of a lost response; the plan says `held: transition_tick`) and refuses `frozen_fill_this_tick` on a tick that booked a fill on the book after the walk (`t.filled_books`, written by `_book_fill` after its commit; a standing frozen reduce keeps standing — its leaves and the venue moved in step). F2: a frozen seat set for an act in flight moves by the leg-signed fill inside `_book_fill`, so the replace re-plan (`_plan_seat`) and the clamps read the venue as the fill left it. F3 (the owner's option b): a REGISTERED BOOK NEVER THAWS — the register is an audit row and the third term of the freeze comparison only; `_frozen_venue_own` no longer subtracts it (registered shares are the book's to exit toward his net, exactly as a frozen book's: book 77 registered 1,128 with him gone sells the 1,128 and never buys), and a live book whose slug carries a row (a hand-written row: the preset registers frozen books alone) is refused every increase by name (`registered_no_increase`). After the registered shares are sold the book reads venue 0 against explained = registered and stays frozen `frozen_venue_flat` until the row is removed by hand — the worker never writes the register. F4: on every refusal past the eligibility and knob checks (`frozen_venue_unread`, `frozen_coheld`, `frozen_venue_flat`, `frozen_venue_unexplained`, `frozen_reduce_only`, `frozen_no_his_exit`, a plan with no side) a standing frozen reduce is cancelled under the refusal's name (`_frozen_refuse`, an exit's cancel). M1: a `manual` row in a live state the manual read does not count (`submitting`) is co-held (`_SQL_SLUG_COHELD`), and a `placement_lost` book sells no more than its leg plus what its own lost rows asked for (`_SQL_LOST_QTY`, `_lost_bound`; past it, or unreadable, `frozen_venue_unexplained`; a `venue_ledger_disagree` book has no such bound). L1 the knob's comment; L2 `mirror-register` on a book id with no row prints `REFUSED: no mirror_books row with id N`. Pinned: `backend/tests/test_e5_review_pins.py` (the four findings as straight tests, the review's mutant killers and rails), `test_e5_a_registered_book_never_thaws_it_exits_the_registered_shares_toward_his_net_and_never_buys`, `test_e5_a_live_book_with_a_register_row_reads_it_as_manual_in_its_seat_and_never_increases`, `test_e5_a_placement_lost_book_sells_no_more_than_its_ledger_plus_its_lost_rows_ask_for`. Census keys added before the pinned last key: `frozen_fill_this_tick`, `frozen_venue_unexplained`, `registered_no_increase`.
- **E5 v3 — the re-check's folds on L2 (hard2/E5_review_v3.md; fix-then-land, no critical or high).** V3-1: the placement_lost bound counts only lost rows that ADD to the leg (`_lost_bound` keys on `_order_action`; a lost CLOSE — the seven books' own shape after P3 — explains no surplus). V3-2: a freeze counts its census name and writes its recent entry on a transition or a reason NEW to the book (an overfill on a frozen book still counts); the disagreement branch of `_tick_book` re-freezes an already-frozen book under its own reason without a new event (the freeze's age and the tick's `frozen_reasons` gauge move, the census and the recent list do not), and P3's mark writes its one recent entry itself. V3-3: a read that failed this tick (`frozen_venue_unread`: the venue, his market, the co-hold) leaves the standing frozen reduce alone — the plan is unknown, not unwanted. V3-4: `mirror-register` takes only a book frozen `placement_lost` or `venue_ledger_disagree` (the reasons the exit follows; any other freeze registered would have been stuck for good). V3-5: the review's fourteen pins (`test_e5_review_v3_pins.py`) exercise F1's two guards and F2's seat move behaviourally, where before only a census name covered them.
- Left out, by design: no auto-thaw of `venue_ledger_disagree` without a register row; no adoption of a fill into the register; the increase path untouched; a book whose venue holds LESS than its ledger (a lost close's sale) cannot be registered (the sign rule) — its stale ledger is a human's to correct; the durable per-order post-only latch (task 30) is separate; no `mirror-unregister` preset (a sold-out registered book's row is removed by hand).

## 26. L1 (2026-09-07) — the loss-stop re-arm restarts the window

Owner 16:51Z: "Let's rearm completely and let's get everything running with all the upgrades we just did!" The operator re-arm (render-ops `mirror-rearm`, confirm=DO) deleted `mirror_loss_stop` and the worker placed for 16 minutes; at 17:07:45Z it re-tripped itself — `{"sum": -5023.9545, "books": 214, "limit": 5000.0}` — on the morning's losses (tripped 09:46:36Z at -5,002.58), which `_SQL_LOSS_SUM`'s trailing 24 h kept until the next morning: every re-arm re-tripped within a tick or two of any new loss. A re-arm that cannot hold is not a re-arm. The limit does not move (`MIRROR_LOSS_STOP_USD`, `capped_env` 5000; the environment may only lower it); the window's START does.

The rule: the loss sum counts only what happened AFTER the newest re-arm. `mirror-rearm` is ONE statement that deletes the stop and upserts `mirror_loss_rearm` = `{"at": now() ISO, "by": "render-ops", "prior": <the deleted stop's value or null>}` (a data-modifying CTE, self-contained; the read-back SELECT beside it as before). The worker (`_loss_stop`, where `_global_guards` read the sum) reads the key beside the stop's — a raised read is a stop exactly as the stop key's, a key with no parseable tz-aware `at` reads as ABSENT and logs once (fail closed toward the FULL window, never a shorter one) — and hands `_SQL_LOSS_SUM` its ONE parameter, the window's start `_loss_window_start` = GREATEST(now − 24 h, at): both arms and the `books` count clock by `$1::timestamptz`, the text carries no interval of its own. A re-arm older than 24 h changes nothing. The receipt a trip writes carries `since` (the start's ISO) and `rearmed_at` (or null) beside sum/books/limit, so mirror-state shows what window tripped; the quiet-tick mode line prints `loss=<sum>/<limit> since <HH:MM>` beside the day rail, before `venue=` and the trailing dict (the logs action keeps 400 characters of a line; `main()` hands `_mode_line` the tick's reading, `_last_loss` — a module global like `_last_mode`, not a stats key: the health endpoint serves 40 keys and an ON tick already fills them). A STANDING stop key holds whatever the re-arm key says: only the preset's DELETE clears it; the exit carve-out (reduces pass under the stop), the day cap, the receipt write path and `_increases_refusal`'s order are untouched.

Pinned (`backend/tests/test_l1_rearm_window.py`; the statement and the preset executed against Postgres in `test_mirror_loss_sum_real_pg.py`): the start with no key, at a re-arm inside 24 h, at the edge for one older; the statement's text (`$1::timestamptz` in all three places, no interval, no now(); the fake reads the window from the parameter and refuses a text with its own); the tripped stop of 17:07:45Z (sum −5023.95) with a re-arm half an hour before the tick and a $100 loss after it reads −100, not −5,123.95, and the candidate opens, where the same rows without the key trip on −5,123.95; the receipt's `since` / `rearmed_at`; malformed (no `at`, a naive `at`, a non-string, a non-object, bad JSON) → absent, one warning across ticks, and no stop where nothing old stands; unreadable → stop, no sum read, no receipt, the reduce out; a standing stop holds against a newer re-arm key and the reduce passes under it; the preset's statement (yaml-loaded, grepped verbatim; executed: the stop deleted, the prior carried, the upsert on a second run, the SELECT reading both rows); the mode line's fragment (five characters of `since`, the quiet line unchanged); the limit and the refusal order unchanged.

## 27. L2 (2026-09-07) — the copy sleeve's breaker, read over the mirror's window

Owner 18:3xZ, twenty minutes after the L1 re-arm: "Turn the trip off (reset it)." The 18:23:39Z tick refused 14 increases under `loss_breaker` with no `mirror_loss_stop` key standing: that census name is not the mirror's stop but the copy sleeve's own 24 h breaker (`live_executor._loss_breaker_tripped`, `PMUS_LOSS_BREAKER_USD` 5000), which `_global_guards` reads first among the increase-only guards. It sums `pnl` over EVERY `live_orders` row settled or cashed out in a fixed trailing 24 h — and the mirror keeps its position as one standing `live_orders` row (lane `mirror`), which settles there — so the morning's −5,000 tripped it for the rest of the day whatever `mirror_loss_rearm` said. Two rails counted the same losses over two different windows, and the wider one won.

The rule: the mirror reads the sleeve's sum from the start ITS stop uses. `_loss_breaker_sum(pool, since=None)` is the sleeve's reading — the 24 h text unchanged with no `since`, `settled_at > $1::timestamptz` with one — and `_loss_breaker_tripped(pool)` (the copy lane's call, signature and text untouched) wraps it. `_global_guards` reads the stop key first (once, `t.stop`, reused by the stop step: behind a standing or unreadable stop nothing else is read, as the L1 review pinned, and the sleeve reads its full window), else the re-arm key ONCE per tick (`t.rearm`, reused by `_loss_stop`), computes `_loss_window_start` off it exactly as the stop does, hands the sum that instant, and refuses `loss_breaker` on the sleeve's own threshold; a raised re-arm read is the FULL window here (the wider one; `_loss_stop` then stops the tick on that same read), a malformed key reads as absent, an unreadable ledger is `loss_breaker_unreadable` as before. The reading is published as `sleeve=<sum>/<limit>` on the quiet-tick mode line beside `loss=` (`_last_sleeve`, a module global like `_last_loss`), and render-ops `loss-breaker` prints both windows of the sleeve's rows by lane, whale and status, then the newest settled rows.

Pinned (`backend/tests/test_l2_sleeve_window.py`): the sum helper's two texts and the parameter it passes; the tripped helper's signature and 24 h call unchanged; with the re-arm half an hour back and a ledger reading −6,000 over 24 h but −100 since the re-arm, the candidate opens and the census carries no `loss_breaker`; with no key, the same ledger trips on the full window and the line is logged; a re-arm older than 24 h, a malformed key and an unreadable one each read the full window (the unreadable one a stop as L1 pinned, the reduce still out); one read of the key per tick; the mode line's `sleeve=` fragment; the preset's statement and its place in the error line; the copy lane's `maybe_execute` call untouched.

## 28. E6 (2026-09-07) — the tick's time, measured and budgeted

Owner 18:3xZ / 19:1xZ ("Need everything running and running at mirror to him"). The mirror was placing again (L2 live 18:55Z, E5 live 19:26Z) but the tick ran 80-127 s with 74-77 live books (19:10Z 79.8 s / venue_calls 89, 19:21Z 98.7 s / 112, 19:28Z 127.2 s) and his median entry latency measured 86.8 s (probe 18:49Z, lat_p90 382.8 s): a tick that long IS the latency. Every existing book was read every tick (U12), most of them quiet — on target, nothing open, no fill of his for an hour — behind the 0.35 s pacer, in front of the candidates; and after each deploy (four in the hour) the process-local terminal memos were empty, so every book on an EXPIRED market was re-read once and every candidate walked again (venue_halted 38 / 78, cand_unread_capped 335 / 325 at 18:59Z / 19:28Z). Three parts, all in `mirror_live.py`; no rail raised, the env may only lower; pacing, the per-market data-API throttle, what a read decides and order placement are untouched. **Part 1, measured**: the tick publishes a bounded `timing` block — seconds (1 dp) for `walk` (step R), `orders` (step O), `books` (step B, wall), `books_venue` (the books' paced venue calls: their quote reads and every paced write the walk sent, summed per call, so under the parallel walk it can exceed `books`), `books_data` (their per-market data-API reads, the same way), `candidates`; the counts beside them (`venue_calls`, `snap_market_reads`); the books by outcome class (`read`, `on_target`, `placed`, `no_mark`, `terminal_skipped`, `quiet_skipped`); the budget's numbers (`budget`, `quiet_budget`, `quiet_reads`, `cand_budget`, `map_cap`, `cand_cap`). It rides INSIDE the `short` block (`.detail.short.timing`): the health endpoint serves 40 top-level keys and an ON tick fills them (pinned never truncated), `integ` is pinned under 40 of its own, `fills_dedup` is the key the sanitizer drops on a capped tick and is pinned by value. The quiet-tick mode line prints `t=walk/orders/books/cands read=N quiet=N placed=N` right after the day rail (before `loss=` / `venue=`, inside the 400 characters the logs action keeps), nothing on a backed-off tick; the engine-diagnostic probe prints the raw heartbeat whole, so no probe change.

**Part 2, the budget** (`VENUE_CALLS_PER_TICK = capped_env("MIRROR_TICK_VENUE_CALLS", 60, floor 20)` — its own name: `MIRROR_VENUE_CALLS_PER_TICK` is E2's soft guard on writes and candidate reads, 80, untouched; `TICK_TARGET_S` 25, `HOT_S` 600, `QUIET_EVERY_TICKS` 3, `CAND_MIN_PER_TICK` 10). The rule, exactly: a book is HOT — read every tick, never budgeted — when this process has never read it (or the row's `last_plan.at` is not the one this process wrote: another hand moved it), its last read was not on target with nothing placed and nothing open, it is not live (frozen: E5's exit path), an order is open or non-terminal on it, its last plan was `exit_take` / `take_at_his_level` / `reduce_unfilled` (`QUIET_EXIT_PLANS`, read off `last_reason` and the plan's `kind` / `reason`), its market WOKE this tick (`t.woken`: a fill ingested late — the poller catching up after a deploy, a backfill — carries an old stamp but the wake is the strongest money-in-motion evidence the process has; review HIGH-2), or a fill of his on its market sits inside `HOT_S` (the fills the tick already holds; an unreadable stamp is hot). Every other book is QUIET: read on the third tick after its last read (`_quiet_memo`: book id → {seq, quiet, at}, process-local like `_cand_cursor`), skipped `book_quiet_skipped` before then — plan `no_plan` with `read_on` (the tick it is read on), `tick`, `last_read`, and the prior plan's `flat_since`, `short_proof`, `mark`, `bid`, `ask`, `exit_px_src` carried (`_SKIP_CARRIED`: the next read and a sibling's game room read them off the row; the mark also lands on `t.marks`); a quiet book DUE for its read that the budget cannot fit (`quiet_budget = max(0, budget − steps R and O − one read per hot book the terminal memo will not skip − CAND_MIN_PER_TICK)`, reserved at the decision; a memo-skipped book makes no call and costs nothing, review MEDIUM-2; the candidates' floor is reserved so the due tick stays inside the budget, MEDIUM-4) joins a FIFO QUEUE (`_quiet_deferred`, book id → the tick it was queued on) and the next ticks read the queue's head FIRST, `max(quiet_budget, DEFERRED_MIN_PER_TICK = 10)` of it a tick — the floor so a budget the hot books alone spend (0) can never starve a quiet book — the overflow staying queued with `read_on` its turn (review MEDIUM-1: as built the whole deferred cohort was read next tick whatever the budget, 102 reads at a budget of 20 after a restart). The rule, exactly: a queued book's worst wait past its due tick is `ceil(N_quiet / max(quiet_budget, DEFERRED_MIN_PER_TICK))` ticks, and no tick reads more quiet books than that slot count; at today's ~14 quiet books that is one tick. On a skipped book the standing-row close, step M and the episode close (`_maybe_close_episode` with step M's market reading, the row's last target and the carried flat clock; `vanished` / `venue_flat` handed False — those closes wait for a read tick) still run; no cancel, no placement, no venue call; the skip writes `last_reason` and `last_plan` only (`_SQL_BOOK_SKIP`: the last read's `his_net` / `venue_net` / snapshot / drift stand on the row, review LOW-1). The memo and the queue forget a book the moment it closes and any book the walk no longer lists (LOW-2). `QUIET_EXIT_PLANS` are CENSUS names (`_mirror_stop`), never written as a row's `last_reason` or a plan's `kind` / `reason`, so the exit clause matches hand-written rows only; money is covered by the other clauses — an exit's take leaves an open order or a placement and a placed / open book's verdict is never quiet, an unfilled reduce leaves the target off the ledger (LOW-4, pinned). The candidate stage runs after the books and takes what the budget leaves — `cand_budget = max(10, 60 − calls so far)`, map reads and quote reads together, map reads first (a candidate maps before it reads); the MAP cap (the 19:42Z tick: `map_reads_capped` 108 at `map_venue_read` 10, ~$100k of his 24 h flow unmapped by the old cap) is what sits ABOVE the quote reads' floor, `max(ms.MAP_READS_PER_TICK, cand_budget − CAND_MIN_PER_TICK)` — the old cap the budget's floor, never its ceiling; an operator who SET `MIRROR_MAP_READS` lowered a rail and it is honoured as a ceiling under the share — and the quote-read cap is `min(MAX_MARKETS_PER_TICK, max(CAND_MIN_PER_TICK, cand_budget − map reads))` (the U12 text `t.cand_reads >= MAX_MARKETS_PER_TICK` stands beside it): the quote reads never fall under their floor, so the mapped candidates behind the resolver reads are read on the very tick at the floor (review HIGH-1: as built the resolver spent the whole share and they were not), and the tick's total stays ≤ budget + CAND_MIN_PER_TICK. With ~75 books: the hot ones (20-35) every tick, a third of the quiet ones, 10-20 candidates. Fail closed: an empty memo (a restart), a malformed memo entry, an unreadable row or plan reads the book; the stage never starves below 10. **Part 3, the restart artifact**: both terminal memos are kept under ONE `ingestion_state` key, `mirror_terminal_memo` = `{cand: [[whale, cid, until]…], book: [[whale, cid, until, state]…], at}`, bounded (entries whose `until` has passed dropped on write, at most 4000 kept, the soonest to expire dropped first), written at most once per 60 s and only when the bounded snapshot changed (a failed write is logged and retried on a later tick), read ONCE at boot into `_terminal_until` / `_terminal_book_until` / `_terminal_book_state` (expired and malformed entries dropped, and a book entry whose state is not one of `ms.STATE_TERMINAL` — the only states the memo's writer records — dropped too: a HALTED entry in the key would skip a live book for its `until`, review LOW-3); an unreadable or malformed key is the empty memo (today's behaviour), never a raise; nothing is written before the boot read was made (a first tick refused ahead of it — `tables_absent`, `intent_guard_unreadable` — would otherwise persist a fresh process's empty memo over the old process's, review MEDIUM-5).

Pinned (`backend/tests/test_e6_tick_budget.py`, 29 tests, 33 items with the malformed-memo parametrization): the constants and `capped_env`'s range; `book_quiet_skipped` before the pinned last key; the timing block's keys, types and served home (top level not truncated, `integ` under 40, `fills_dedup` whole); the outcome classes; the per-call sum under the parallel walk; the mode line's fragment, its slot and its absence on a backed-off tick; a book with an open order / frozen / not on target / with his fill inside HOT_S / with an exit plan standing / woken is never skipped; the rotation (read on tick 1 and 4, skipped on 2, 3 and 5 with `read_on`); 30 quiet books at the floor budget: 8 read on tick 4 (20 − 2 − the candidates' floor), 22 queued with `read_on` their turn, the queue's first ten read on tick 5 and the next ten on tick 6, the last two ahead of the due cohort on tick 7, then never more than ten a tick and every book read again within six ticks; the quiet budget's arithmetic (a memo-skipped book costs nothing, a woken book is hot); `QUIET_EXIT_PLANS` census names only, a reduced book hot through its verdict; step M and the standing row close a skipped book; the flat close on a skipped book with the carried clock; the skip's carried fields and the sibling's room; a row written by another hand, a malformed memo, an unreadable plan, a cleared memo → read; the quiet block's place in `_tick_book` and no order path; the candidate stage's share (33 behind 25 books, the floor of 10, the cap under the floor, 40 for a lone book, the quote-read cap never under 10 behind the map reads); 28 map reads at budget 60 with 20 hot books (38 − 10) and the walk going on past the map cap, 10 at the floor, an operator-lowered map cap a ceiling; the source order in `_tick` / `tick_once`; the memo written bounded, once per 60 s, on change, retried after a failed write; read once at boot with expired and malformed entries dropped; malformed and unreadable keys empty; the every-guard / refusal-order and served-cap pins of the worker file re-run. In `test_mirror_live_worker.py` the E2 candidate-count pins (25 / 46 books, the r2 / r3 request counts) now read the budget's arithmetic, the P2 short-proof pins take the skip tick, the full-game memo pin takes A's quiet tick, and the served-prefix pin carries the new name. The review's pins (`backend/tests/test_e6_review_pins.py`, 26): the nine defects above as they were found and fixed (a woken book read whatever its fills' stamps; the mapped candidates read behind the resolver reads at the floor; the deferred queue read under the slots, FIFO, never en masse; memo-skipped books costing the quiet share nothing; the due tick inside the budget; no memo write before the boot read; the skip leaving `his_net` / `venue_net` on the row; a closed book forgotten; a HALTED memo entry dropped) beside the rails the review found standing.

## 29. E7 (2026-09-07) — the candidate rotation stops re-reading what has not changed; the data-API wait measured

Owner 18:3xZ / 19:1xZ ("Need everything running and running at mirror to him"). With E6 live (20aacb3, 21:03Z) the tick was 47.9 / 51.3 s (21:15Z / 21:20Z; venue calls 56 / 54 of 60) and its timing block said where the rest went: the candidate stage 25.9 / 13.3 s with `map_reads_capped` 114 / 86 — 100+ markets waiting on a resolver read every tick — and the 24 h refusal census (`cand-refusals` 21:17Z) said those reads were the SAME ANSWERS every rotation: `unmapped` 276 markets / 1,667 rows / ~$147k on 183 markets in the day, each row a paced resolver call for one unchanged answer (por-est-aro-2026-09-07-total-0pt5 unmapped @20:04 @20:20 @20:35 @20:51 @21:09) under a FLAT, process-local 900 s memo (`_unmapped_until`, `ms.UNMAPPED_TTL_S`, the C2 reason "a market unlisted at first sight may be listed a minute later") that no fill of his released; and `no_mark` 179 markets / 1,121 rows / $1.01M — the morning's tennis, his last fill hours old, the matches over, the venue still OPEN with an empty book (the U10 per-market refusal `rules.mirror_target` returns for a candidate) — re-read at every rotation slot with NO memo at all, a paced quote read AND a per-market data-API read each time (a candidate's snap read follows its quote read one for one). The rule, exactly, all in `mirror_live.py`; nothing raised, env may only lower; this decides WHEN a candidate is read, never what a read decides (mapping, admission, `snapshot_stale`, the throttle's rate, `READ_PACING_S` and a BOOK's reads — E6's rule — are untouched). **The no_mark memo**: a CANDIDATE whose quote read found the market OPEN (the venue's own state on that read) with no mark (`r.mark` None: an empty book, or a bid with no ask) is remembered per (whale, condition_id) in `_no_mark_until` for `NO_MARK_TTL_S = capped_env("MIRROR_NO_MARK_TTL_S", 900, floor 60)` and skipped under `cand_no_mark_skipped` at both walk sites (the loop and `_name_unread`) — no slot, no quote read, no per-market read, no refusal row — until the memo runs; NEVER written on a HALTED / SUSPENDED / PREOPEN read (those reopen: D1's rule), a read naming no state or a read that raised (`no_quote` is _bbo's name for those: a transient or an untellable, never memoised), and NEVER from a book's read (`_tick_book` never calls the writer; a book on an OPEN empty market is read every tick as before). `no_price` is not memoised either: it is his LEVEL unreadable off his own fills, a fact about his fills — the read had a mark — that changes exactly when a new fill of his lands. **Release by his fills**: both candidate memos store the read's `at` beside the `until` (`_unmapped_memo[(whale, cid)] = (at, ttl)`, `_no_mark_memo[(whale, cid)] = at`); at the walk, BEFORE either memo is checked, a memoised market whose newest fill of his is NEWER than the memo's `at` — the `last_ts` the candidate order already ranks on, handed back by the same one query (`ms.active_conditions(..., stamped=True)`; no new read) — or that WOKE this tick (a fill ingested late carries an old stamp; E6 review HIGH-2's reasoning) is read this tick, the memo dropped and counted `cand_memo_released`. C2's concern answered by evidence instead of a clock: a market he is still trading is re-checked at once, one he stopped trading is not. A stamp the walk could not read (None, a naive datetime) releases nothing and grows nothing: no evidence, the memo's own TTL stands. **Growth, the unmapped memo only**: a market read unmapped again (any of the four exits that write that memo: `unmapped`, `map_source_unverified`, the grammar class's refusals, `long_token_unknown`) whose previous entry still stands in the dict and with NO fill of his since that read (the fills the read holds; a fill with no readable stamp counts as one; a wake counts as one) doubles its TTL, 900 → 1,800 → 3,600 = `UNMAPPED_TTL_MAX_S` (a constant, no env: nothing to lower under the base); a release by fill, a fill among the re-read's rows, an entry cleared by hand or a deploy resets it to the base. An expired entry is kept an hour past its `until` for that judgement, then pruned (`_prune_cand_memos`). The no_mark memo keeps the flat TTL (the book may fill in; his fill releases it anyway). `map_reads_capped` stays a no-verdict: never memoised, read again next tick.

**The memos persist** across a deploy under ONE `ingestion_state` key of their own, `mirror_cand_memo` = `{unmapped: [[whale, cid, until, at, ttl]…], no_mark: [[whale, cid, until, at]…], at}` — E6's rules exactly: entries whose `until` has passed (or with no readable `at` / TTL beside them) dropped on write, at most `_TERMINAL_MEMO_MAX` (4000) per list with the soonest to expire dropped first, written at most once per `TERMINAL_MEMO_WRITE_S` (60 s) and only when the bounded snapshot changed, a failed write logged and retried, read ONCE at boot beside the terminal memos (the same `_terminal_memo_loaded` flag), nothing written before that read (review MEDIUM-5). A key of its own rather than two more lists in `mirror_terminal_memo`, which the brief asked for: the E6 pins fix that key's exact shape (`{cand, book, at}`, `set(snapshot) == {cand, book}`) and the pins won. Fail closed, always toward a READ: an unreadable or malformed key is the empty memo (a deploy reads everything, today's behaviour) and is logged; a malformed entry is dropped — the wrong length or types, an `until` not ahead, an `at` in the future or past its `until`, a memo longer than today's rail (`NO_MARK_TTL_S` lowered since it was written: the lowered rail is honoured, the market read), an unmapped TTL off the ladder [900, 3600] or an `until` past `at + ttl`. So after the deploy a memoised market spends no slot (pinned: no quote read, no resolver read, no per-market read on the first tick), the loaded `at` is what his next fill is judged against, and the loaded TTL is what the next unmapped re-read grows from. **Part B, the data-API wait measured**: `_market_snap` awaits `whale_exits.market_positions`, which waits on the PROCESS-WIDE `ratelimit.data_api_throttle()` (`settings().data_api_max_rps`, 6.0, shared with the live poller, the backfill and the reconciler) before its request; `market_positions` now takes an optional `timing` dict and adds the seconds it spent inside the throttle's wait and inside the request (a call cut short inside the wait is all wait), and the tick publishes `short.data_api = {books_data_wait, books_data_req, data_rps}` beside E6's timing block — a sibling block, because E6 pins the timing block's keys exactly (`books_data`, their sum, stands there) — served whole through `.detail.short.data_api`, so the next decision (whether a hot book's per-market read can be reused inside a window when no fill of his landed) is made from numbers: at 21 / 26 snap reads with `books_data` 21.8 / 35.9 s the split says how much of that is the shared throttle's queue. Nothing about the read, its budget, the throttle's rate or `_confirm_gone` (byte-identical) moves.

Pinned (`backend/tests/test_e7_cand_memo.py`, 26 tests, 52 items with the malformed-entry parametrizations): the constants and `capped_env`'s range for `MIRROR_NO_MARK_TTL_S` (lowered honoured, raised refused, the growth cap a constant); the two census names before E6's key with the E6 tail (`keys[-2:]`) standing; an OPEN empty book memoised — skipped `cand_no_mark_skipped` for the TTL with no quote read, no per-market read, no slot, no refusal row — read again when the TTL runs and memoised again on THAT read; a stamp older than the memo's `at` holds, a newer one releases (`cand_memo_released`), the market read that tick and memoised on the new read, a book opening at once when it is quoted; a woken market released whatever its stamp; an unreadable stamp (absent, naive, junk) releases nothing and a fill with no stamp grows nothing; the unmapped TTL 900 → 1,800 → 3,600 → 3,600 across re-reads with nothing of his, reset by his newer stamp, by a fill among the rows, by a cleared entry, and never grown while his newest fill on the market is inside the hour (review MEDIUM-2: a market he entered at first sight and the venue lists twenty minutes later is re-read at the base TTL); every writer of the unmapped memo goes through the one writer and `map_reads_capped` is never memoised; an env-lowered no_mark TTL honoured to the second; a book's read writes neither memo (behaviour and source); HALTED, raised and state-less `no_mark` reads never memoised, an OPEN bid-only book memoised; `no_price` not memoised; a memo-skipped candidate writes no refusal row, the released read its own; a memoised market the cap left unread not named `cand_unread_capped` unless his fill would have released it (naming drops nothing); both memos persisted under the bound, once per 60 s, on change, the failed write retried, the terminal key's shape untouched; the boot read with expired entries dropped, the deploy re-reading nothing memoised, the loaded `at` and TTL in force; nine malformed unmapped shapes and seven malformed no_mark shapes each dropped with the market read; a malformed or unreadable key empty, logged, read once; nothing written before the boot read; the prune's hour; the data-API block's keys, types and served home with E6's block still exactly its keys; the wait and the request measured on the books' reads only (a slow throttle lands in `books_data_wait`, a slow request in `books_data_req`, a candidate's read in neither); `market_positions`' `timing` optional, measured on a refused read too, the request unchanged; the source order (release before the memo checks before `_walk_candidate`; the boot read and the persist beside E6's); the stamped query the one query; the E6 pins and the worker's served-prefix and gate-counter rails re-run. In `test_mirror_live_worker.py`: the autouse fixture resets the four new globals, `_comparable` drops `short.data_api` (wall time), the served-prefix pin carries the two names (−59 → −61, −60 → −62, the convention every builder followed), the OPEN-empty `_bbo` pin clears the new memo between its fresh worlds as it clears D1's, and one E7 test emits both names for the file's every-name census; in `test_mirror_live_day_cap.py` the two U10 pins that re-read c1..c3 OPEN-empty in a second world of one process clear it beside the E1 clear they already make (the process remembers the market: that is the rule).

## 30. E9 (2026-09-07) — the wake fast path; every fill of his on a market we hold answered or named

Owner 22:4xZ ("I need it being proportional, directional and only taking buys within 1c of him and sells (or exits) within 1c of his price. This means latency must be flawless and exceptional"). With E6 + E7 live (6ffbbc1, E7 at 22:33Z) the tick was 14.9 s at 22:44:50Z (walk 0.2, orders 3.2, books 10.0 — the data-API wait 9.5 s of the 12.1 s summed over 9 snap reads at `data_rps` 6.0 shared with the pollers, request 2.7 s — candidates 0.8) and his fills' ingest lag a median of −1 s every hour (we see the fill as it lands; p90 81-137 s is the poll catching up): ingestion is not the median's bottleneck. Yet his fill on a market we hold reached our FIRST order after a median of 27 s (22:00Z), 39 s (21:00Z), 66 s (20:00Z) and a p90 of 468-661 s, answered inside 30 min for 51 of 113 / 225 of 347 / 355 of 574 fills (`latency-census` 22:49Z); the paired day (22:49Z) read `cents_over` at a median of 0.0 across 47 markets — the price rule holds — and the gap is the FRACTION (0.2-0.4% of his dollars on his three biggest winners). So with the tick at 15 s and ingest at 0, the median is one tick of waiting plus the walk's order, and the p90 a market that waited its turn or was refused with no row that said why. Two parts in `mirror_live.py`; every rail holds, nothing raised, no env knob of its own (nothing to lower under five markets and two seconds that is not the rails themselves); identity-only mapping untouched; entries rest and take at HIS cent and exits within `MIRROR_EXIT_TOL` exactly as before, because the fast tick plans and places through the same functions. **Part 1, answered or named**: every plan write (`_write_plan`, and the quiet skip's own write) carries `last_plan.his_fills_seen` — one entry per fill of his the tick holds on the market (`ms.his_fills`, which now also selects the row's `detected_at`) that no earlier plan recorded: `{id, ts, det, at, order, name}` — the fill's trades id, its stamp, the ingest's clock, the tick's `at`, our order row id when THAT tick placed on the book (`t.placed_books`), else the plan's reason as its census name (`rules.plan_reason_key`: `on_target`, `rest_placed`, `game_cap_full`, `no_mark`, `mirror_loss_stop`, `book_quiet_skipped`, `market_unreadable`, `closing_…`, …). Written once and never renamed — the tick that first planned with the fill in hand is its answer — bounded at `HIS_FILLS_SEEN_MAX` = 20 (the newest fills kept, by stamp then id); a fill with neither id nor stamp is not recorded; an unreadable prior list is an empty one. No venue call, no new table. The read-only `fills-answered` preset (render-ops `sql`, the help line regenerated from the case labels) reads every fill of his of the last 6 h on a market with a book against the row's entry for it: by name (`answered` = an order went out that tick, `unseen` = no plan has held the fill yet, else the refusal name) with the count, his dollars and the seconds from his fill to the tick that answered or named it (median, p90), then the top 30 unanswered by his dollars. A memoised CANDIDATE has no plan row to carry the list (a migration is out of scope): its fill is named by the E7 release read's refusal row (`cand-refusals`), not here.

**Part 2, the wake fast path**. (1) The wake now fires on EVERY newly inserted fill of a mirrored whale — BUY and SELL, chain and poll, the copy probe on or off — from the ingestion pipeline where the fill is written (`pipeline.ingest_trade_result` → `_mirror_wake`, after the dedupe gate and before the copy lane's fresh gate; an env-only predicate, `live_executor.mirror_mode`, never a read) and from `_enrich` the moment a chain row learns its condition; the copy lane's own gate keeps its call exactly where the spec 3.1 pins hold it (a second wake for one market is a set add) — E7 review LOW-2 closed by the road the brief named, not by moving the gate's call above the SELL return (test_mirror_live_handoff pins the gate block's text byte for byte). (2) `notify` hands the market to the fast path too (`_FAST_WOKEN`, bounded like `_WOKEN`) and, once `main()` has armed it with the loop's pool, venue adapter and data-API client (`_arm_fast`), schedules ONE fast tick on the running loop (`_fast_run`: the `FAST_TICK_MIN_S` = 2 s floor since the last fast tick waited out, then `fast_tick_once` for the next `FAST_TICK_MAX` = 5 woken markets, the floor again between runs until the woken set drains; outside a loop, or unarmed, nothing is scheduled and the poll's tick reads the market as before). The fast tick runs BEFORE the next full tick and never beside one (the review's fold, the paragraph after this one; never two fast ticks: `_FAST_LOCK`): the full tick's prelude read the same way — the table and column guards, `_read_mode`, the venue, `_global_guards` with the loss rails (SAFE, a tripped guard or the backoff: every woken market `fast_tick_skipped` by that name, nothing read) — then, per woken market, a BOOK through `_tick_book` under its per-book lock (the row re-read under the lock) or, with no book, `_walk_candidate` behind the walk's own checks in the walk's order (the memo release by the wake, the three memos, the E2 guard, the candidate caps): the SAME functions, rails and refusal names — no second planner, no new price rule. It makes no positions walk (it plans on the last full tick's, `_last_walk`, inside `FAST_WALK_MAX_S` = 90 s, which nothing of ours can have moved on a book with nothing open) and no step O (a book with an order open — the DB rows read this fast tick, or the row's `open_order_id` — is left to it, and every sibling with an order open reads as non-terminal so the game's room is unreadable and no increase is sized beside it; an exit is never touched by the cap). BOUNDED and SHARED: the venue calls the fast ticks make since the last full tick (`_fast_calls`) come off that tick's budget (`t.fast_calls`: `_quiet_budget` and `_cand_budget` subtract them) and a fast tick refuses a market once `t.venue_calls + t.fast_calls ≥ VENUE_CALLS_PER_TICK` (`budget_spent`); the E2 soft guard's counter and the ops budget are seeded with the fast ticks' spend the same way (a woken candidate `venue_calls_capped`, an increase `ops_capped`, an exit exempt as ever). FAIL CLOSED, by name: the per-book lock held (step O or the walk has the book — `_lock_for(id).locked()`, asked, never awaited: the lock decides), a full tick in flight whose walk is not yet past the book's whole GAME (`_full_tick.walk_done`; that tick reads the market itself, hot by the fill, and sizes the game in its one fixed order) or that is cancel-only, a candidate beside any full tick in flight, a book that is not live (E5's exit path reads the venue), an order open, no walk to plan on or one older than the bound, a fill booked on the book after that walk (`_last_filled`, the running tick's `filled_books`; E5 review F1) — each `fast_tick_skipped` with the reason on the fast tick's own stats (`fast.skipped`), the lock and walk cases put back for the next fast tick at most `FAST_RETRIES` = 2 times; any error `fast_tick_failed` (the market waits for the full tick, which reads it woken and first). The full tick, at its start, takes the woken markets off the fast path (they are its to read, first), folds the fast ticks' census in, and publishes `short.fast` = `{fast (seconds), n, markets, placed, skipped, failed, calls}` beside E6's timing block (whose keys the E6 pins fix exactly, so a sibling key as E7's `data_api`); the mode line prints ` fast=N` after `placed=`. Census: `fast_tick`, `fast_tick_placed`, `fast_tick_skipped`, `fast_tick_failed`, before E7's pair (the E7 pins hold the last four keys). NOT in scope: the poll cadence, the data-API rate, pacing, the resolver, what a read decides.

**The review's fold (2026-09-08; CRITICAL-1, HIGH-1, MEDIUM-1, MEDIUM-2): the fast tick is SERIALISED against the full tick.** `fast_tick_once` runs under `_TICK_LOCK` — the full tick's own lock, taken inside `_FAST_LOCK` — and `tick_once` WAITS when the hold is a fast tick's (`_fast_holding`, True only between a fast tick's acquire and its `finally`) while keeping its `overlap` return for a FULL tick's hold (the L2 and E9 pins fix that); no await sits between `tick_once`'s check and its acquire. Why: the full tick plans every book off the `_SQL_BOOKS_OPEN` list and the step-O rows it read at its start, and `_tick_book` never re-reads the row — so a fast tick beside it that placed AFTER that read was invisible to it: an IOC take at his cent that filled (`ledger_net` 300 on the row, 0 in the walk's dict) was sized again, 600 held against his 300 (the unique index refuses a second REST, never a second take after a terminal row); and a rest on a sibling, placed while the full tick sat in its candidate stage, was in no `open_by_book` / `nonterminal` the candidate's room read (`_game_exposure` → `_resting_add_usd`), so the game rested $3,335 against its $2,500 cap. Under the lock the full tick reads the row as the fast tick left it (the take booked, on target; the rest standing; the room less the rest, `game_cap_scaled`), and a fast tick that arrives while a full tick runs waits and then reads the market `order_open` / `fill_after_walk`. The fast tick pops its ≤ 5 woken markets only once it HOLDS the lock: a full tick that started while it waited drained `_FAST_WOKEN` at its start (they are its to read, woken first) and the fast tick then finds nothing and returns — never a 10-24 s stale plan on a market the full tick already answered. The 2 s floor is unchanged, and so is the wake-to-placement clock with no full tick in flight (a wake past `WAKE_MIN_GAP_S` still runs the fast tick first: main's resume is one hop behind the fast run's first step, so the fast tick holds the lock, answers the market, and the full tick waits, then reads it `order_open`). `_fast_gate`'s lock / `walk_done` / `full_tick_pending` clauses and `_fast_candidate`'s full-tick clause are dead under the serialisation and kept, harmless, as the fail-closed floor. MEDIUM-2: the full tick's start now SEEDS `t.guard_calls` and `t.ops` from `_fast_guard_calls` / `_fast_ops` (as `fast_tick_once` seeds its own) before resetting the three counters, so the E2 soft guard and the ops budget count the fast ticks' spend once per window (a candidate `venue_calls_capped`, an increase `ops_capped`, an exit exempt as ever) instead of twice. MEDIUM-1: `his_fills`' outer SELECT projects `d.detected_at`, so `his_fills_seen.det` is the ingest's clock on the real schema (the inner CTE alone left it `None` on Postgres; the fake pool hid it). MEDIUM-3 (the preset's second statement carrying its own CTE chain) was folded before the review landed. Pinned in `backend/tests/test_e9_review_pins.py` (the review's 25, its five xfails now passing as written with their harnesses driven through the lock: the take sized once, 300 held; the game ≤ $2,500 with B's plan reading A's rest in the room; the guard and ops seeds; `detected_at` and the second statement on real Postgres) plus the fold's own: a wake landing during a full tick's step O — the fast tick waits, the full tick finishes and places, the fast tick then reads the market `order_open` (one placement, never two); a full tick arriving on a fast tick's hold waits and does not return `overlap`, one arriving on a full tick's hold still does; the fast tick that waited finds its markets drained and places nothing (the pop after the acquire, by source too); the wake-to-placement clock with no full tick in flight unchanged (the builder's pin re-run). In `test_mirror_live_worker.py` the autouse fixture hands each test fresh `_TICK_LOCK` / `_FAST_LOCK` (a contended acquire binds an asyncio.Lock to the loop it waited on, and `asyncio.run` makes one per test) and the hold flag down.

Pinned (`backend/tests/test_e9_fast_path.py`, 27 tests, 35 items with the parametrizations): the constants and the names (no env knob, the E7 and E6 tails exactly); the wake for his SELL and BUY, chain and poll, through `ingest_trade_result` against a fake pool; no wake on a duplicate, a condition-less row, a whale the mirror does not hold or the mirror off; the wake with the copy probe off, reading the deploy alone, before the fresh gate, the enrichment's wake after its UPDATE, the gate's own call standing; `notify` outside a loop or unarmed scheduling nothing; the fast tick reading and placing the woken market inside the floor (the wall clock measured from `notify` to the placement) and touching no other book, the fill answered on the row by our order's id; the bound of five and the two-second floor (seven wakes: five then two, the floor slept twice); the budget shared (the quiet budget and the candidate share read the fast ticks' calls, `budget_spent`, the spend accumulated and reset by the full tick); the E2 guard and the ops budget seeded (an exit exempt); the lock refusal retried twice then left to the full tick, with the source order (the lock asked, the row re-read under it); a full tick in flight (pending until its walk passes the game; cancel-only refused; a candidate refused; a fill it booked refused); a failing fast tick named and the market read by the full tick, the census folded, a raise in the prelude naming every market; the frozen / open-order / no-walk / stale-walk / booked-fill refusals; a sibling with an order open (no increase, `game_unreadable`; the exit still goes); the mode ladder and the loss rails (SAFE, a halt, the loss stop under the full tick's name, the backoff, exits-only answering his SELL); six identical worlds (on target, an increase, no_mark, mode_db_off, the exit take, the exit rest out of tolerance) writing the same `last_reason`, the same plan, the same placements and cancels, the same census names in a full tick and a fast tick; the entry at his cent and the exits within the tolerance at the same wires; `his_fills_seen` naming every fill once with the ingest clock, carrying the order id, bounded at twenty with the newest kept and never renamed; the list on the skip, the unreadable and the closing paths, a keyless fill not recorded, an unreadable prior list empty; `short.fast` (keys, types, the fold, the served surface, the reset, `_comparable`); the mode line's ` fast=N` and the E6 line unchanged; the full tick taking the woken markets off the fast path; two fast ticks never overlapping; the preset read-only with the help line the case labels in order; the E6 / E7 / every-guard / refusal-order / served-cap pins re-run; the docs. In `test_mirror_live_worker.py` the autouse fixture resets the fast path's state, `_comparable` drops `short.fast`, the served-prefix pin carries the four names (−61 → −65, −62 → −66), the W1 / R4 memo-skip pin reads its plan beside the list, and one E9 test emits the four names for the every-name census; `test_e7_review_pins.py`'s LOW-2 xfail is now the pin of the pipeline's wake.

## 31. E10 (2026-09-08) — the mirror's per-market read stops queueing behind telemetry

Owner 22:4xZ ("latency must be flawless and exceptional"). With E9 live (a5a5a25) the tick was 25.3 / 34.5 / 43.9 s at 00:59 / 00:50 / 00:55Z with `short.timing.books` 19.5-24.7 s of wall at `MIRROR_BOOK_CONCURRENCY` 6, and E9's fast tick waits behind the full tick (§30's fold), so a shorter full tick is the lever for every wake that lands mid-tick. The read-only map (hard2/E10_map.md) corrected the premise first: `books_data_wait` 20.9-27.7 s is a SUM PER CALL over 16-20 per-market reads (the E6 / E7 docstrings say so and their pins fix that reading), i.e. ~1.4 s of queue per read of which ~0.8 s was the walk queueing behind its own six siblings, on a process-wide data-API throttle (`ratelimit.Throttle`, `data_api_max_rps` 6.0, one lock and one reserved slot in ARRIVAL order — no priority, no lane) offered ~6.6-7.2 rps by the poller's roster pass (~1.4) and fast lane (~3.6), `positions_sync` (~1.1, bursty) and the mirror (0.5-4.0); and two `/positions` bursts — `whale_exits._fetch_positions` (≤ 48 pages per held whale per 120 s) and `_confirm_gone` — were raw `http.get`s off the throttle entirely, so the venue's real rate ran above the number. **Part 1, the priority lane** (`ratelimit.py`): a slot is now handed out only when it FREES, by one pump task per loop that sleeps until the slot frees and picks the lane AFTER the sleep: a PRIORITY caller (`Throttle.acquire(priority=True)`) takes the next free slot ahead of every waiting normal caller; normal callers (`wait()`, `acquire()`, every `polite_get`) keep FIFO among themselves and so do the priority callers among themselves; the RATE does not change (one slot per 1/rate s whatever the mix — the reservation clock advances by the interval per slot exactly as before; 6.0 rps total, and since E10 the environment may only LOWER it: `data_api_rate()` = min(`settings().data_api_max_rps`, `DATA_API_MAX_RPS` 6.0), which `short.data_api.data_rps` now prints); STARVATION BOUND `PRIORITY_BURST` = 12 consecutive priority slots while a normal caller waits, then one normal slot — twelve is two waves of the six-wide book walk, a bound under one wave hands a telemetry page a slot mid-wave that a money-path read then waits behind, and past two waves a normal caller has waited 2 s at the ceiling; a waiter cancelled in the queue (`_SNAP_READ_TIMEOUT_S`) leaves the lane and is never charged a slot. The priority callers in the process are `whale_exits.market_positions(..., priority=True)` from `mirror_live._market_snap` — the book walk, the fast tick and the candidate read all pass through it — via an explicit keyword argument whose default is False, so no other consumer changes, and (the fold below, MEDIUM-2) the mirror's own vanish confirmation `mirror_live._confirm_gone`; the pinned shape holds: one throttled wait precedes the one GET on either lane, the request (`/positions`, one scoped request, `sizeThreshold=0`, the foreign-row refusal) and the `timing` split untouched, `data_api_throttle().wait()` still the normal branch's literal text (test_whale_exits_market_positions:275-293, test_e7_cand_memo:659).

**Part 2 and part 3, the budget's other side.** `positions_sync_interval_seconds` 300 → 900 (`config.py`): `api_positions`, the table the sync writes, is read by the API alone — the UI's whale profile (`api/queries.whale_profile`) and events view (`api/queries.events_view`) AND the edge engine's `/api/signal` alignment (`api/app.api_signal`, read by `edge-engine/src/edge/shadow/runner.py`'s shadow runner) — a 15-minute freshness moves that signal too; nothing under `workers/`, `analytics/` or `ingestion/` reads it, so nothing on the money path does (the fold's LOW-2: it was written "the UI only") — so the UI's book, and `/api/signal`'s positioning, is now at most 15 minutes old (was 5) and the burst ~0.37 rps (was ~1.1, ~18 % of the ceiling); the environment may set it lower for a fresher UI, never under `positions_sync.POSITIONS_SYNC_MIN_S` = 60 (`sync_interval_s()`, applied where the gate reads it; `.env.example` moved with the default). The two out-of-budget bursts go BEHIND the throttle as NORMAL callers so the 6.0 ceiling means what it says: `_fetch_positions` takes one slot per page, the retry's included (at 6 rps a full 48-page walk is ≥ 8 s of wait per held whale per cycle, more under contention; nothing in `whale_exits` times out on it — the client's 25 s timeout is per request and the wait precedes the request, `_cycle` has no deadline of its own and `main` sleeps `INTERVAL_S` after it); `_confirm_gone` is pinned byte for byte (its SHA-256 in test_e7_review_pins:396-408, its params line in test_whale_exits_market_positions:266-273, its own docstring's promise), so its wait sits at its two call sites — `whale_exits._cycle` inside the held check, one normal slot per confirm read, and `mirror_live._confirm_gone`, which since the fold (MEDIUM-2) takes the PRIORITY lane with its wait bounded by `CONFIRM_GONE_WAIT_S` = 5.0 s (a constant beside `_SNAP_READ_TIMEOUT_S`; the environment may only lower it, floor 1.0; a wait past it is read exactly as an unreadable confirm is: NOT gone, HOLD) — it is the mirror's own money read on its exit path, `select_flatten`'s "is his position really gone before we flatten ours", awaited inside the six-wide walk under the book lock, and on the normal lane with no bound it queued behind the whole telemetry FIFO plus up to `PRIORITY_BURST` of its own sibling reads, ~4 s holding a walk slot on the very tick that decides an exit — and the code and those pins won over the brief's "inside" (said in hard2/E10_notes.md). The mirror's reads are protected by part 1, so the exit worker's walk waits behind them and never the other way round.

**Part 4, wall time.** E6's `books_venue` / `books_data` and E7's `books_data_wait` / `books_data_req` stay exactly as they are (keys and the "summed per call" reading pinned). A sibling block `short.wall` = `{books_data_wall, books_venue_wall, books_plan_wall, fast_wait, fast_work}` rides beside them (`_WallClock`, `_wall_block`; one decimal, bounded): the wall-clock seconds the books stage spent with at least one per-market read (`_market_snap`) / paced venue call (a quote read in `_bbo`, a paced write in `_paced`) / planner step in flight — a planner step being a book inside `_tick_book` that is inside neither a venue call nor a data read (`books − venue − data > 0`: its own database reads and writes and the plan arithmetic) — three in-flight counters with timestamps, process-wide and thread-safe (the writes enter from worker threads), read as a delta around `_walk_books` inside the `books` timer so each is ≤ `books` ≤ `tick_s` (a fast tick's around its markets, ≤ its `tick_s`); and E9's `fast` seconds split into `fast_wait`, the fast ticks' wait for `_TICK_LOCK`, and `fast_work`, the work after the acquire (`fast_wait + fast_work` = `fast`; 43.2 s of `fast` for one fast tick at 00:50Z was the wait), a full tick's block carrying the fast ticks since the last publish and reset on it as `short.fast` is, a fast tick's own stats its own split. The mode line prints ` fast=N/W.Ws` (count / work seconds) in E9's ` fast=N` token's place, and ` fast=N` still on a dict that carries no wall block (E9's line pin). **The fold (2026-09-08; the review's MEDIUM-1, MEDIUM-2, LOW-1, LOW-2).** MEDIUM-1, THE PUMP CANNOT DIE QUIETLY: `_serve`'s loop runs under `try/finally`, and a pump that ends by cancellation or by an exception with waiters still queued fails every one of them, in both lanes, with `ratelimit.ThrottlePumpStopped` within the loop step that ends the pump — no new arrival needed — while a pump that died of an exception logs it once, at the time (`log.exception`); a pump that ends because no one waits fails nothing and logs nothing. A failed waiter's `acquire` raises and the caller's own fail-closed path names it: `market_positions` returns None (the mirror counts `snap_market_unreadable`, as for any raising read), the mirror's `_confirm_gone` reads it as not gone, `polite_get` and the exit worker's walk raise as any request error would. The next `acquire` from anyone restarts the pump — a done pump is replaced, its exception retrieved so asyncio does not report the same death a second time at GC — and is served at the rate: the review's harness (n1 granted, the pump cancelled) now reads n2 served, n3 failed by name within one step, then `p` and `n4` one interval apart. LOW-1: each loop's pump serves its own loop's waiters only (a future is resolved safely from its own loop alone), so a LIVE waiter of another loop is skipped without being popped — it stays in its lane for that loop's pump — and only a done waiter, or one whose loop is closed, is dropped; the review's two-thread harness (20 acquires per loop at 50 rps, 1 s timeouts) reads 20/20 on both loops (was 18/20 and 17/20). MEDIUM-2: the mirror's own `_confirm_gone` on the priority lane with `CONFIRM_GONE_WAIT_S` (part 3 above); `whale_exits._cycle`'s site stays normal. LOW-2: the readers of `api_positions` named (part 2 above, `config.py`'s docstring). The arithmetic at the end of part 4 corrected to the review's number. Pinned in `backend/tests/test_e10_review_pins.py` — the review's strict xfail un-marked and passing as written, its two pins of the strand and of the silence now pinning the release by name and the one log record, the lane pin of the mirror's confirm flipped to the priority lane — plus the fold's own: a waiter queued when the pump is cancelled gets `ThrottlePumpStopped` within one loop step and the next acquire from anyone restarts the pump and is served at the rate, the pump that then ends because no one waits failing and logging nothing; a live foreign-loop waiter skipped and never popped, a closed loop's dropped; a wait past `CONFIRM_GONE_WAIT_S` not gone and no read made, the bound 5.0 with the env only lowering it to a floor of 1.0; `priority=True` at exactly three sites by AST over the package — `_market_snap`'s read, the mirror's `_confirm_gone` and `market_positions`' own branch.

## 32. E11 (2026-09-08) — the venue gate: fewer quiet quote reads, the live mirror's claim first

Owner 22:4xZ ("latency must be flawless and exceptional"). With E10 live (ee03310; the 03:22:23Z heartbeat) the tick was 41.8 s at 87 live books: `short.timing.books` 25.7 s of wall, `short.wall` = {books_venue_wall 22.6, books_data_wall 7.7, books_plan_wall 3.1}, `venue_calls` 47, `snap_market_reads` 24. The venue-pacing map (this session) placed the time: `venue_pace.pace()` is ONE global serial gap of `MIN_GAP_S` = 0.35 s held for the whole process, so 47 claims are 16.4 s of floor whatever the walk's width, and the other ~6 s of the 22.6 were the shadow's and price_path's claims (mirror_shadow's four sites, price_path's one) interleaving on the same gate — the live mirror's quote read waiting its turn behind a measurement read. Of the 47 claims ~29 were E6's quiet rotation's due reads (87 books / `QUIET_EVERY_TICKS` 3): a quiet book — on target, nothing open, no exit plan standing, no fill of his inside `HOT_S` = 600 s, not woken, read at least once, live — places NOTHING on its read; its quote feeds the plan row's display fields (`mark`, `bid`, `ask`) and `t.marks` (whose one money reader, the game's exposure, takes the mark only when a book's `avg_cost` is unreadable), so no read a plan could act on is removed by reading it less often — though the skip is the WHOLE read, the per-market data-API read of his position included (the review's design note): the safety net for a fill of his that every ingest path missed widens from ~1.5 to ~4.5 minutes, the witnesses being the fills table (chain, poll, s1) and the wake, and `MIRROR_QUIET_EVERY_TICKS=3` restores the old net — and every event that makes it matter — his fill, a wake, an order, an exit plan, a state change, another hand on the row — makes it HOT the same tick (`_hot_by_row`, `_quiet_skip`: the E6 rule as built, its functions byte for byte since — a SHA-256 pin). **Part 1, the rotation widened: `QUIET_EVERY_TICKS` 3 → 9**, read through `rules.capped_env("MIRROR_QUIET_EVERY_TICKS", 9, floor=1)` so the environment may only LOWER it (more reads, more venue load; `=3` is E6's rotation, `=1` every tick) and never raise it past 9 — fewer reads is less venue load, but a staler display mark is a code change that wants a review; why nine: at 87 books and a ~30 s tick a quiet book is re-read inside ~5 minutes, and the deferred queue's floor keeps the worst wait past the due tick at ceil(N_quiet / max(quiet_budget, `DEFERRED_MIN_PER_TICK`)) ticks as before (87 books at the default budget: tick 10 reads the 48 the quiet share fits, tick 11 the 39 queued — 11 ticks, and the quiet fleet costs two venue calls a tick between due ticks). The deferred FIFO floor (10), `CAND_MIN_PER_TICK`, `HOT_S`, every always-read class, the take arm, the sizing's mark, the `no_mark` / `no_quote` memo and the halt read are untouched. What the E6 rotation pins fix is the RULE — hot every tick, quiet skipped `book_quiet_skipped` with `read_on` = last read + `QUIET_EVERY_TICKS`, the queue FIFO under the slots, the worst gap `QUIET_EVERY_TICKS` + ceil(N / slots) — with the numbers derived from 3; only those numbers moved (each marked "E11:"): the due tick 4 → 10 and the queue's turns 5/6/7 → 11/12/13 (test_e6_tick_budget: the single book's `read_on`, the 30-book queue whose third tick at 3 coincided with the due cohort's next turn, 7 = 4 + 3, and at 9 reads its two alone with ticks 14-18 reading nothing and the cohort back on 19), the loops sized to one rotation (test_e6_review_pins: 10 → 16, 18 → 24 with the worst gap 15 → 21, the due tick read off the constant, `1 + QUIET_EVERY_TICKS == 10`), and the E1 full-game pin's fourth-tick turn (test_mirror_live_worker: the rotation's clock moved to A's turn, the clock-based memo story unchanged).

**Part 2, the priority claim** (`venue_pace.py`): the gate has TWO LANES, the rule of E10's `ratelimit.Throttle` restated for a thread lock — a PRIORITY claim takes the NEXT gap ahead of every waiting normal claimant; normal claimants keep their order among themselves and so do priority claimants (two FIFO deques of claimant tokens); at most `PACE_PRIORITY_BURST` = 12 consecutive priority claims while a normal claimant waits, then one normal, the run resetting when a normal is served or none waits (twelve as `PRIORITY_BURST`: two waves of the six-wide walk, 4.2 s at the gap for a normal under the bound, which neither the shadow nor price_path times out on); the 429 circuit doubles the gap for both lanes as today (`penalize` unchanged, never the gate's lock). How the queue is ordered under a threading primitive: ONE `threading.Condition`; a claimant appends its token to its lane and waits until the gate is free (`_busy`), it is its lane's head and the turn rule (`_turn`) names its lane; it then takes the gate, sleeps its gap OUTSIDE the condition's lock — so a claim can queue behind the sleeper and the lane lengths are what the turn rule reads — records the claim (`_last`) and frees the gate with `notify_all`; a claimant interrupted in the queue leaves its lane, never charged. THE GAP DOES NOT MOVE: whoever claims next computes its wait from the last claim's record and sleeps it out holding the gate, so between ANY two requests through the gate, in any mix of lanes, at least the gap passes — `test_e11_venue_gate` proves it with eight real threads, eight claims each, lanes alternating, on a fake clock (`venue_pace.time` replaced; the claimants and the Condition real): every one of 64 claims lands exactly one gap after the previous, 64 sleeps of 0.35, the rate one request per gap whatever the mix; and 0.70 under the circuit. THE ONLY PRIORITY CLAIMANT IS THE LIVE MIRROR'S TICK — and here the code and the pins won over the brief's map, said in hard2/E11_notes.md: the brief put "the book walk, the candidate reads, the fast tick, the order writes" under `mirror_live._paced` and asked for `pace(gap, priority=True)` there; but `_paced` carries the writes and `_venue_read`'s reads only — the walk's QUOTE READS go `_bbo` → `asyncio.to_thread(ms._paced_bbo, …)` (pinned byte for byte by test_e9_review_pins:412, and test_e6_tick_budget:178-199 patches `ms._paced_bbo` as the mirror's read), which claims through the SHADOW's `pace` name (mirror_shadow.py:1100), as do step R's positions walk (`ms.account_positions_walk`, :1004) and the resolver's reads (`ms.map_market`, :585); a keyword at `_paced` would have left the bulk of the 47 claims on the normal lane, and test_e6_tick_budget:199 pins `_paced`'s literal `pace(ms.READ_PACING_S)` besides. So the lane travels with the TICK: `tick_once` and `fast_tick_once` run their body inside `venue_pace.priority_claims()`, a `contextvars` context that `asyncio.to_thread` copies into every worker thread the tick starts, so every venue claim the tick makes — its own (`_paced`, `_pm_held`'s pages, the grammar reads, the create `pmus.submit_fok` claims for a BUY inside `_paced`'s thread) and those through the shadow's functions — is the mirror's priority claim, while the shadow's and price_path's loops (their own tasks, no context) and pmus's default stay normal; `pace(gap, priority_claim=True)` is the same lane by the call (an explicit argument, default False, no site in the package passes it — the E10 fold's AST census of `priority=True` is the data-API lane's and stays exactly three, which is why the gate's keyword is not spelled `priority`). The mirror's request is simply next; the venue's rate is not raised.

**Part 3, measured, and the pins.** `short.gate` = {`wait`, `claims`} rides beside `short.wall` (whose five keys the E10 pins fix exactly, test_e10_priority_lane:534, so a sibling block as the brief foresaw): the seconds this tick's venue claims spent on the gate — from the call to the claim, the queue behind other claimants and the gap itself, SUMMED PER CLAIM like `books_data_wait` (under the six-wide walk it exceeds the wall time it cost) — and their count, one decimal, the priority lane's delta since the tick's start (`venue_pace.lane_stats`, `_Tick.gate0`, `_gate_block`; a fast tick's block its own claims); served whole, after the wall block and before the fills collapse (the E7 tail pin holds). Pinned in `backend/tests/test_e11_venue_gate.py` (22 tests): the constants, the signature and the gap unchanged (`ms.READ_PACING_S` = `price_path.READ_PACING_S` = 0.35); the gap between any two requests under mixed load with real threads; the circuit doubling both lanes and lifting; a priority claim served before eight waiting normals (its measured wait two gaps, the eighth normal's ten); a priority arrival during the holder's sleep taking the freed gap ahead of a queued normal; FIFO in both lanes; the burst bound (fifteen priorities behind one normal: twelve, the normal, three; a run at the bound handing the next gap to the normal; no bound with no normal waiting); a claimant interrupted in the queue leaving its lane; the return value and an idle gate handing the slot out at once; the context naming the lane and reaching `asyncio.to_thread`, reset on exit; the only claimant by AST (`priority_claims()` at exactly `tick_once` and `fast_tick_once`; the keyword at no site; the shadow's four, price_path's one and pmus's claims bare; the E6 literal, the E9 read, the E10 census); the real tick on the real gate at a fake clock — step R, step O, the quote read and a placement all on the priority lane, none normal, `short.gate` counting them, the same shadow function outside the tick a normal claim; the fast tick's own block and the full tick's delta; the block's keys served whole beside the wall block's; the wait a sum per claim with the queue in it; `QUIET_EVERY_TICKS` 9, the env lowering to 1 and never raising, the paragraph; a quiet book read on tick 10 and a hot book every tick, a fill of his making it hot at once; the floor of one; the 87-book shape; the E6 rule byte for byte and its hot-path pins re-run; the E6 / E7 / E9 / E10 / E2 pins re-run; this section. NOT in scope, untouched: `MIN_GAP_S`, `MIRROR_BOOK_CONCURRENCY`, the WebSocket feed, `HOT_S`, the take arm, any read's decision.
