## 49. T2, the per-fill record (2026-09-08, FILL lane 4)

THE RULE. Every fill of his a tick holds on a market with a book is written ONCE to `mirror_fill_answers` (migration 060) with the name the tick gave it and the order it placed — the same entry `_fills_seen` appends to the plan's `his_fills_seen` — and is never lost to the list's 20-entry bound (`HIS_FILLS_SEEN_MAX`, E9's contract: the tick's working memory, unchanged) or to the book's close. The rows this lane is built on: the fills-missed preset at 17:37Z (hard2/hourly_1737.txt row 1659) read ALL 3,234 fills of his on our markets in 24 h, $1,365,379.81, of which `unseen` — no plan held the fill — was 1,105 fills / $517,203.44 (row 1660), and $397,223.87 of that (76.8%, FILL_plan.md line 98 and section 3 item 5 at line 147, R4's attribution) fell on markets with a LIVE or closing book: the tick HAD named those fills, and the name was gone twenty fills later on a busy book — book 611 cs2-g2-ast10, 301 chain rows on a live book (hard2/post_fvv_1707.txt row 334), 118 `unseen` fills / $30,288.20 (hourly_1737 row 1687) — and gone for good at the close (book 285 lal-elc-rso total-2, our exit placed at 20:14:35 against his fill at 20:14:12, the book closed: hard2/post_exits_1707.txt row 334; Oz/Denchev 544, 8 `unseen` / $16,110.20 at hourly_1737 row 1691 beside our 2,709 sh @0.520 bought 13:35:51-13:40:14, hard2/verify_1750.txt row 564). The fills-missed census now keys on the table FIRST, then the plan's list, then the 120 s order window. The table is chosen over a larger list (FILL_plan.md line 92: ~400 entries × ~130 bytes on every plan write of 60-90 books × 2,880 ticks a day of jsonb churn, against about 6,000 rows a day — his fills on mirrored markets 4,905 + 1,207 = 6,112 in 24 h, hard2/hisday_1750.txt rows 551-552). NO order path reads the table; nothing here sizes, places or cancels. Expected dollars: $0 — it re-labels.

THE CODE. `backend/migrations/060_mirror_fill_answers.sql` (059's header style; additive, re-runnable, no DEFAULT on any column — the serial id's nextval is the plan's own BIGSERIAL): `mirror_fill_answers (id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY, whale TEXT NOT NULL, condition_id TEXT NOT NULL, fill_id TEXT NOT NULL, fill_ts DOUBLE PRECISION, detected_at DOUBLE PRECISION, at DOUBLE PRECISION NOT NULL, book_id BIGINT, order_id BIGINT, name TEXT NOT NULL, tick BIGINT, UNIQUE (whale, fill_id))` and the index `mirror_fill_answers_condition_ts (condition_id, fill_ts)`; the API's start.sh applies the sorted glob on boot, the workers never run migrations. `mirror_live._fills_seen` builds the list exactly as before and, when `t.fill_answers is True`, ALSO queues one row (`t.fill_rows`, `_fill_answer_row`: whale, condition_id, fill_id, fill_ts = the entry's `ts`, detected_at = its `det`, at = the tick's clock when the entry was first named, book_id, order_id = the entry's `order`, name, tick = `t.seq`) for every entry of the list whose ingest clock (`det`, else `ts` — `_seen_clock`'s order) is past the book's high-water mark `_fills_hwm_of(book, prior)` = the later of the prior plan's `fills_hwm` and the flush memo `_fill_hwm[book id]`; absent, every entry the list holds, once; and for every entry this tick APPENDED that the bound keeps, whatever its clock (a row that commits late with an earlier `detected_at` is the list's fill, so it is the record's; an entry under the hwm the bound drops is a fill the lookback re-appends every tick — already written, never queued again). The queue reads the UNBOUNDED list, so a burst of forty fills on one tick is forty rows and a list of twenty. `_carry_fills_hwm(book, plan)` writes `plan["fills_hwm"]` beside `his_fills_seen` in `_write_plan` and on the quiet skip's own write (`_SKIP_CARRIED` untouched) — the hwm as the LAST SUCCESSFUL FLUSH left it, never this tick's rows. `_fill_answers_guard(t, stats)` probes the table once per tick (`_SQL_FILL_ANSWERS_GUARD`, `SELECT fill_id FROM mirror_fill_answers LIMIT 0`) after the 059 probe on both tick paths (`_tick`, `_fast_tick`). `_flush_fill_answers(t)` beside `_flush_candidate_refusals`, called where it is called (the full tick's tail before `_instruments`, the fast tick's tail): the rows a failed flush kept (`_fill_pending`, keyed (whale, fill_id)) then this tick's, ONE `INSERT ... SELECT FROM json_populate_recordset(NULL::mirror_fill_answers, $1::json) ... ON CONFLICT (whale, fill_id) DO NOTHING` (`_SQL_FILL_ANSWERS`) under `asyncio.wait_for(..., CAND_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT_S)` (5.0 s, the candidate flush's own bound: the pool carries no command_timeout and the write runs under `_TICK_LOCK`); success advances `_fill_hwm[book]` to the newest clock written; failure keeps every row for the next tick. `.github/workflows/render-ops.yml`: `fills-missed` gains a first CTE `fa` (fill_id, name, order_id, book_id from the table, whale rn1, `fill_ts` within 25 h) joined before the `his_fills_seen` LATERAL; `s` carries `aorder = COALESCE(fa.order_id, the plan's readable order id)`, `o` takes `o.id = s.aorder` first and the 120 s window only when neither the record nor the plan names an order, the class reads `'refused:' || COALESCE(o.fa_name, o.e->>'name', 'unnamed')` and `unseen` only when neither holds the fill, the per-book key `COALESCE(o.obook, o.fa_book, o.seen_book)`; lane 0's `ostate` / `state` naming and its (class, state) and (class, decision) statements are unchanged (section 45). The `fa` CTE reads the table only through `xmltable` over `query_to_xml` inside a `CASE WHEN to_regclass('mirror_fill_answers') IS NULL` — a plain FROM cannot be made conditional, and the hourly bundle runs its nine presets in ONE `psql -c` under ON_ERROR_STOP, so an absent table would have lost take-band and on-target-why for every hour between a deploy and the boot that applies 060; the choice is pinned and the standalone preset is the one that fails by name. A new standalone preset `fill-answers` (service sportsassets-db; the case label after `closed-while-he-traded`, `hourly` last, the help arm regenerated): per market with a book — the newest book (id, slug, state), his fills held in 24 h (chain/s1 collapsed as fills-missed reads them), the rows written, how many carry an order, the names (name=n), the oldest fill written vs the oldest the plan's list still keeps and the plan's `fills_hwm`; then the table's totals. The hourly stays NINE presets; the fills-missed copy inside it is the guarded one.

THE NAMES. Census `fill_answer_write_failed` (the tick's one INSERT failed or timed out; the rows kept, the hwm not advanced) and `fill_answers_absent` (the table not there this tick, or its probe failed: nothing queued), inserted as one block before E19's `drift_smaller_open` (keys[−13]) and `registered_no_increase` (keys[−12]) — keys[−15] and keys[−14], after E14's `take_in_band` (now keys[−16]); the tail pins that count from the end move by two (−95 → −97, −96 → −98; E17's eight −24:−16 → −26:−18; E20's −16 → −18, E14b's −15 → −17, E14's −14 → −16). Plan `fills_hwm` (the ingest clock of the newest fill the last successful flush wrote for the book), written beside `his_fills_seen` on every plan write once a flush has succeeded, and carried by the quiet skip. Decision: this lane WRITES none — `rules.order_decision` is E14's and migration 059's list stands as written (`'rest'`, `'take'`, `'take_in_band'`, `'cover'`, `'exit_rest'`, the replace causes after a cancel); the preset READS the words into its (class, decision) block as before.

THE RAILS. None new; no knob; no environment read (`MIRROR_FILL*` appears nowhere; `rules` is untouched). The bounds, constants in code: one INSERT per tick of at most `FILL_ANSWERS_FLUSH_MAX` = 5,000 rows (the rest wait in the memo), the memo at most `_FILL_PENDING_MAX` = 20,000 rows (past it the OLDEST are dropped — the plan's list still names them; about three days of his fills on mirrored markets at 6,112 a day), the hwm memo at most `_FILL_HWM_MEMO_MAX` = 4,000 books (a book dropped re-reads its hwm off its own plan), the write under `CAND_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT_S` 5.0 s. `HIS_FILLS_SEEN_MAX` 20 unchanged.

FAIL CLOSED. The table absent (060 not applied) → `fill_answers_absent` counted, `UndefinedTableError` on the heartbeat, logged once per process; nothing queued; the plan's list and the order window carry the census as today; no hwm written; the tick goes on and places as it would. The probe failing for any OTHER reason → the same name with the error's name on the heartbeat, nothing queued this tick, the tick goes on — the 059 probe's refusal branch is NOT copied, because that column decides which INSERT sends an order and this table is on no order path: refusing trades on a measurement probe's blip would be the reversal path; the hwm stands, so the next tick that reads the table present queues what this one held (chosen; named here). The INSERT failing or timing out → `fill_answer_write_failed`, logged once per process, the rows kept in the memo (bounded), the hwm NOT advanced — fail closed toward RE-WRITING, never toward losing a name; `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` makes the re-write idempotent and keeps the FIRST name (the list's own rule: written once, never renamed). A restart between a flush and the next plan → at most one tick's fills re-queued off the plan's older hwm, refused by the conflict clause. `fills_hwm` unreadable on the prior plan and no memo → every entry the list holds queued once. A fill with no readable id (`_fill_key` None) → skipped on both the list and the table, as today. A fill that lands late with a clock under the hwm (a `det`-less row, or a row the ingest stamped before a later row committed: `ingestion/pipeline.py` stamps `detected_at` at line 128 and starts the INSERT at line 132 — the column list names the stamp at 136 — so a slow batch commits after a faster row with a later stamp) → written when the list keeps it, named `book_quiet_skipped` / the tick's reason (the record names what the list names; the reviewer's re-pin of the builder's hwm-only rule, which dropped it); one older than the list's twenty newest by stamp rolls off the list the tick it lands and off the record with it. The `fa` CTE against an absent table → reads nothing, the preset and the hourly run (the class falls back to the list and the window); the `fill-answers` preset against an absent table → fails by name (the runner's non-zero exit, as verify-day's did at hard2/verify_1750.txt row 599) — it is the measurement, never the order path. The fast tick on a book with an order open → `order_open`, no plan, nothing queued (book 661's shape, hard2/closerows_1750.txt row 529: the replace-cancelled cover rest 609 @0.620 filled 89.24); the full tick after it names the fill once.

THE TESTS (`backend/tests/test_fill_t2_record.py`, 18; `backend/tests/test_render_ops_fill_answers.py`, 8). The migration: sorts last after 059, one CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS with the eleven columns, their types and nullability and the UNIQUE (whale, fill_id), one CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS on (condition_id, fill_ts), no DEFAULT, parsed by pglast beside the worker's two statements; on a scratch database (the E12 idiom; skipped without a server, run here on a local PostgreSQL 16) applied twice, the guard's UndefinedTableError before it, the INSERT of two rows then the same keys with other names refused (`INSERT 0 0`, the first names kept), another whale's same fill id a row. Book 611's shape: 40 fills on one tick → one INSERT of 40, the list the newest 20, every row's fields, the hwm the newest ingest clock (the first plan without it, the next with it); 3 new fills → 3 rows named `rest_placed` with the order; nothing new → nothing written; a restart → nothing already written re-queued; a restart between a flush and the next plan → one row re-written, refused, the first kept. The write failing → counted every tick, logged once, the list unchanged, no hwm, the rows kept and written once by the first tick that can; a hung write → cut at `CAND_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT_S`, counted; the batch bound (3 → 3 then 2) and the memo bound (5 → the oldest 2 dropped). Book 661's shape: the fast tick on a book with an order open writes nothing, the full tick names the fill once; a woken bare book writes its own row from the fast tick's flush. The table absent → named, `UndefinedTableError` on the heartbeat, nothing queued, the tick places, no hwm; the same on the fast tick; the table appearing → the list's entry written once with its FIRST name; a probe blip → named, never refused. The quiet skip carries the hwm and writes a late fill by its ingest clock; the clockless late row the list keeps is written too and moves no hwm. The census place (keys[−15], keys[−14], `_new_stats`), the one emit site each, `HIS_FILLS_SEEN_MAX` 20, the bounds, no knob, the table named by the two statements alone and read by no order path (`_act`, `_place_reserved`, `_entry_take`, `_exit_take`, `_tick_candidate`, `_fast_gate`, `_maybe_close_episode`, `_flatten_send` name none of it), the queue inside `_fills_seen` before the bound, the two `_carry_fills_hwm` sites, the two flush sites, the decision words untouched, this section. THE RULE. (a) THE TURN. A sign-flip close is a TURN: on a `cashed_out` / `cancelled` verdict with `plan.sign_flip` True, `_maybe_close_episode` writes `plan["turn"] = {from: the book's intent, to: the opposite intent, his_net: plan.net, at: t.now}` BEFORE `_SQL_BOOK_STATE`; the read path's `_write_plan` runs after the close (the `finally` of `_tick_book`), so the closed row's last plan carries it -- book 309's own shape, last_reason `on target` beside the plan's close (post_booksnew_1707 385), the shape lane 0b's `closed-while-he-traded` preset already reads (`b.last_plan->'turn'`, section 45 item 5). (b) THE REOPEN REFUSED. A candidate refused on a condition whose newest CLOSED book's plan carries `turn` writes `reopen_refused = {name, at, his_net, ask, his_px, band}` on THAT closed book's plan beside migration 054's refusal row, and counts `reopen_refused` -- once per refused candidate per tick (`_note_reopen_refused`, called from `_walk_candidate` after `_note_candidate_refusal`; the fast path's `_fast_candidate` walks through the same call, so his fill waking a turned market records the same way). Only a refusal WITH a verdict on the market -- the candidate's quote read made (`mark` on the walk's context: `drift`, `side_band`, `snapshot_stale`, `venue_already_holds`, the game cap, the short gates, `target_zero`, `no_mark`) -- is a reopen refused; a refusal before the read (`unmapped`, `event_stale`, the map and read caps, `cand_game_full_skipped`) writes nothing, and a memo skip (`cand_terminal_skipped`, `cand_no_mark_skipped`) is not a refusal. ONE read per pair per tick, cached on the walk's context (`d["reopen_of"]`): `_SQL_BOOK_TURN` (`SELECT id, last_plan ... WHERE whale, condition_id, state = 'closed' ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1` -- the same row `_SQL_BOOK_FLIP` reads for E15's witness, pinned clause for clause), bounded by `CAND_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT_S` (5.0 s) like the refusal flush, and bounded in count by the candidate reads (MAX_MARKETS_PER_TICK 40). The write, `_SQL_BOOK_REOPEN_REFUSED` = `UPDATE mirror_books SET last_plan = COALESCE(last_plan, '{}'::jsonb) || $2::jsonb WHERE id = $1 AND state = 'closed'`, MERGES the entry (the newest refusal overwrites; the 054 table keeps the history), touches only a row still 'closed', and names NO `updated_at`: the fills-missed census picks the plan of the newest-UPDATED book on the condition (section 45 item 4), so a bump would move which closed book's `his_fills_seen` it reads -- pinned on the fake and by the statement's text. NO REFUSAL IS WIDENED: `rules.admission`, `rules.select_flatten`, `_flip_since`, `_open_flow`, `_fast_gate`, `_venue_market_ended`, `_memo_terminal_book` and `_close_settled` are hashed against the tip this lane was built on (84317ae) in the tests; the refusal is named first and the record is written after it; nothing here admits. (c) THE FLAT-CLOCK GUARD. `rules.episode_close_reason(..., he_holds: bool | None = None)`: when `due` came ONLY from the flat clock -- none of `market_closed_or_resolved`, `vanished_confirmed`, `sign_flipped` True -- and `he_holds` is True, the verdict is `he_holds`; anything but the bool False on that path (None, the default; junk) is `he_holds_unread`; both are not-a-close verdicts like `not_due` (the docstring's table gains them); `episode_close()` inherits None and so closes nothing on the clock-alone path. Only `he_holds` False -- his sizes READ as nothing on the book's own side -- lets the clock close as before. `rules.he_holds_on_axis(his_long, his_other, short)`: a long book reads `his_long > 0`, a short book `his_other > 0`, by `select_flatten`'s `_size` reading (a finite number at or above zero); None when EITHER size is not such a number. `_tick_book`'s read path hands `he_holds=rules.he_holds_on_axis(r.his_long, r.his_other, short)`; the quiet skip's call and the closing branch's call hand nothing (None). THE FLIP CLOSE IS NEVER GUARDED (FILL_plan section 0 item 3, section 5): `sign_flipped` True, `market_closed_or_resolved` True and `vanished_confirmed` True close exactly as before whatever `he_holds` reads -- pinned at the rule for each of the three, with `he_holds` True and None, and through the worker for the flip (book 467's shape, 30 s) and the market's end.

THE NAMES. Census `he_holds` (a book flat at target 0 past MIRROR_FLAT_CLOSE_S held open because his fills show him holding on its side), `he_holds_unread` (held because the sizes could not be read this tick -- the quiet skip, the closing branch with an unreadable markets row), `reopen_refused` (a candidate refusal recorded on the turned market's closed book) -- ONE block, inserted before E19's `drift_smaller_open` (keys[-13]) and `registered_no_increase` (keys[-12]), after E14's `take_in_band`: keys[-16:-13]; the tail pins that count from the end move by three (test_mirror_live_worker -92: -> -95: and -93 -> -96, test_e9_review_pins -92:-88 -> -95:-91 and -93 -> -96, test_e17_standing_reanchor -24:-16 -> -27:-19, test_e20_wrong_sign_hold -16 / -15 / -14 -> -19 / -18 / -17, test_e14b_exit_take_rests -15 / -14 -> -18 / -17, test_e14_take_band -14 -> -17 and the successor of `take_in_band` -> `he_holds`, test_e19_smaller_reading's predecessor chain from `drift_smaller_open` now `reopen_refused`, `he_holds_unread`, `he_holds`, `take_in_band`, `exit_take_rested`, `wrong_sign_hold`, `adopt_prior_venue_settled`). `reopen_refused_write_failed` is counted on the census dict (not a CENSUS_KEYS name, like `refusal_write_failed`) and logged once per process. Plan: `close` carries the two new verdicts; `turn` {from, to, his_net, at} on the flip close's plan; `reopen_refused` {name, at, his_net, ask, his_px, band} on the CLOSED book's plan. THE RAILS. None added; no knob, no migration, no numeric constant. MIRROR_FLAT_CLOSE_S 3,600 s (`capped_env`; callers may only LENGTHEN it through `max(flat_close_s, MIRROR_FLAT_CLOSE_S)`, the line pinned) unchanged. `he_holds` is a reading of his fills' sizes, fail-closed toward NOT ending the book: a flat book at target 0 buys nothing, so holding it open is the closed direction. The reopen record's two statements are each bounded by CAND_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT_S (5.0 s, E-W2's bound); the read runs once per refused READ candidate per tick and never on an abandoned tick.

FAIL CLOSED. His sizes unreadable on the axis (`_size` None on either token) -> `he_holds_unread`, not closed on the clock; the market's end, the confirmed vanish and the flip still close it. On the read path the sizes always read as numbers (`net_positions` floors at 0 and rounds to 6 dp, and `his_fills` raises on a failed read rather than handing a stand-in), so the unread arm on that path is a dead one in practice: in the live census the word is the quiet skip's and the closing branch's. The quiet skip (no reading) -> None -> `he_holds_unread` on the flat-clock path only -- so a QUIET flat book's clock close now lands on its next READ tick (E6's rotation: at most QUIET_EVERY_TICKS = 9 ticks later), where before it landed on the skip; a book with a fill of his inside HOT_S is read every tick and closes at 3,600 s exactly (both pinned). A book already 'closing' whose markets row cannot be read this tick (`market_live` None, the clock alone) -> `he_holds_unread`, held 'closing' until the row reads again (before: closed on the clock with no reading at all -- the plan is silent on this path; the named hold is the fail-closed choice). `_SQL_BOOK_TURN` unreadable, hung, a closed book with no `turn`, a plan that is not a dict -> no `reopen_of`, the 054 row as today, nothing counted. The plan UPDATE failing -> `reopen_refused_write_failed`, logged once, the row stands. A refusal before the market read -> no read, no entry. An abandoned tick -> nothing more. The turned market's reopen refused by any name -> as today, by that name: the lane names and counts, never admits. The turn itself rides the close tick's plan write; if that write fails the row carries the prior tick's plan (`sign_flip` True, no `turn`) and the reopen's refusals go to the 054 table alone.

The third frozen-for-life shape of the day (task 65's class; the two earlier of the day are 266 / 347, FILL_plan_C section 0.2).

THE RULE. On a book that was FROZEN when its tick began (`was_frozen`; E5 review F1: never on the transition tick) under `frozen_reason` `placement_lost`, with exactly ONE row in state `lost` and `order_id` NULL marked lost BEFORE this tick's clock (`_SQL_LOST_ROWS` / `_SQL_LOST_ROWS_047`: the open-orders read's own projection with `WHERE o.book_id = $1 AND o.state = 'lost' AND o.order_id IS NULL AND o.done_at < to_timestamp($2)`, tag `ml-lost-rows`; the 050 and 047 shapes derived from `_SQL_ORDERS_OPEN` / `_SQL_ORDERS_OPEN_047` so the projections cannot drift), on a tick whose venue read is FRESH (`t.walk_at` not None, the guard `_thaw_verdict` uses), when the venue's position less the ledger less the registered shares equals the lost row's quantity ON ITS SIDE within `FLAT_TOL_SHARES` (`_lost_fill_delta`: a SELL row -- a long book's reduce, a short book's add -- leaves the ledger DOWN by its quantity, a BUY row up; the desk's `manual` shares are not subtracted, so a manual position on the slug lands on `lost_fill_unexplained`, E5's register being the road for it): the tick re-reads the venue's trade log for that market BY ORDER (`_trade_log_fills` with its default `by_order` True: exact quantity, exact wire, our side, unknown to every ledger and protected id; `since` = placed - 30 s as `_reconcile_placing` hands it) over the window [placed - `_ORPHAN_SKEW_S`, `t.now`] -- the widened window is this lane's ONE departure from round eight's rule, taken ONLY when the position delta already proves a fill of exactly that size on that side, so the shared-account concern is answered by the delta, not assumed; the 'placing' road (`_reconcile_placing`, `_reconcile_lost_close`) keeps the 20-minute window byte for byte. When the log names exactly ONE order id whose fills sum to the lost quantity (within `FLAT_TOL_SHARES`): the row is adopted exactly as `_reconcile_placing`'s trade-log branch adopts one -- `_SQL_ORDER_ADOPT` with `_adopt_reason(o, "adopted from the trade log after the window")`, the fill-weighted `avg_px`, `_book_delta(maker=True)`, `_finish_order(..., "booked from the trade log after the window")` (the row `filled`, `filled_rest` counted as for any adopted rest) -- census `lost_fill_adopted`, `_recent(book, "lost_fill_adopted", order_row, order, shares, px)`, one warning line. The book STAYS FROZEN this tick (the frozen exit that follows holds `frozen_fill_this_tick`, E5 review F1 / F2: the walk is stale by the fill) and thaws on the NEXT tick's own venue == ledger rule (`_thaw_verdict` None for placement_lost -> `_thaw`): the lane calls no thaw. Every other reading names a hold and books nothing. Bounds: at most ONE trade-log read per frozen book per tick, only while the delta matches; the read repeats no more often than `rules.MIRROR_LOST_FILL_REREAD_S` (300 s) per book, read off the later of the plan's `lost_fill_at` (durable, the memo the brief names) and the per-process `_lost_fill_read_at` (bounded at `_LOST_FILL_MEMO_MAX` 4,000 books, the oldest half dropped past it; it covers a quiet skip, whose plan carries `_SKIP_CARRIED` alone); the read is a `_venue_read` and counts against the venue budget as every one does. ## 52. The reads batch C is judged by (2026-09-09, FILL lane 8)

THE ROWS. The largest class of the 22:25Z fills-missed table has no cause row: `missed_replace` 745 fills / $381,750.75 / 686 resolved / roi -0.0012 / ci95 0.1153 (h2225 996), and the (class, decision) block prints filled / partial / missed_expired_ioc only (h2225 1061-1080), so the only reading of WHICH cause re-quoted the day's rests was the 77-row proxy on the filled and partial rows -- replace_cent 25 + 9 = 34 (1066 / 1075), replace_qty 9 + 24 = 33 (1068 / 1076), replace_side 5 (1079), ttl 4 (1077), replace_unread 1 (1069). At 02:22Z the class reads 733 / $324,249.20 / 676 / +0.1800 / 0.1174 / lag 19 s (h0222 508). take-band folds a replaced rest into `rest`, so bucket 1 read 82 rows / 45 filled / 39 replaced under ONE fill_pct (h2225 1209) and the rest row 278 / 111 / 140 (1212; 324 / 122 / 156 at 02:22Z, h0222 724, with `take_in_band` printing its first 11 rows / 8 filled, h0222 727). Of the four engines C2 named behind the replaces -- the clock (lane 7), the touch chasing under a standing his cent (lane 12), the 45 s floor, the resize -- no file counts one: the clock's shape is 4731 -> 4732 at 22:44Z (tick_2245 393-394: 6 @0.41 cancelled `replace` and re-placed 12.1 s later at the same 6 @0.41); the touch's is 823's 4672 -> 4674 (h2225 899 / 897: a BUY_LONG rest of 34 sh cancelled after 25 s, 4674 placed 42 s after it for 7 sh; h2225's order table carries neither wire nor his_level, so "0.47 re-placed at 0.46 under one his cent" is the plan's ASSUMED shape (FILL_plan_C lane 8, FILL_C_design_selection 89), read on no row, and the bid at the cancel is on no row either); the resize's is 816's 4692 -> 4699 (h2225 879 / 872: 1,403 with 631.85 filled, re-quoted at 752); 829's four rests of 92 sh for his one 22:13:57 fill (h2225 883 / 878 / 874 / 868: cancelled after 21 / 139 / 87 s, the fourth filled) are the clock's shape three times on the short side if the wire held -- the file carries the quantity, not the wire (the fixture's 0.30 throughout is chosen). On the exits: 20 of the 31 `he_reduced_we_held` books carry stuck dollars, $2,900.48 of the $3,663.95 (exitspaired_2304 594-597), and six of them -- 313 0.24 / 0.26 (533), 332 0.37 / 0.37 (536), 364 0.31 / 0.27, 525 0.34 / 0.34, 320 0.31 / 0.31, 699 0.38 / 0.37 (FILL_C5_exits.md 23) -- had our filled fraction within five points of his cut: 554.44 + 310.06 + 133.61 + 66.85 + 18.18 + 5.13 = $1,088.27 of proportional following printed as "we held"; 683 (0.35 against his 0.43, $331.99, 534) is eight points off and genuinely under-sold. And the per-IOC same-tick rest line lane 0b promised for lane 1 (section 46's live proof) is not in exitsband_2304: the file carries the band buckets and the books statement only, so lane 1's $246.20 bound has no read; today's long exit IOCs number 94 -- 63 whole, 70 - 63 = 7 partial, 94 - 70 = 24 zero (exitsband_2304 576).

THE RULE. Six read-only amendments in `.github/workflows/render-ops.yml`, nothing in the worker, nothing in the rules. (1) `fills-missed`, the (class, decision) statement: `WHERE class IN ('filled', 'partial', 'missed_expired_ioc')` gains `'missed_replace'` -- one word in TWO places, the standalone case and the hourly's copy (`grep -o | wc -l` reads 2; the old three-word clause 0) -- so the class prints `missed_replace x {replace_cent, replace_qty, replace_side, ttl, replace_unread}` with n, his_usd, n_resolved, roi, ci95, band_med_c, lag_med_s; the word is the row's own (`COALESCE(decision, 'unrecorded')`), never a list. (2) `take-band` and `exits-band`, the split: a replaced rest prints under its own word -- `rest_replaced` on take-band, `exit_rest_replaced` / `cover_replaced` on exits-band (both statements) -- so n, filled_n, fill_pct and roi read per side; the cause words still fold, to the replaced word instead of the placement word, and never print as a word of their own. `replaced_n` STAYS on the `rest` / `exit_rest` / `cover` row: it is now `sum(count(*) FILTER (WHERE replaced)) OVER (PARTITION BY regexp_replace(decision, '_replaced$', ''), bucket)`, so the rest row and its replaced sibling both read the bucket's replaced count and ALL reads the total, as before (the plan's "for the pin's continuity": exits-band's held-shape pin keeps replaced_n 2 on the exit_rest row). (3) `take-band`, a SECOND statement (the plan calls it the fourth; on 82ebe77 take-band carries one statement, so this is its second): the replaced entry rows of 24 h on BOTH intents (kind increase / take; BUY_LONG on a long book, SELL_LONG on a short -- the exits-paired reducing clause's mirror image, so a short book's add is in the count), each paired with the NEXT entry row of the SAME book through `LEAD(his_cent) / LEAD(wire_c) / LEAD(qty) / LEAD(placed_at) OVER (PARTITION BY book_id ORDER BY placed_at, id)` over ALL the book's entry rows (a band take that replaced a rest is a next row too), then counted over the replaced ones: `touch_moved` = the same his cent (floor-cent of his_level, numeric-rounded: lane M's HIGH-3) and a different wire; `same_quote` = the same cent, wire and qty (the clock's shape); `qty_regrow` = the same cent and wire, `LEAD(qty) > qty` with the growth under 10 % of the leaves (qty - filled); `other` = his cent moved, or a resize outside that (816's shape reads here); `unread` = no next row, or his_level / wire NULL on either side. Beside them `future_clock` = decision 'replace_unread' AND done_at - placed_at < 45 s AND filled = 0 -- a proxy until the row can say more (C2's `replace_unread` on 4731 is inferred from the clock, not read: the mirror-tick preset's `zz order` rows cut the row's JSON at 280 characters, `left((to_jsonb(o) - 'placed_at' - 'receipt')::text, 280)` -- 2,400 is the heartbeat arm's cut -- before `decision`, tick_2245 393), and it overlaps the pair words -- and the replaced rows' done_at - placed_at median. By side (long / short from b.intent) and hour, `GROUP BY ROLLUP (side, hour) ORDER BY 1, 2 DESC`: the side's ALL row leads each side, and the long side's `touch_moved` against its `replaced` is the share lane 12 is gated on. The wire is compared as `round(wire::numeric, 4)` on both sides (float8 equality is not the reading). (4) The word pins: every statement that groups by decision prints the row's own word -- take-band's first (`COALESCE(o.decision, 'unrecorded')`, the only `o.decision IN` its two cause folds), fills-missed's third, exits-band's first (its population is the 059 exit words plus the causes, as lane 0b wrote it) -- pinned by a word test on each, and on the scratch database `take_on_add` (lane 10) and `rest_held` (lane 12) print as their own rows on take-band the day a row carries them. (5) `exits-paired`: the arm `we_matched_his_cut` -- `hs.exit_ts IS NULL AND his_reduced_pct >= 0.25 AND abs(COALESCE(our_exit_filled, 0) / our_peak - his_reduced_pct) <= 0.05` (0.05 chosen, the plan's) -- BEFORE `he_reduced_we_held` in the CASE of both statements (a CASE reads in order); `stuck_after_his_exit_usd`'s four-word list is untouched, so a match is never stuck; the totals gain `matched_cut_n` after `reduced_we_held_stuck_usd`. The arm has no upper bound on our fraction (the plan's formula), so a book where he cut 0.60 and we sold 0.58 reads `we_matched_his_cut` where before it read `no_exit_by_him`; 683's eight points stay `he_reduced_we_held`. (6) `exits-band`, a THIRD statement, the per-IOC same-tick rest line: every exit IOC of 24 h on a LONG book (SELL_LONG, tif IOC, whatever word it carries -- a row with none still counts) by whole / partial / zero, and for each the exit_rest row on the SAME book placed within 15 s after it -- its decision `exit_rest`, or a cause word once a replace overwrote it (lane 0b's HIGH-1 lesson; a rest with no word is not counted): iocs, ioc_shares, ioc_filled_sh, rested_15s, rest_filled_any, rest_shares, rest_filled_sh, rest_lag_med_s, with ALL. Before lane 1 that count was 0 by construction. The hourly is regenerated as the nine presets' SQL joined (still nine; the help arm unchanged: no label added); take-band's second statement rides it, bounded at 2 x 24 + 3 = 51 rows, under the runner's 1,500-line cap (the headroom, from the files -- the cap is `head -"${HEAD:-300}"` over psql's own lines; the `== sportsassets-db` echo before them and the `psql exit` line after are the runner's, outside the pipe: h2225's hourly ran 1,267 psql lines (h2225 2-1268; take-band's section 1200-1221 is 22 of them) and hourly_2030's 1,216 (383-1598, before take-band existed; its on-target-why ends "(30 rows)", so it was not cut); this lane adds at most 51 + 4 (the pairs table's header, rule, count and blank) + 6 (rest_replaced x the five buckets unread / <=0 / 1 / 2 / 3+ and ALL) + 6 (missed_replace x the five cause words and unrecorded) = 67 lines, so the worst read on file is h2225's 1,267 + 67 = 1,334, 166 lines under the cap, which cuts on-target-why, the last section, first); exits-band and exits-paired stay standalone (service sportsassets-db).

THE NAMES. Census: none (CENSUS_KEYS untouched; zero names added; no tail pin moves). Plan: none. Decision: none WRITTEN -- migration 059's list stands as section 48 left it (`'rest'`, `'take'`, `'take_in_band'`, `'cover'`, `'exit_rest'`, plus `'exit_take_in_band'` / `'cover_in_band'` on a band IOC, and the replace causes `'replace_cent'`, `'replace_qty'`, `'replace_side'`, `'ttl'`, `'replace_unread'` written over a cancelled row); `rest_replaced`, `exit_rest_replaced`, `cover_replaced` are READ labels the presets print for a row whose `decision` is a cause word, and no row ever carries them. THE RAILS. None added; no knob, no migration, no constant in the worker. Every string is read-only SQL (no INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE / DROP / TRUNCATE / ALTER, no need_confirm, no $ARG), parsed under pglast (fills-missed 6 statements, take-band 2, exits-band 3, exits-paired 2, the hourly 33) and run on the real schema on the scratch database. The numeric thresholds are the plan's: 0.25 / 0.05 (exits-paired), 45 s and 10 % (the pairs statement), 15 s (the per-IOC line), 24 h everywhere.

FAIL CLOSED. A NULL `decision` prints `unrecorded` (fills-missed, take-band); a row whose decision is no cause word is never `replaced`, so an unrecorded rest is neither `rest_replaced` nor a pair. A NULL `his_level` or `wire` on either row of a pair, or no next row on the book, prints `unread` -- never `touch_moved` / `same_quote`, never a guess; the LEAD reads the SAME book's next row only (PARTITION BY book_id, pinned; never condition_id). `future_clock` on a row without `done_at` reads false. A rest with no word after an IOC is not counted as its same-tick rest; a rest placed 15 s or more after it is not either. On exits-paired a book with no reducing row of ours (`our_exit_filled` NULL) reads as 0 filled, so book 266's shape stays `he_reduced_we_held`; `our_peak` 0 is `no_position` before either arm. Lane 0b's `ostate` naming (the review's CRITICAL-1) is untouched: fills-missed's chain is byte-identical but for the one WHERE word. Named, not fixed: take-band's `fill_pct` (lane 0b's) divides by `count(*)` on the ROLLUP row, so the hourly on a database with NO long-book entry row inside 24 h raises division by zero at take-band and stops there under ON_ERROR_STOP -- pre-existing (the original preset on an empty scratch table raises the same), reproduced during this lane's build, not widened by it (the pairs statement counts and never divides).

## 62. The loss stop at $10,000 (2026-09-09, owner order)

Owner (2026-09-09 ~13:55Z): "Make the loss stop 10k (not 5k)". The copy sleeve's 24 h breaker had tripped at 13:40:06Z on -$8,104.36 realized (threshold -$5,000; `wlog_loss_1343`) after two proportional copies of his losers settled at 13:22:15Z -- book 1032 (aec-itfme-matvav-evgtiu, short 3,727 sh at 0.2624, -$2,752.00) and book 1056 (astatc-cs2-bb-ast map 1, short 3,731 sh at 0.40, -$2,238.60) -- with the mirror's day P&L at -$5,857.60 over 256 books (`h1335_db` mirror-pnl). The mirror refused increases from 13:40:15Z ("the mirror refuses increases") and ran exits only.

THE RULE. Both stops move to $10,000 together: `rules.MIRROR_LOSS_STOP_USD = capped_env("MIRROR_LOSS_STOP_USD", 10000.0)` (the mirror's own realized-loss stop over the re-arm window, section on L1) and `live_executor.PMUS_LOSS_BREAKER_USD` default "10000" (the copy sleeve's rolling-24 h breaker, which the mirror reads as its `limit` at every tick -- L2's rule, unchanged). Nothing else changes: the window rule, the re-arm key, the refusal order, the sleeve sum's statement, the mode line's `loss=` / `sleeve=` fragments.

THE NAMES. None new. Census `loss_breaker` / `mirror_loss_stop` as before.

THE RAILS. The same two constants, higher by the owner's word. `MIRROR_LOSS_STOP_USD` stays `capped_env`: the environment may only LOWER it (a value above $10,000 reads $10,000). `PMUS_LOSS_BREAKER_USD` keeps its plain env override (pre-mandate code, 2026-08-12), unchanged here.

FAIL CLOSED. Unchanged: an unreadable sleeve sum is `loss_breaker_unreadable` (exits only); a tripped `mirror_loss_stop` key holds until re-armed by hand.

WHAT IT DOES ON DEPLOY. The breaker re-reads the rolling sum every tick: at -$8,104.36 against -$10,000 the first tick after the deploy stops refusing increases -- the owner's order re-arms it by raising the bar, no `mirror-rearm` needed and none run. The next trip is at -$10,000 realized in 24 h.

Pinned: `tests/test_mirror_live_rules.py` (the default 10,000), `tests/test_l1_rearm_window.py` (the default by source; the file's fixtures, cut at the $5,000 stop, run under an autouse `_fixtures_limit` that sets the module attribute to 5,000 -- the tick reads it at tick time -- so the 17:07Z shape's -5,023.95 / -5,123.95 pins hold as written), `tests/test_l1_review_pins.py` (the same fixture), `tests/test_l2_review_pins.py` (10,000). No test deleted, skipped or weakened.
## 65. The loss stop switched off (2026-09-09, owner order)

Owner (2026-09-09 ~18:45Z), verbatim: "Remove the stop loss for the time being so that we are not down when it turns". Context: the day's mirror ran -$5,857.60 by 13:35Z and the sleeve breaker tripped at 13:40:06Z (section 62); after the 14:07Z re-arm the window read a further LOSS of $2,029.55 at 18:20:40Z, the sleeve $1,436.85 (`wlog_sleeve_1838` row 1056 mode line: `loss=-2029.5491/10000.0 since 14:07 sleeve=-1436.8519/10000.0`; negative is a loss, the trip is at `lost <= -limit`). The owner's read is that his whale's bad week turns, and a stop that takes the mirror off on the losing side of the turn misses the winning side.

THE RULE. `rules.MIRROR_LOSS_STOP = env_switch("MIRROR_LOSS_STOP", True)`: ON in code, as every switch is (section on MIRROR_SHORTS, MIRROR_FROZEN_EXITS); the environment may only turn it OFF (`MIRROR_LOSS_STOP=off` / `0`), never raise a limit -- `MIRROR_LOSS_STOP_USD` stays `capped_env(..., 10000.0)` and `PMUS_LOSS_BREAKER_USD` its plain "10000". OFF, at the three sites the worker reads the switch (`_global_guards`' sleeve read, `_global_guards`' stop step, `_loss_stop`): the copy sleeve's breaker sum is read and published but never blocks (over the re-arm window with no stop key standing; behind a standing or unreadable stop key it is still the full 24 h, as L1 left it -- the re-arm key is not read for the sleeve there) (no `loss_breaker`, no `loss_breaker_unreadable`, no LOSS BREAKER log line); a standing or unreadable `mirror_loss_stop` key does not hold and is left exactly as it stands (neither deleted nor rewritten: it holds again the tick the switch is back on -- the re-arm stays the owner's word, `mirror-rearm` untouched); the mirror's own sum is read over the re-arm window and published but never trips (no stop key written, an unreadable re-arm key or sum publishes `sum` None and blocks nothing). ON, every path is byte-for-byte what L1 / L2 / section 62 left: the readings carry no `stop` key.

THE NAMES. Census: none new (`mirror_loss_stop` / `loss_breaker` / `loss_breaker_unreadable` simply do not count while off). Heartbeat / mode line: the published readings (`_last_loss`, `_last_sleeve`) carry `"stop": "off"`, and the mode line prints `loss=<sum>/off since <HH:MM> sleeve=<sum>/off` -- `off` where the limit was, the sums still in view every tick. Plan fields, decision words, migrations: none.

THE RAILS. One switch, OFF-only from the environment, read by the MIRROR alone: the copy lane's own gate (`live_executor.maybe_execute` -> `_loss_breaker_tripped`, the entry system, off by owner order since 2026-09-05) does not read it and keeps its own $10,000 breaker. Nothing else moves: the limits, the window rule, the re-arm key, the refusal order (the stop step is still the last guard), the sleeve sum's statement, the exits carve-out (the stop only ever blocked increases; a reduce passes under a tripped stop ON and under the switch OFF alike).

FAIL CLOSED. Toward the owner's order: OFF, an unreadable sum or key blocks nothing -- there is no stop to fail closed toward, and the reading says so (`sum` None). ON, unchanged: an unreadable read is a stop. A word the switch does not know (absent, blank, a typo) is ON.

HOW IT IS APPLIED. The Render environment: `MIRROR_LOSS_STOP=0` (render-ops env-set, confirm=DO) and a restart / the deploy that carries this section. Turning the stop back on is `env-del MIRROR_LOSS_STOP` and a restart, on the owner's word, no deploy; a stop key that tripped meanwhile is not written (nothing trips while off), so the first armed tick reads the window fresh.

Pinned: `tests/test_loss_stop_switch.py` (the switch by source and by every word; the L1 trip shape past the limit opens OFF and trips ON; a standing stop key opens OFF, stands untouched, holds ON; an unreadable stop key and an unreadable re-arm key open OFF and are a stop ON (the review's two mutants); an unreadable sum opens OFF with `sum` None and is a stop ON; the L2 -6,000 shape opens OFF with no LOSS BREAKER line and the mirror's own sum still read; an unreadable sleeve opens OFF and refuses ON; the readings carry no `stop` key ON; a reduce passes either way; the mode line's `off`; this section). test_l1_rearm_window / test_l1_review_pins / test_l2_sleeve_window / test_l2_review_pins re-run green: their pins are the ON path. No test deleted, skipped or weakened.
## 67. The cap is per trade (2026-09-09, owner order)

(Section 66 is E25's, FILL lane 25, landing separately; this section is numbered past it.)

Owner (2026-09-09 ~21:05Z), verbatim: "I need the 2500 cap to be on a per trade basis not on a per match or market basis. Meaning we could have more than 2500 on an individual match if there are more than one trades in the same match. This needs to be fixed immediatley". The case: Galatasaray / Sporting, game ucl-spo-gal-2026-09-09 (`h2022_db` zz book rows and mirror-pnl rows 1221 / 1267). His ledger read short 39,446 shares on "No Sporting to win" and short 28,851 on "No Galatasaray to win"; at the 10% ratio that is 3,945 and 2,885 shares, $2,526 + $1,989 = $4,515 of collateral at our fill prices (3,945 x (1 - 0.3596), 2,885 x (1 - 0.3107); 0.3596, 0.3107). The per-game $2,500 (section on E1) went first-come: book 1221 (opened 18:45:54Z) took $2,025.65 at cost (3,163 sh), and when book 1267 opened (19:23:12Z) the game's other books held $2,054.99, so its room was $445.01 and it was sized at 645 sh = $444.60 (`game_cap_scaled`; 645 x (1 - 0.3107) = 444.60). We were 80% and 22% of his proportion on the two legs; the mandate is 10% and 10%.

THE RULE. `rules.MIRROR_NET_CAP_USD = max(MIRROR_NET_CAP_FLOOR_USD, unbounded_env("MIRROR_NET_CAP_USD"))`: UNBOUNDED by code default (math.inf, unbounded_env's one spelling, as the count caps and the day cap are since 2026-09-06). So the per-market cap at the mark (mi.target_shares, reached through rules.mirror_target) and the per-game room built on it (rules.game_room / game_capped, E1) never bind; `mirror_target` admits positive math.inf as unbounded (every other non-finite or unreadable cap is still `net_cap_zero`; `cap_usd` None now reads the module at call time instead of freezing the import-time default), `game_room` returns math.inf under the unbounded cap, and the worker writes the plan's `game_room` as null (a JSON row; jsonb refuses an infinity) while still recording `game_exposure`. The $2,500 lives on the ORDER: `rules.MIRROR_CLIP_USD` (capped_env 2500.0, the mirror lane's per-order clip applied by `room_scale`) is untouched, so a target past $2,500 at the mark is reached across more than one rest, each at most $2,500 -- his 100,000-share market at 0.50 is a 10,000-share target whose FIRST rest is 5,102 shares at a 0.49 wire.

THE NAMES. None new. `game_cap_scaled` / `game_cap_full` / `game_unreadable` / `cand_game_full_skipped` are never emitted under the unbounded cap (the plan carries no `game_cap`; `game_unreadable` is guarded by `math.isfinite(room)` in `_tick_book` -- the review's MEDIUM-1: with no room to fail toward the book is sized in full and the plan's null `game_exposure` alone records the unreadable sum); they come back exactly as E1 built them the moment the environment lowers the cap. The standing REST of an add is compared against the plan as the clip would size it (`_act`: `p_cmp`, `rules.room_scale(p.qty, wire, MIRROR_CLIP_USD, ...)` on the same collateral the rest was placed on -- the review's HIGH-1): compared raw, a clipped rest read `qty` (grew) every tick past the rest-life floor and was cancelled and re-placed as its own twin twelve times an hour to `replace_capped`; now it stands (`open_order_pending`) and the remainder follows the tick after it fills.

THE RAILS. The environment may still LOWER `MIRROR_NET_CAP_USD` to a finite figure (the 1-share probe's $25); an override at or under 0 lands on the $1 floor as before; 'inf' or an unparseable value is the default (unbounded, not a lowering). `MIRROR_CLIP_USD` stays capped_env at $2,500. mi.MARKET_NET_CAP_USD ($2,500) stays the shadow's own number for its would_orders column; `_shadow_check` reconstructs the shadow's raw from its `capped` rows and re-caps at the cap THIS lane sized at (unbounded), so `shadow_live_disagree` does not fire on the difference.

HOW IT IS APPLIED. The deploy that carries this section, and `env-get MIRROR_NET_CAP_USD` on render-ops BEFORE it: any standing value is a LOWERING under the new reader (a standing `2500` is E1's per-game cap unchanged, byte for byte) and must be `env-del`ed (confirm=DO) with the restart that applies it. The first tick's proof is `book=<id>` on any game with two live books: `game_room` NULL on both plans; a number there means the environment still lowers the cap.

FAIL CLOSED. Unchanged where it matters: a cap nobody can read is `net_cap_zero` (no exposure); an unreadable sibling under a LOWERED cap is still no room. Under the unbounded cap there is no room to fail toward, and the reading says so (`game_room` null).

THE SUITE. Every test written under the $2,500 per-market / per-game cap runs under an autouse fixture in tests/conftest.py that sets the module attribute to 2,500.0 the way an operator's lowering lands it (the reader and the import default are untouched); tests/test_cap_per_trade.py declares `NET_CAP_UNBOUNDED = True`, opts out, and pins the production default: the constant and its environment readings, `mirror_target` / `game_room` on the unbounded and the lowered cap, the two-market game sized at the full 10% (B 3,000 where the per-game cap gave 1,400; no game_cap, game_room null, the plan JSON-clean), a 10,000-share target reached 5,102 shares a rest, the lowered cap re-arming E1's room byte for byte, and this section. test_mirror_live_rules re-pinned where it read the default: the reloads read math.inf, `mirror_target`'s `cap_usd` default is None. No test deleted, skipped or weakened.

LIVE PROOF AFTER DEPLOY. First `env-get MIRROR_NET_CAP_USD` reads no value (else `game_room` prints a number and the order is not carried out). Then the next game with two markets over $2,500 at 10% shows both books at their full 10% target on `book=<id>` (no `game_cap` on the plan, `game_room` null) and the hourly census reading `game_cap_scaled` 0; a market whose 10% is over $2,500 at the mark shows a first rest at the clip and a second rest the tick after it fills.

## 69. E27 -- the entry's tolerance: the take inside a band of 2c capped at 5% of the cost per share, on a long add and, for the first time, on a short add; the remainder rests at his cent as today; exits untouched (2026-09-09, FILL lane 27)

(Sections 66 and 68 are E25's and E26's, FILL lanes 25 and 26, landing separately; this section is numbered past them.)

Owner (2026-09-09 ~21:1xZ, item 1 of the seven-item list "the only thing that stands between us and him by portional standpoint"), verbatim: "1. Yes, lets take it immediately with a tolerance that you feel wont impact profitability" -- the answer to "fill rate at his price", the first of the seven things between us and his proportion. Until this lane the take inside the band was lane 2's ONE cent on LONG adds only (section 47, decision D1 (a)); a short book's add never took (section 47: "a short book's add is never band-taken"; FILL_C2_missed_replace.md 95: "Not proposed: widening any band beyond 1c ... a take on the short book's add (lane 6's gate)"). The owner has now ordered the tolerance; D1 is SUPERSEDED by this section on the long side and extended to the short.

THE TOLERANCE, DECIDED FROM THE DATA ON FILE. FILL_R5_counterfactuals.md 75-78 (rule (i), the band table, at the missed classes' +3.46% ROI at his price -- fills-missed at 20:22Z agrees: h2022_db 2086-2089 missed_replace 755 fills / $211,126.45 at +0.0208, missed_open 497 / $164,170.71 at +0.0371, partial 148 / $71,138.38 at +0.0237, against filled 926 / $216,379.52 at -0.1979 (2085): what we miss wins, what we fill loses): a 1c band converts 38.2% of the unfilled rests for +130 of P&L (75), a 2c band 57.7% for +52 on 18,452 of added stake at 1.34c paid (76), a 4c band 80.6% for -276 (77), a 5c band 87.1% for -440 (78); "1c-2c is the only range where the converted dollars stay non-negative after the spread" (91). So 2c is the widest band whose converted dollars stay non-negative, and it is CAPPED AT 5% OF THE COST PER SHARE so a cheap contract never pays a fifth of its stake for a fill (his 0.10 at 2c would pay 20% of the stake; capped it pays the 1c floor, lane 2's own): the long side keeps the 1c floor (D1 is never narrowed by the cap), the short side has no floor (a band under a cent is no band: today's rest). The pessimistic bound stands as R5 wrote it (89, A6: converted rests carrying the filled class's ROI land every band at about -24% paired); the FVM's missed class reads +0.3327 against filled 0.02 (probe_2010_full.log 1377: filled 287 / missed 113, 72 unresolved), so the missed class may carry more than +3.46% -- the lane is built so a wider band is one constant's default with a review (the E14 comment's own rule), never a shell's. The take-band table at 20:22Z (h2022_db 2333-2335, 2342, 2344; 24 h): rest bucket 1c 36 rows 80.6% filled roi -0.3556, bucket 2c 72 rows 70.8% filled roi -0.1620, bucket 3+ 146 rows 59.6% filled roi -0.0856, `take_in_band` 153 rows 80.4% filled paid_med 1.0c roi -0.3539, `take` (at level) 249 rows 49.0% filled roi -0.7215; the replaced rows by side, long 359 (2353) / short 603 (2376) -- the short's rests are the larger churn and had no take at all.

THE RULE. (A) THE BAND. `rules.take_band_width(his_px, intent)` -> the band in price for this add: on a LONG add min(MIRROR_TAKE_BAND, max(0.01, MIRROR_TAKE_BAND_FRAC x his)) -- the 1c floor never above the constant itself, so an environment that lowers the constant under a cent is honoured (a departure from the plan's max(0.01, min(...)) wording, which would have raised a sub-cent lowering to a cent; equal to it wherever the constant is at or above a cent); on a SHORT add min(MIRROR_TAKE_BAND, MIRROR_TAKE_BAND_FRAC x (1 - his)) (the collateral per share on a short is one minus the contract price, `rules.room_scale`'s own rule; `intent` is the BOOK's, only the exact BUY_SHORT spelling is the short formula); None when his price is not in (0, 1), when MIRROR_TAKE_BAND is unreadable or at or under 0 (off), when MIRROR_TAKE_BAND_FRAC is unreadable, or when the width is not a positive finite number; a frac at or under 0 is the frac's own off (the short side no band, the long side its floor alone). The table at the default: long his 0.10 -> 1c (the cent 0.11), 0.30 -> 0.015 (0.315 floors to 0.31: a cent over), 0.42 -> 0.02 (0.44), 0.80 -> 0.02 (0.82), 0.995 -> no cent on the ladder; short his 0.65 -> 5% of 0.35 = 1.75c -> sell_wire(0.6325) = 0.64, 0.50 -> 2c -> 0.48, 0.90 -> 0.5c -> none, 0.95 -> 0.25c -> none. `rules.band_cent(his_px, band=None)` keeps its signature and its floor-to-cent rule (buy_wire(his + band), strictly above his cent, else None) and reads the LONG width when `band` is None -- every existing caller and test reads the new default through it. `rules.short_band_cent(his_px, band=None)` -> sell_wire(his - band): the lowest cent at or above his level less the band; None unless STRICTLY under his own sell cent sell_wire(his), and None for a band under a cent (the short side's no-floor rule, whatever cent the subtraction happens to cross -- narrower than the long side's, where lane 2's 0.479 + 0.005 -> 0.48 stands). `rules.take_in_band` keeps its signature for the long side (hashed); `rules.short_take_in_band(bid, ask, his_cent, band_cent)` -> the bid STRICTLY under his cent and at or above the band cent (`not at_or_through(SELL, his_cent) and at_or_through(SELL, band_cent)`), both cents on the ladder with the band cent under his, the ask not read.

(B) THE WORKER. `mirror_live._take_band` (the long side) keeps its shape and its verdict words (`unread` / `at_level` / `off` / `uncounted` / `out` / `waiting` / `in_band`) and stamps the plan's `take_band.band` with the WIDTH this lane computed and `take_band.frac` = {his_px, cost, width} beside it, so `book=<id>` shows why the cent is what it is (his 0.30: cost 0.30, width 0.015, the cent 0.31). `mirror_live._short_take_band(t, book, r, his_px, plan)` mirrors it on a SHORT book's add with the SELL-side reads -- his cent `rules.sell_wire(his)` (his level in LONG space, `_his_level`'s figure: the price the contract is sold at), the BID instead of the ask, `rules.short_take_in_band`, `rules.take_allowed` on the SELL side at the band cent -- the SAME plan key `take_band` and the SAME verdict words, `frac.cost` = 1 - his; `at_level` on the short means the bid at or over his sell cent. THE SHORT'S AT-LEVEL TAKE (the review's HIGH-1): the lane as built left `at_level` to today's rest at the short wire ceil(max(his, ask)) -- so his 0.65 with the book 0.65 / 0.66 RESTED at 0.66 while 0.64 / 0.66 was band-taken at 0.64, a cent paid exactly where a free fill stood. Now a short add whose bid is at or over his sell cent but UNDER the short wire (so the take-at-wire, which fires in a locked book alone, did not) sends ONE SELL IOC limited at his cent -- a sale at his price or better, as the long has taken at his cent since E4 -- through the same `_entry_take(..., first=True)` (decision 'take', census `take_at_his_level`, the remainder resting at the short wire with decision 'rest' as today); an arm in `_act` after the band's IOC and before the rest, reading the plan's `take_band` (`verdict` at_level, `his_cent`), behind the 059 columns like the band (an uncounted take is not sent), behind the same wait (`rules.take_allowed` on the SELL side at his cent), re-read at the send (`bid_moved` rests the whole quantity), and OFF with the band (MIRROR_TAKE_BAND at 0: the short side byte for byte today). The band arm in `_act` that read `if p.side == BUY and not short and leg_action == "add": band = _take_band(...)` reads the short's band beside it (`elif p.side == SELL and short and leg_action == "add": band = _short_take_band(...)`), and the band IOC that follows goes out for the short exactly as the long's does: ONE IOC limited at the band cent through the same `_entry_take(..., first=True, in_band=True)` call (its row's decision 'take_in_band' -- the SAME word; the row's side / intent tell the sides apart, and the take-band preset's THIRD statement -- this lane's, the review's HIGH-2 -- grades the short add's rows on the bid under his SELL cent: `band_c` = ceil-cent(his_level) - bid_at_place, `given_c` = ceil-cent(his_level) - wire, our_usd the collateral, roi on (wire - payoff) / (1 - wire); the first statement grades LONG adds alone, as lane 0b built it and test_render_ops_take_band pins it), sized on the room at that cent through `_room_qty` (the per-order clip, section 67; the collateral (1 - the band cent) a share on a short), re-read at the send as every IOC is (E18's `_ioc_reread` at the band cent, the SELL side reading the bid: `bid_moved` withholds it), the unfilled remainder resting at the wire -- his cent on a long, the short wire on a short -- with decision 'rest', byte for byte today's rest (the plan's quantity goes in and `_place_reserved`'s own room re-read at the wire sizes it, lane 2's LOW-1). The at-level take (the ask at or under his cent on a long; the bid at or over the wire on a short, and -- HIGH-1 -- at or over his sell cent under the wire, at his cent) fires FIRST and is never this. The IOC's limit is the band cent whatever the ask inside it: the venue fills at the ask or better, the row's `ask_at_send` records the re-read's ask, and the take-band preset's `paid_med_c` reads the fill against his cent. THE FAST ADD: `_fast_book` reaches the band through `_tick_book` -> `_act` alone (no band read of its own, pinned by source), so a woken flat book on either side band-takes on the wake through the same two readers; a rest standing on the woken book is `order_open` as before; E21's keep-branch take off a standing rest is not this lane's (the short's add there stays "cadence only", section 54).

(C) NOT TOUCHED (hashed against 52e1d52 in the tests): exits and reduces -- lane 3's exit band INERT at 0.01 (D2; `rules.exit_terms`' `take_band` / `cover_band` and `MIRROR_EXIT_TAKE_BAND` / `EXIT_BAND_INERT_AT` / `MIRROR_EXIT_TOL` byte for byte; `_exit_take`, `_flatten_send`, `_flatten_vanished`, `_frozen_exit`, `_cover_qty`, `_sell_qty` name nothing of the entry band; a reduce and a cover never reach either reader: on a short book the cover is the BUY plan, on a long book the reduce is the SELL plan); the catch-up allowance (E12, section 33: `rules.open_catchup` decides the TARGET on the mark -- a block the catch-up refuses is never bought, band or no band; this lane decides the PRICE of the take on whatever target exists, never its size); the standing-rest keep branch (E18: a rest already standing is never converted by the band, on either side, on either tick path); the rest cent (his cent on a long, the short wire on a short); the loss stop, the day cap, the per-trade cap and the clip (the IOC is sized by the same `room_scale`); the shadow; every census name and every from-end pin; `_ioc_reread`, `_entry_take`, `_place`, `_place_reserved`, `_short_wire`, `_wire_for`, `_room_qty`, `rules.admission`, `rules.rest_decision`, `rules.take_allowed`, `rules.at_or_through`, `rules.order_decision`.

THE NAMES. Census: NONE new -- `take_in_band` counts both sides at its one emit site (the band arm, after the at-level take and before the rest); the take-band preset's long table reads the long side and its third statement (side 'short') the short side. Plan: `take_band` {his_cent, band, band_cent, bid, ask, frac {his_px, cost, width}, verdict} on every first-sight add plan of a long AND now a short book (`band` is the width, None when off; `frac.cost` his price on a long, 1 - his on a short). Decision: 'take_in_band' now names a SHORT add's band IOC too; migration 059's comment stands as written and the list it names at the INSERT is restated: 'rest', 'take', 'take_in_band', 'cover', 'exit_rest' (with lane 3's 'exit_take_in_band' / 'cover_in_band' and lane 10's 'take_on_add', and the replace causes after a cancel as before) -- 'take_in_band' on an add's IOC in band whatever the book's side; the short's at-level take (HIGH-1) writes 'take' as the long's does. Heartbeat: none. The short side's own names `short_open` / `short_add` count PLACEMENTS -- a band IOC (or the at-level IOC) and the rest after it are two -- not books. Refusal names: none new, none widened.

THE RAILS. `MIRROR_TAKE_BAND = capped_env("MIRROR_TAKE_BAND", 0.02, floor=0.0)` -- the default rises 0.01 -> 0.02 by owner order; the environment may only LOWER it: 0.01 is lane 2's long band byte for byte (the width reads exactly 0.01 at every long price) with the short side at 1c where 5% of its collateral admits a cent (his 0.65 -> 0.64, 0.50 -> 0.49, 0.90 -> none); 0.005 is honoured (no cent inside it on most prices; 0.529 -> 0.53 as lane 2 pinned); 0 is the band off on both sides, today's rest; a negative value lands on the floor 0.0; junk / inf / blank / 1e400 the default (0.02, the ceiling); 0.03 lands on 0.02. `MIRROR_TAKE_BAND_FRAC = capped_env("MIRROR_TAKE_BAND_FRAC", 0.05, floor=0.0)` -- lower = a narrower band on cheap contracts (0.02: 1.04c on a long at 0.52, 0.7c on a short at 0.65 -> no band); 0 = the band off on the SHORT side and the 1c floor alone on the long side; 0.10 lands on 0.05. Both read at call time by `take_band_width` (a test's monkeypatch and the operator's lowering apply without a restart of the rule); `MIRROR_TAKE_BAND=0` in the environment is the one line that turns the lane off. No env_switch (OFF is the constants at 0); no migration (061 the newest); no new decision word; render-ops.yml: the take-band preset gains a THIRD read-only statement, the short add's own (decision, bucket) table, in both places (the standalone case and the hourly's copy; the hourly stays ten presets, the table adds bucketed rows only -- h0222.txt's take-band block is rows 714-738 of 784 under the runner's HEAD 1500); the rail count 22 -> 23 capped_env, the waits 5. `MIRROR_TAKE_AFTER_S` is read through `rules.take_allowed` by both readers, so a LENGTHENED wait makes the band rest-first on the short as on the long (`waiting`). The band IOC is one op and one re-read, never a cancel: the replace budget is not spent by it.

FAIL CLOSED, by input. His price unreadable or off (0, 1) -> no band (the width None; the short reader `unread`, his cent None even where sell_wire would have capped 1.5 at 0.99). Either constant unreadable, or MIRROR_TAKE_BAND at or under 0 -> no band (`off`, the rest as today). The width under a cent on the short side -> no band (`off`). A band cent not strictly past his own cent (above on a long, under on a short) -> no band. The tick's bid (short) or ask (long) missing or off the ladder -> `unread`, the rest (a one-sided book with no mark is refused `no_mark` before any plan, as today). The 059 columns absent (`t.order_cols` not True) -> `uncounted`, no band IOC, the rest by the 050 INSERT on both sides. The re-read at the send past the band cent -> the IOC is not sent (`ask_moved` on a long, `bid_moved` on a short: {bid_at_plan, bid_at_send, wire = the band cent}), the whole quantity rests at the wire, one op; the re-read unreadable or the call budget spent -> `ioc_quote_unread` / `ioc_reread_capped`, the rest. A rest already standing -> never converted (the keep branch; the fast gate's `order_open`). A reduce, an exit, a cover, a flatten, the frozen exit -> never read. The short's at-level take (HIGH-1): the plan's `take_band` missing or not a dict, its verdict not `at_level`, its `his_cent` unreadable or off [0.01, 0.99], the 059 columns absent, MIRROR_TAKE_BAND unreadable or at or under 0, the wait not elapsed, or the leg not an add -> the rest at the short wire as today (never an IOC); the re-read's bid under his cent -> `bid_moved`, the whole quantity rests. The room at the band cent under one share -> `over_room` as today (the take-first's own rule; on a short the collateral at the band cent is higher than at the wire, so the room binds a share earlier there: $0.35 of room buys a share at the 0.66 wire and none at the 0.64 band cent). A spent venue budget -> nothing. The band is judged on the tick's quote and again on the re-read; both must hold. Under `MIRROR_TAKE_BAND=0.01` in the environment the long side is lane 2 byte for byte (pinned: the same cents, rows and verdicts under test_e14's fixture at 0.01).

THE TESTS (`backend/tests/test_e27_take_tolerance.py`, 22; `backend/tests/test_render_ops_take_band.py` gains one). ## 58. The tick's record: the tick ring, the fast tick's prelude, the speed block (2026-09-09, FILL lane 14)

THE ROWS. The heartbeat carries ONE tick. At 22:44Z it read tick_s 38.0 at 15 live books (hard2/tick_2245.txt rows 331 / 298) with timing walk 0.3 / orders 1.0 / books 11.3 / candidates 16.2 / venue_calls 52 / cand_cap 36, wall fast_wait 2.3 / fast_work 8.4 and fast n 1 / calls 4 / placed 1 (row 320); fifteen minutes earlier, at 22:29Z, 12.4 s (tick_2229.txt row 330) with candidates 0.8 and fast_work 2.4 for the SAME one-market / four-call / one-placement fast shape (row 319); at 20:29:53Z, 76.9 s at 64 books (hourly_2030.txt rows 426 / 392: candidates 32.5 / books 31.7 / fast_wait 43.6 / fast_work 8.0 / venue_calls 106 / cand_cap 10, row 415); at 17:06:36Z, 51.9 s at 54 books (h1707.txt rows 44 / 10: candidates 12.4 / books 27.1 / fast_wait 45.3 / fast_work 1.8 / venue_calls 85, row 33). Four heartbeats, four ticks; the distribution the owner's "as fast as his" line is judged on -- and the 6.0 s (8.4 - 2.4) that sit between the two fast ticks of the same shape -- were in no file, so FILL_plan_C section 2's D5 (the yield, lane 15) and section 3 item 6 (lanes 11 / 13 at 15 and at 54-64 books) had nothing to wait on. Expected dollars: $0 -- this lane is the instrument, not a change.

THE RULE. Nothing in the order path changes (`_act`, `_place`, `_place_reserved`, `_entry_take`, `_fast_gate`, `_fast_book`, `_fast_candidate`, `_tick_book`, `_walk_candidate`, `_tick` and the whole rules module are hashed against 219f140 in the tests). (a) THE RING. At the END of `tick_once`'s `finally`, after every block is built and after the candidate memo's persist, the full tick appends ONE entry to `_tick_ring` -- a deque of `TICK_RING_MAX` = 240 entries (about 2 h at POLL_S 30 s; a constant, no knob) -- read off the blocks the tick just built and the census the heartbeat carries, never re-measured: `[at, tick_s, walk, orders, books, candidates, books_live, venue_calls, cand_cap, cand_reads, yielded, fast_n, fast_wait, fast_work, fast_prelude]` (`TICK_RING_FIELDS`; `at` the tick's clock `t.now` to one decimal; `cand_reads` is `t.cand_reads`, the candidate walk's own reads that the 054 refusal rows record; `yielded` reads 1 only when the heartbeat's `yielded_tick` is the bool True -- lane 15's key, 0 until it lands; the three fast fields are the fast ticks since the last publish, as `short.wall` and `short.fast` carry them). A block missing (an abandoned or refused tick) or a field unreadable reads 0 / 0.0; an entry that cannot be built at all is logged (`tick ring entry dropped`) and dropped -- the record never breaks the tick. `_persist_tick_ring` then writes the ring under ingestion_state `mirror_tick_ring` = `{"ticks": [[...], ...], "at": ISO}` the way `_persist_cand_memo` writes the candidate memo: never before the boot read (E6 review MEDIUM-5's rule, kept), at most once per `TERMINAL_MEMO_WRITE_S` (60 s), a failed write logged (`mirror_tick_ring write failed (<type>)`) and retried on a later tick with the ring kept in memory meanwhile, an empty ring never written -- and with NO boot read: the ring is the process's own record, a deploy starts it empty and the preset prints no rows for the hours before it. (b) THE PRELUDE. `fast_tick_once` hands the instant it acquired `_TICK_LOCK` to the tick (`t.fast_acquired`); `_fast_tick` stamps `t.timing["fast_prelude"]` ONCE, just before its per-market loop -- after the table and column probes, the mode ladder, the venue, the global guards, the ratios, the books read, the open rows and the caps -- as the seconds since that acquire; a fast tick that never reaches the loop (nothing woken, a guard refused it, or it raised before the loop) is prelude alone: `fast_tick_once`'s `finally` settles it to the whole work. The stamp is a value recorded, never branched on. (c) THE BLOCK. `_speed_block` serves `short.speed` = `{cand_yielded, fast_prelude, fast_walk, ring}` on both tick paths: `cand_yielded` the census count under lane 15's name (0 until it lands), `fast_prelude` the fast ticks' prelude since the last publish on a full tick (`_fast_speed`, summed and reset on the take exactly as `_fast_wall` is) and its own on a fast tick, `fast_walk` 0 (the fast tick's own positions walk is NOT built -- FILL_plan_C section 0.3's "Not built" list; the key is the block's settled shape), `ring` the ring's length as this tick leaves it. A sibling of E10's `short.wall` and E9's `short.fast`, whose keys the E10 / E9 pins fix exactly and which are byte-identical before and after this lane. (d) THE PRESET. `.github/workflows/render-ops.yml` gains `tick-ring` right after `mirror-tick` in the case order (the help arm regenerated, `hourly` still last) -- one read-only SELECT over `jsonb_array_elements(s.value->'ticks')` of the `mirror_tick_ring` row grouped by hour and ordered by the hour's INSTANT, newest first (not its time of day: across midnight 00:00 prints above 23:00), LIMIT 48: ticks, tick_med_s / tick_p90_s / tick_max_s, cand_med_s / cand_max_s, books_med_s, fast_wait_p90_s, yields, fast_prelude_med_s, every field read by its POSITION (e->>1 tick_s, e->>4 books, e->>5 candidates, e->>10 yielded, e->>12 fast_wait, e->>14 fast_prelude) behind jsonb guards (the value must be an object whose `ticks` is an array; an entry must be an array of at least 15 elements whose read positions are numbers; the instant must sit in [0, 4102444800] and every other read position in [-1e9, 1e9], each range read as a `numeric` inside a CASE so the `::float8` cast in the SELECT never sees a jsonb number past float8's range) -- and the SAME statement wired into the hourly after mirror-tick's section, so the hourly carries TEN presets (test_render_ops_hourly's PARTS and the two files that re-run it re-pinned: nine was lane 0b's count). The WHERE word `s.key = 'mirror_tick_ring'` lives in TWO places (the case and the hourly's copy), both pinned.

THE CODE. `backend/sportsassets/workers/mirror_live.py`: `_STATE_TICK_RING`, `TICK_RING_MAX` (beside `_STATE_CAND_MEMO`); `_tick_ring`, `_tick_ring_last` (beside `_cand_memo_last`); `_fast_speed` (beside `_fast_wall`); `_Tick.fast_acquired`; `_fast_speed_take`, `_speed_block` (beside `_gate_block`); `TICK_RING_FIELDS`, `_tick_ring_entry`, `_tick_ring_append`, `_persist_tick_ring` (beside `_persist_cand_memo`); `tick_once`'s `finally` (the speed block after the gate block, the append and the persist after the candidate memo's); `_fast_tick`'s stamp; `fast_tick_once`'s hand-in, settle and block. `render-ops.yml`: the `tick-ring` case, the hourly regenerated, the help arm regenerated. `tests/test_mirror_live_worker.py`'s `_comparable` strips `speed` beside `timing` / `data_api` / `fast` / `wall` / `gate` (the ring's length grows on every full tick); `tests/test_e9_review_pins.py`'s `_STATE_*` list gains `_STATE_TICK_RING` (E13's precedent for `_STATE_TERMINAL_CONFIRM`); the hourly's count re-pinned nine -> ten in test_render_ops_hourly, test_render_ops_fill_answers and test_pnl_m_review_pins.

THE NAMES. Census: NONE (0 names; keys[-13] is still `drift_smaller_open`, no tail pin moved). Heartbeat: the `short.speed` block (`cand_yielded`, `fast_prelude`, `fast_walk`, `ring`); the ring entry's `yielded` reads the heartbeat key `yielded_tick` lane 15 will set. Plan fields: none. Decision words: none -- migration 059's list stands as section 53 left it. Ingestion_state key: `mirror_tick_ring` (written by the full tick alone; the fast path writes no state, the E9 pin holds). Log lines: `mirror_tick_ring write failed (<type>)`, `tick ring entry dropped (<type>)`. Preset: `tick-ring`; its columns above.

THE RAILS. None: no knob, no wait, no environment read (`MIRROR_TICK_RING*` appears nowhere), no migration. `TICK_RING_MAX` 240 and `TERMINAL_MEMO_WRITE_S` 60.0 are constants; the ring is bounded by the deque's maxlen, the write by the memo cadence (one INSERT ... ON CONFLICT per minute at most, about 240 x 15 numbers), the preset by LIMIT 48 (the ring holds about two hour buckets). The order path is byte-identical (hashed).

FAIL CLOSED. The ring write failing -> logged, the ring kept in memory, the write clock NOT advanced, so the next tick retries and carries every entry (pinned: the failed attempt, then the retry with both). A first tick refused ahead of the boot read -> its entry on the ring (tick_s and 0.0 where the blocks are), nothing written; the boot read never reads the key, so an old process's ring in the row is overwritten by the new process's first eligible write with only what it measured itself. A block missing on an abandoned tick -> 0.0 where the block does (every block is built before the append). A field that is not a number (a bool, a string, NaN) -> 0 / 0.0, never a raise; an entry that cannot be built -> dropped, logged. `fast_prelude` on a fast tick that skipped everything -> the prelude alone, equal to its `fast_work`; on a fast tick that raised before the loop -> the same, under `fast_tick_failed`. The preset against a NULL-typed (`'null'::jsonb`), scalar, array-shaped or object-without-`ticks` value, a `ticks` that is not an array, an entry that is not an array of 15 numbers, an instant out of range, a number past float8's range in any read position (a jsonb number is a `numeric`, so `1e400` passes `jsonb_typeof = 'number'` and a bare `::float8` would RAISE -- the range guard per position keeps the cast from running; the worker never writes such a value, an operator's hand edit could) -> prints NOTHING for it and never an error that stops the hourly bundle under ON_ERROR_STOP (pinned on the scratch database with thirteen malformed values and the hourly's text through its first eight sections; a SQL NULL cannot stand in the row -- 001_init.sql 175 declares `value JSONB NOT NULL`). The row absent (a deploy's first minute) -> no rows.

THE TESTS (`backend/tests/test_fill_c14_tick_ring.py`, 32, on test_mirror_live_worker.py's `_tick` and test_e9_fast_path.py's `_fast`; the scratch-database pins on lane 0b's World, skipped without a server, run here on a local PostgreSQL 16). The constants, the key, no knob, the persist's shape (the boot-read gate, the cadence, the log line, no `_state(` read, no boot reader naming the key); the 22:44Z / 22:29Z / 20:30Z heartbeats read as entries by position (tick_2245 320 / 331 / 298 / 336; tick_2229 319 / 330 / 335 and its books_live 16 at 297; hourly_2030 415 / 426 / 392 / 431) and a lane-15 yield as `yielded` 1; a missing block or an unreadable field reads zero, a broken tick object is dropped; three full ticks -> three entries in order with `short.speed` beside `short.timing` / `short.wall` / `short.fast` / `short.gate` at exactly their pinned keys, the entries JSON-clean; 241 -> 240, the oldest dropped; the write at most once a minute after the boot read (NOW, +30 no, +59.9 no, +60 yes, four entries carried, `at` ISO); the write refused -> logged by name, the ring kept, the clock not advanced, the retry carrying both entries; an empty ring (the first tick's entry dropped) never written; nothing written before the boot read and the deploy's overwrite; the fast tick's prelude on a fake clock (the table guard 1.5 s, the one book 3.0 s -> `short.speed.fast_prelude` 1.5 beside `fast_work` 4.5 / `fast_wait` 0.0, `short.wall` and `short.fast` at exactly their pins; the next full tick publishing 1.5 and the ring entry carrying `[1, 0.0, 4.5, 1.5]`; the tick after 0.0); a fast tick that skipped everything (the guard refused it -> 1.5 == `fast_work`; nothing woken -> 0.0; raised before the loop -> 2.2 == `fast_work` under `fast_tick_failed`); the order path and the rules hashed (sha256[:16] of ten sources and the rules module), no order-path source naming the ring or the prelude, the stamp after the caps and before the loop with one mention; the label after mirror-tick in case order, the help arm, the hourly's copy byte for byte between the mirror-tick and mirror-pnl markers, TEN parts, the hourly test's four pins re-run, one read-only statement ordered by the hour's instant, both WHERE places, the positions and the guards (the seven `numeric` range CASEs, no bare `::float8` in the WHERE), the eleven columns, pglast on the statement and on the hourly, the cap arithmetic (1,075 + 5 + 2 + 48 + 1 + 1 <= 1,500); on the scratch database (its session pinned to UTC) a three-tick ring by hour (22Z: ticks 2, tick_med 25.2 = (38.0 + 12.4) / 2, p90 35.4 = 12.4 + 0.9 x 25.6, max 38.0, cand 8.5 / 16.2, books 8.3, fast_wait p90 7.3 = 2.3 + 0.9 x 5.5, yields 0; 20Z: ticks 1 at 76.9 / 32.5 / 31.7 / 43.6), a yield counted once, a 23:50Z and a next-day 00:10Z tick printing 00:00 above 23:00, and thirteen malformed values (among them `1e400` at positions 0 and 1, a string at 14, a 14-element entry) printing nothing with the bundle running over each;
