This lane is the extension, not a duplicate.

THE RULE. When a book closes under a sign flip -- a `cashed_out` / `cancelled` verdict with `plan.sign_flip` True, the turn lane 5 writes as `plan.turn = {from, to, his_net, at}` -- the closing tick, full or fast, wakes the market for the fast path exactly as his fill does: once `_SQL_BOOK_STATE` has succeeded on that path, `_maybe_close_episode` calls `_turn_wake(book)` -> `_fast_wake(condition_id)` (notify's fast half, byte-identical: the market joins `_FAST_WOKEN` bounded by `_WOKEN_MAX`, one fast run is scheduled when main() has armed the path and a loop is running, nothing otherwise), writes `plan.turn.woke` = whether the market sits in the woken set after the call, and counts `turn_woke_fast` when it does. The next fast tick (`FAST_TICK_MIN_S` 2.0 s after the last) takes the market off the set, re-reads the open books, finds none on the condition, and runs `_fast_candidate` -> `_walk_candidate` -> `rules.admission` with every clause in its own order and every refusal by its name exactly as today: `full_tick_pending`, `walk_stale`, `unmapped`, `cand_no_mark_skipped`, `cand_terminal_skipped`, `venue_calls_capped`, `capped_tick`, then the walk (`side_band`, `drift`, `snapshot_stale`, `venue_already_holds`, the game cap, the short gates, `no_mark`, `market_closed`); `_flip_since` hands the flip's first sight as today (flow-only, fail closed toward NOT buying the block). NOTHING IS ADMITTED THAT IS REFUSED TODAY: a refusal at the wake is recorded by lane 5's `reopen_refused` on the closed row beside the 054 row, and the next wake is his next fill, as today. A book that closed inside THIS fast tick: the tick took the market off the set at its start and its `books` were read before the close, so the re-wake puts the market BACK for the NEXT fast tick (the run loop's next iteration, the floor between), where the books are re-read and the market has none. A close on a full tick: the wake is made while the full tick holds the tick lock; the scheduled fast run waits on it and runs the moment the full tick lets go (the first wake runs at once -- no floor to wait out). The wake is `_fast_wake` alone, never `notify`: it writes nothing to `_WOKEN` and does not set `_WAKE` (no full tick is started on a turn -- FILL_plan_C.md's "Not built": C6 B), and a full tick that starts before the fast run drains `_FAST_WOKEN` at its start exactly as it drains his fill's fast wake, so the market is then that tick's candidate stage as before this lane.

## 54. E21, the add's take (2026-09-09, FILL lane 10)

THE ROWS. fills-answered at 22:25Z named `open_order_pending` on 95 fills of his / $22,841.69 at a lag median of 3 s and a p90 of 29 s (hard2/h2225.txt 938): the tick SAW each fill and refused it because a rest of ours stood, and left it to the next poll. The 22Z `increase` line reads 48 placed / 26 filled, his_to_order median 18 s, p90 116 s, order_to_fill 68 s (835; the `take` line 8 / 8 / 6 s / 44 s / 1 s, 836; the first-order line 69 fills, 62 answered, median 15 s, p90 96 s, 917), and the only two first orders over 60 s in the hour were the `order_open` shape: book 825's rest 4703 placed 22:18:24 for his 22:18:19 fill, his next fill 22:18:25 answered by 4707 at 22:19:53 = 88 s (866 / 869); book 826's 4695 at 22:16:50 for his 22:16:49, his 22:16:51 fill answered by 4702 at 22:17:59 = 68 s (870 / 876). The class `refused:open_order_pending` is 38 fills / $44,884.07 / 36 resolved / +0.5465 ci 0.2998 (1002) -- the one class where speed pays -- of which book 347 (the frozen short) holds 2 / $31,970.05 unresolved (1088); in_book 33 / $39,118.01 / +0.4080 (1044), before_open 3 / $3,615.90 (1045), no_book 2 / $2,150.16 / +0.9608 (1046). At 22:44Z the census read replace_capped 0 / take_capped 0 / fast_tick_skipped 0 (tick_2245 300) and the fast block n 1 / 10.7 s / calls 4 / placed 1 (320) inside a 38.0 s tick (331).

THE RULE. When a fill of his that GROWS his position lands on a market where our ENTRY rest is standing, the fast tick no longer refuses the book `order_open` and leaves it to the next poll: it reads that one order's status (one paced call on the shared budget, books nothing), re-plans the book under its lock, and runs today's `_act` on it -- so the decision is the full tick's own, seconds after his fill instead of a poll and a tick later. `keep` (the rise inside max(1 share, 2%) -- `same`; or the rest under the 45 s floor -- `min_life`, the growth carried as `add_pending` exactly as today: no young rest is cancelled by this lane); `replace` cause `qty` past the floor (budget-gated as today; on a long book lane 2's band-take on the re-plan when the ask is one cent over, section 47); or the take off the rest when the ask is at or through his cent -- which now sizes the IOC at the PLAN's grown quantity on a LONG book's entry (before the lane `left = int(min(p.qty, o.qty - booked_filled))`: the rest's own leaves, his add dropped), through `_room_qty` (the $2,500 clip and the game cap by construction), the remainder resting at his cent as `_entry_take` does. The IOC an ADD fires from the keep branch writes its own word, `take_on_add`, so the read tells it from the first-sight `take`. Every other wake on a book with an order open stays `order_open` (an exit rest, a flatten, a cover, an IOC still placing, an 'unknown' or 'placing' row, a rest placed after the fast tick's own clock), `order_open_game_unread` (another book of the SAME game has an order this fast tick did not read: the fast tick makes step O for the admitted book alone, so the game's room would read unreadable -- `_resting_add_usd` None, `_game_exposure` None, `rules.game_room` 0, the target clamped to the ledger -- and the re-plan would CANCEL the admitted rest under `on_target`, a cancel the full tick never makes; books 825 / 829 are one game, lib-flu-cpa-2026-09-08 -- the review's CRITICAL-1, `_game_siblings_readable`) or lane 3's `order_open_his_exit` (a reducing wake -- tested FIRST, a count, no cancel; X2(a) stays deferred). THE FAST PATH NEVER FREEZES A BOOK AND NEVER BOOKS A FILL: a status read that returns nothing is named `fast_status_unread` and the row is left exactly as it was for the full tick's own `_reconcile_open`, which applies `order_state_unknown` at its own step O a poll later, byte-identical -- the one named departure from "the fast tick inherits the full tick's rule", because a fast tick fires on every fill wake (FAST_TICK_MIN_S 2 s, FAST_TICK_MAX 5) and a venue blip on the status read would freeze books at WAKE cadence instead of poll cadence (FILL_plan_C section 5, both judges). An increase refusal on the re-plan (`drift`, the day cap, the sleeve caps) cancels the rest under its own name exactly as the full tick does at its next poll: the same rule, earlier. On a SHORT book the gate passes for a SELL_LONG entry rest on ORDER_INTENT_BUY_SHORT with his add on the short axis, and the plan goes through `_act`'s short rest path (`_short_wire`) exactly as today -- never a `take_on_add`, never the grown-quantity sizing: on a short book the lane changes cadence only.

THE CODE. rules: `MIRROR_FAST_ADD_REPLAN = env_switch("MIRROR_FAST_ADD_REPLAN", True)` beside `MIRROR_FROZEN_EXITS` (in `__all__`; read through the module at call time, three sites: the gate, the sizing, the word); `order_decision(action, is_take, short_cover, in_band=False, on_add=False)` returns `'take_on_add'` only when `is_take and on_add is True and action == "add" and not in_band` (the band's word wins; a cover stays `'cover'`; a reduce's IOC `'take'`; a truthy non-bool is not True). mirror_live: `_order_open_his_add(t, book, fills)` beside `_order_open_his_exit`, pure, True only when every fact reads -- `fills` a list; the open row this fast tick read (`t.fast_open`) with `state == "open"` (never 'placing' / 'unknown'), `kind == "increase"`, `tif != "IOC"`, an `order_id`, the book's `intent` a string whose leg action for the row's side is `add`; `placed_ts` a number at or before `t.now` (the fast tick stamps its clock before its lock, section 51: a rest placed after it is the full tick's, whose clock is stamped after the lock -- read here it would be `future`); ## 63. E23 -- a cancelled rest whose venue fills outran the cancel is booked: the cancel re-reads the row's final figure, and a book frozen venue_ledger_disagree adopts its own cancelled rows' unbooked fills from the trade log (2026-09-09, FILL lane 23)

THE ROWS. Book 986 aec-atp-ugobla-danrin-2026-09-09, ORDER_INTENT_BUY_LONG, ratio 0.1, opened 11:15:29, avg_cost 0.5600, gross_buy $230.70, standing row 412015 filled 411.95 @0.5600 (hard2/book_986_1426.txt lines 998 and 1019). Its twelve order rows (lines 1003-1014): 5823 increase BUY_LONG GTC 2937 @0.56 cancelled `cancel_pending` filled 6.13 @0.5600 (11:15:29 -> 11:34:22); 5908 2874 @0.56 cancelled `ttl` filled 405.82 @0.5600 (11:34:37 -> 11:45:00) -- the two that booked the standing row's 411.95 (6.13 + 405.82; the whole-share ledger column reads 413 -- +1.05 of the rounding every booking leaves on it, `_book_fill`'s int(round(...)), task 24's class); then TEN rests of 2467 @0.56 -- 5966, 5998, 6056, 6126, 6180, 6218, 6250 (`snapshot_stale`), 6263, 6292, 6326 (`loss_breaker`) -- every one cancelled with venue_state `canceled`, filled 0 and avg_px NULL, placed 11:45:17 .. 13:16:00, done 11:55:31 .. 13:22:18. The mirror-frozen read at 14:23:15Z (hard2/frozen_1423.txt line 998; the read's banner line 994): frozen `venue_ledger_disagree` 14:03:49, read_at 14:23:07, ledger 413, venue 1215, manual 0, registered 0, his_net 28764.9, target 2876, frozen_exit {held: frozen_no_his_exit, target: 2876, venue_own: 1215}; the 14:23Z hourly's `frozen_reasons` {venue_ledger_disagree: 1} (hard2/h1423_db.txt line 19) and its zz book row of 986 `state=frozen` (line 93); the shadow's verdict "frozen: venue and ledger disagree" with ledger 411 / venue 1215 from 14:22:46 to 14:25:51 (book_986_1426 lines 1084-1088), where its 11:46:04 verdict had read venue 412 against ledger 411 (line 1089): the venue agreed at 11:46 and read 1,215 at 14:03 -- 802 = 1215 - 413 whole shares against the column -- 803.05 against the standing row's 411.95 when the venue's 1215 is exact -- on our side at our cent that arrived while the ten rests stood, with nothing that could ever book them: E16's thaw needs venue == ledger, no path re-reads a cancelled row, and E22 (section 56) names this class in its DOES NOT FIX. WHY THE ROWS READ FILLED 0 (found on 67fbaf3, not guessed): the cancel path ALREADY reads status -- `_cancel_and_settle` reads `_order_status` up to CANCEL_READS (3) times, CANCEL_READ_GAP_S (0.3 s) apart, until `le._rest_terminal`, books the delta off that read (`_book_delta`) and writes the row through `_finish_order`. A row written `cancelled` with venue_state `canceled` is the write of a TERMINAL read (a non-terminal read leaves the row `unknown` under `cancel_pending`); a row whose fill the read carried books it (5908's 405.82 came through this very path); so on each of 5966 .. 6326 the cancel's terminal status read carried filled 0. The deploy restart the brief names (14:03Z) is not the cause: every one of the ten was done between 11:55:31 and 13:22:18, and a restart between the cancel and the read leaves `unknown`, never `cancelled`. WHEN the venue matched the 802 against those ids -- after the cancel's acknowledgement, or before it with the status not yet carrying them -- is in no row; what the rows prove is that the cancel's terminal read is not the row's final figure.

THE RULE. PART A, THE CANCEL RE-READS THE FINAL FIGURE (prevention; `_cancel_and_settle` -> `_cancel_final_figure`). When the cancel's status read is terminal on a row `_cancel_reread_wanted` names -- a GTC / GTD rest with an order id, any kind but a take: an increase, a reduce, an exit rest, a cover rest -- the tick makes ONE more paced `_order_status` read (a `_venue_read` on the shared budget, the same read `_finish_order` trusts) BEFORE the row is booked and written. The final read carrying MORE filled than the cancel's answer -> `cancel_fill_late`; the row is booked off the final read exactly as a terminal fill is booked (`_book_delta` -> `_book_fill`: the order's cursor, the standing row's add with the order id, the ledger, avg_cost, gross_buy), then written by `_finish_order` as before -- `cancelled` with its cancel word, filled and avg_px as the final read carried them; the `recent` entry `cancel_fill_late` {order_row, answer, final, px}. The final read carrying no more -> the cancel's answer is booked and written as today, nothing counted. The final read that cannot be made (None, no `filled_shares`, or a non-terminal state) -> `cancel_fill_unread`; the row is booked and written off the cancel's answer as today AND memoed (`_cancel_reread_pending`, keyed by the order row's id: {oid, book, whale, seq, at, tries, reason}) for the next tick's re-read -- never a freeze. `_cancel_reread` runs on the full tick after step O (`_reconcile_orders`) and before any book is planned: every entry noted on an EARLIER tick (never the tick that noted it, by `seq`) is read once -- the row by `_SQL_ORDER_ROW` / `_SQL_ORDER_ROW_047` (the open-orders projection with `WHERE o.id = $1 AND o.state = 'cancelled' AND o.order_id IS NOT NULL`, tag `ml-order-row`), its book by `_sql_book_read`, under the book's lock -- and what the status reports filled beyond the row's booked figure is booked through `_book_delta` (`cancel_fill_late`, the `recent` entry); a row no longer `cancelled`, or gone, drops out without a read; a read that cannot be made counts `cancel_fill_unread` again and the entry is dropped after `_CANCEL_REREAD_TRIES` (3) reads with a warning (the book's own venue-vs-ledger freeze and part B are the road from there). A final read or a re-read PAST the row's quantity -> `cancel_fill_overfill` (a refusal name on the census dict, not a CENSUS_KEYS name), nothing booked off that read, the book frozen `overfill` by `_freeze` (the E5 freeze path; the `recent` entry `cancel_fill_overfill`), the row written off the cancel's answer; the desk is NOT tripped -- `_trip_live_off` is for a SALE past the ledger, and a status figure past the row is a reading nothing can book (the choice, named here for the reviewer). A cancelled row booked late keeps its state and its cancel word: the fill is booked, the record says what the cancel was. PART B, THE ADOPTION (cure; `_disagree_fill_adopt`, called from `_tick_book`'s disagree branch inside its `was_frozen` arm, after E22's `_lost_fill_adopt` and before `_frozen_exit` -- never on the transition tick, E5 review F1). On a book that was FROZEN when its tick began under `venue_ledger_disagree`, on a tick whose venue read is FRESH (`t.walk_at` not None, the thaw's own guard), when the book has cancelled rows WITH an order id booked below their quantity and done before this tick's clock (`_SQL_CANCELLED_ROWS` / `_SQL_CANCELLED_ROWS_047`: the open-orders projection with `WHERE o.book_id = $1 AND o.state = 'cancelled' AND o.order_id IS NOT NULL AND o.done_at < to_timestamp($2) AND o.booked_filled < o.qty - 0.000001`, tag `ml-cancelled-rows`; the predicate read again in code so a drifted statement cannot widen it), with no manual shares on the slug, the surplus = venue - ledger - registered on the book's own side (positive on a long book, negative on a short: its adds take the ledger down) and no larger than the rows' unfilled room: the tick reads the venue's trade log for the market (`_cancelled_log_fills`: `pmus.recent_trades(slug, since)` through `_venue_read`, raw) over [the earliest such row's placed_at - `le._ORPHAN_SKEW_S` (30 s), now], at most once per `rules.MIRROR_LOST_FILL_REREAD_S` (300 s) per book (E22's memo shape: the plan's `disagree_fill_at` and the per-process `_disagree_fill_read_at`, its own dict, bounded at `_LOST_FILL_MEMO_MAX` 4,000 books), and matches the log's fills BY OUR OWN ORDER IDS -- never a fingerprint, never a size match: the ids are the venue's own answer to our placements; a fill under any other id is ignored and never booked. When the log's fills for those ids, less what each row already booked and signed by each row's leg action (`_order_action`: a cancelled reduce's late fills take the ledger down; a short book's leg in ledger space), would leave the whole-share ledger column inside the freeze's own tolerance of the venue (`mi.VENUE_LEDGER_TOL_SHARES`, 1.0 -- the one condition under which E16's thaw can take the book; never an exact match against the column's surplus, which carries the rounding of every booking: 986's 413 over 411.95): each row's unbooked fills are booked at the log's prices (the unbooked part's fill-weighted price: the id's notional less the booked part at the row's avg_px, over the unbooked shares) through `_book_delta` with `{state: the row's venue_state, filled_shares: the id's total, avg_px}` and `maker` as the row was placed -- the same cursor, standing-row add, ledger and avg_cost as a terminal fill -- the row's reason becomes `adopted from the trade log after the cancel` (`_adopt_reason`, so a frozen reduce's marker survives; `_SQL_ORDER_REASON`), the row keeps its state and its cancel word, one `recent` entry `disagree_fill_adopted` {order_row, order, shares, px} per row, the census `disagree_fill_adopted` once per book per tick, one warning line. The book STAYS FROZEN this tick (the frozen exit that follows holds `frozen_fill_this_tick`: the walk is stale by the fill) and the EXISTING thaw takes it -- and for `venue_ledger_disagree` that is E16's two-reads rule (`_thaw_verdict`, untouched): `thaw_held: one_read` on the next fresh agreeing tick, `thawed` (`venue_agrees`) on the one after, then the live plan toward his net; this lane calls no thaw (E22's rule). Every other reading names a hold and books nothing (THE NAMES). Bounds: at most one trade-log read per frozen book per wait, only when the surplus is on the book's side and inside the rows' room; the read is a `_venue_read` and counts against the venue budget.

THE NAMES. Census, ONE block of six inserted before E19's `drift_smaller_open` (keys[-13]) and `registered_no_increase` (keys[-12]), after FILL lane 11's `cand_market_closed_db` (now keys[-20]): keys[-19:-13] = `cancel_fill_late` (the final read, or the memo's re-read, carried more filled than the cancel's answer; booked), `cancel_fill_unread` (the final read or the re-read could not be made; the row off the answer, memoed), `disagree_fill_adopted` (the rows' unbooked fills booked from the log), `disagree_fill_unread` (the log unreadable: raises, truncated), `disagree_fill_unexplained` (manual shares on the slug; the surplus nothing or on the other token; more than the rows' room; the log naming nothing for our ids, one warning line; the log's fills for our ids already booked; a partial: the log leaves the column more than the freeze's tolerance under the venue), `disagree_fill_ambiguous` (the two readings of one id disagree: the log under the row's booked figure, over its quantity, our id on the other side, a fill of ours the log does not price, or the log naming more than the venue holds past the freeze's tolerance). THE READ (`pmus.recent_trades`, `pmus.trade_own_order`, `pmus.trade_other_order`). The venue prints BOTH orders of a fill in full (aggressorExecution.order and passiveExecution.order, each with id / side / intent / quantity / cumQuantity / tif / price / manualOrderIndicator). The account's OWN order is the aggressor order when the trade's `isAggressor` is the bool true, the passive order when it is false -- and only that: a counterparty AGGRESSOR's order is printed with its own intent and its own indicator (the 20 rows of 15:53-16:14Z on 1156's market read isAggressor false / the aggressor order BUY BUY_LONG MANUAL / the passive order ours, CCYHSY30WSJH = 6852 BUY_SHORT AUTOMATIC: vt_1156_1639 row 114 and its kind), so "carries an intent" is NOT ours (27 of the 44 rows carry a defined intent on both orders and would read so; a 28th carries ORDER_INTENT_UNDEFINED on the aggressor, which the parser's `_bare_word` reads as no intent). Each parsed row keeps E22's ten keys byte for byte and gains `manual` (True when the own order carries MANUAL_ORDER_INDICATOR_MANUAL, False on any other indicator word, None when the indicator or the own side is absent), `own_order_id`, `own_side` (the contract side, BUY / SELL: a SELL is a short or a sale of the long token, a BUY the long token bought or a short covered), `own_intent`, `own_qty` / `own_price` / `own_tif`, and `self`. THE SELF RULE, read from the rows and said here: a self-trade is the account on both sides, and the venue then prints the account's OTHER order as the counterparty -- so `self` is True when the counterparty order's id is one of the account's own order ids in the same read, or when one trade id is printed twice (once per side); "both orders carry an intent" is not the rule (it would flag the 20 counterparty app orders). No row of the day reads self (no counterparty id of the 44 is an own id; no trade id repeats).

THE RULE (`_hand_net`, `_book_hand_reduce`, `_hand_closing`; the paragraph over `_hand_log_fills`). On a book whose FRESH walk (`t.walk_at` not None) disagrees with ledger + manual + registered past `mi.VENUE_LEDGER_TOL_SHARES` (1.0; the desk's `manual` rows and E5's register are the existing terms and go first: the hand is read only for what they leave) -- a live book at E16's suspect stage, a frozen book under any reason, a CLOSING book with shares held and no order in flight (`_hand_closing`, before the closing branch's `_maybe_close_episode`) -- the venue's trade log for the slug is read (`pmus.recent_trades` through `_venue_read` on the shared budget) since the book's `opened_ts` - `le._ORPHAN_SKEW_S` (30 s), with NO bound at the tick's clock (`t.now` is the tick's START and the positions walk, step R, runs seconds later: a hand fill the venue dates inside the tick is in the walk, and the gap check below refuses what the walk does not hold), at most once per `rules.MIRROR_LOST_FILL_REREAD_S` (300 s) per book ON THE SAME RESIDUAL (venue - ledger - manual - registered, whole shares): a residual that moved past the tolerance is a new reading (the desk traded again: 1156's second short 3 min 20 s after the first) and reads at once, at most once per book per tick; the read is made FIRST on every disagreement, before E20's wrong-sign check, before E16's suspect, before E22's and E23's adoptions. The account's own MANUAL fills on the slug (a row whose `manual` is True; a zero-quantity print is nothing; a fill dated before the window is not counted) are walked in time order against the book's ledger in long-token terms -- a hand BUY of the long / cover of a short is +, a hand SELL / short is - -- less what an earlier read already adopted per hand order id (the record below): the part on the book's side (the same sign as the leg, or any hand fill on a flat leg) is `adds`; the part against the leg is a REDUCE up to the leg, and what lands past the leg is `adds` too (the desk's own opposite position: 1129's 186). The whole reading is one judgement: the whole-share ledger the adoption would leave (ledger + reduces) plus manual, registered and adds must agree with the venue inside the tolerance in EITHER direction, else `hand_ambiguous` and nothing is adopted. Then: (C1) `adds` are the desk's own -- the FOURTH explained term: the comparison is judged on venue - ledger - manual - registered - adds, the UNROUNDED adds (the seat below takes the whole-share figure; a fractional manual row beside a fractional hand fill would otherwise leave the rounded delta past the tolerance on a book the hand explained: gap 0.7, delta 1.2, E16's suspect) (`hand_explained`; the book does NOT freeze, stays live on its LEDGER exactly as today, sizes nothing on the desk's shares: the plan's venue seat `venue_seat` = venue - manual - registered - adds on every re-plan, the short proof is not recorded on a reading with the desk's shares in it, the sign-flip close reads the venue flat net of them); `_frozen_venue_own` subtracts them (`r.hand_adds`, set on the reading for the tick) so no frozen reduce ever sells or buys back a hand share (1156's seat reads -247, not -2049); E20's wrong-sign check is never reached on a book the hand explained (its delta is inside the tolerance) or adopted (the tick returns); the check reads `venue_own` = venue - adds, equal to the walk's figure on every reading that reaches it (1129's shape takes no hold because the residual is EXPLAINED, not because `venue_own` is 0). (C2) `reduces` -- the desk selling the book's long, covering the book's short (1075's 3,966; 1129's 4,852 of the 5,038) -- are ADOPTED as the book's exit at the hand's fill-weighted price through `_book_hand_reduce`: ONE transaction -- `le._book_mirror_sell` (the standing row's sale, min(shares, the leg, the row's shares), the row's pnl), `rules.book_sell` + `_SQL_BOOK_LEDGER_SELL` (the ledger, gross_sell, realized at the hand's price against the book's avg_cost: 1075 (0.8623 - 0.97) x 3966 = -427.14, 1129 (0.41 - 0.3116) x 4852 = 477.44) and the adoption record -- the same primitives a terminal exit fill of ours books through, with NO mirror_orders row (a hand order is not a placement of ours: migration 059 records the word that placed a row and a hand order carries none; no `decision` word, 059's list stands as section 47 left it) and no fill_answers row (the record answers HIS fills, and a hand fill is nobody's answer). `hand_adopted` is counted, `t.filled_books` gains the book, the plan carries the adopted shares and the hand's order ids, and the book is judged on its new ledger NEXT tick (the plan sized above the comparison is the old ledger's: the adoption tick writes the plan `kind: hand_adopted` and places nothing): 1075 -> ledger 0 -> the closing branch's own close on the same tick (the flat row, `le._close_mirror_episode`, `closed_cashed_out` -- the existing close path, never `_thaw`, never a new one); 1129 -> ledger 0 with the desk's 186 short as `adds` explained on the next tick, then the live path on 0; a live book reduced in part -> its live plan on the smaller ledger. (C3) a hand reduce past the book's shares splits: the book's part adopted, the rest `adds`. (C4) THE RECORD: the adopted shares per hand order id live in ingestion_state under `mirror_hand:<book id>` ({adopted: {order id: shares}, at, orders, px}), written INSIDE the booking's transaction, so the same log read after the adoption (the window opens at the book's open) never books them twice: a later read takes each order's recorded shares off its fills first; a record naming more than the log is `hand_ambiguous`. (C5) AN ORDER OF OURS IN FLIGHT: on the live / frozen branch a hand REDUCE found under a standing row of ours (`t.open_by_book`, `t.nonterminal` or the book's `open_order_id`) is NOT adopted -- the row is cancelled under `hand_reduce` through `_cancel_open_for` (E23's part A books what it filled; a cancel the budget refuses stands and is tried again; a non-terminal row, nothing to cancel, waits for step O), the tick writes the plan `kind: hand_order_open` and plans nothing on that book (the ledger may have moved under the cancel's settle while the walk did not), the reading (`hand_order_open` on the plan: the same fields, verdict `order_open`) is NOT memoed, and the next fresh walk with nothing standing adopts. An adoption under a standing exit row would leave that row sized on the OLD ledger, and its fill past the new one is `_book_fill`'s overfill: the freeze and the desk trip (the 20:57:19Z class; the venue fills a SELL_LONG past the holding into a short, 1129's own 5,038 on 4,852) -- a path to `mirror_live` OFF the tree without the lane cannot reach on this shape. The closing branch's own cancel runs before `_hand_closing`, which reads nothing with an order standing, as before. (C6) THE RECORD IS READ BEFORE THE LOG: an unreadable or malformed record spends no venue call. On a frozen book the explanation leaves the disagree branch un-entered, so the EXISTING thaw takes it -- E16's two reads for venue_ledger_disagree (`one_read`, then `thawed`), one read for the rest, `thaw_off` with rules.MIRROR_FROZEN_EXITS off -- this lane calls no thaw; on a live book nothing is suspected or frozen and his target is followed on the ledger (1156: the increase 53 toward -300 in the fixture world, a BUY_SHORT at his cent; never a BUY of the long token).

THE NAMES. Census, ONE block of four inserted before E19's `drift_smaller_open` (keys[-13]) and `registered_no_increase` (keys[-12]), after E23's six (now keys[-23:-17]): keys[-17:-13] = `hand_explained` (hand fills on the book's side explain the venue: counted once per read that so reads, and on the adoption tick when the sale left the desk's own past the book), `hand_adopted` (a hand reduce booked as the book's exit), `hand_unread` (the log could not be read: raises, truncated; the book's `opened_ts` unreadable; the adoption record unreadable or malformed), `hand_ambiguous` (a self-trade, a hand fill without its order id, its side or its price, one order id printed with disagreeing figures (side / quantity / limit / tif), the record naming more than the log, or a hand net that leaves the comparison outside the tolerance either way -- including no hand fill at all on a disagreement: nothing adopted, the freeze as today, E5's register the road). The tail pins that count from the end move by four (test_mirror_live_worker -118: -> -122: with the CENSUS literal gaining the four names in place, -119 -> -123; test_e9_review_pins -118:-114 -> -122:-118, -119 -> -123; test_e17 -50:-42 -> -54:-46; test_e20 -42 / -41 / -40 -> -46 / -45 / -44; test_e14b -41 / -40 -> -45 / -44, -34:-31 -> -38:-35, -27 / -26 / -25 / -20 -> -31 / -30 / -29 / -24; test_e14's +27 offset from `take_in_band` now `hand_explained` and +31 `drift_smaller_open`, -40 -> -44; test_e19's predecessor chain from `drift_smaller_open` now the four names then E23's six, each older link by four; test_fill_t1 -34:-31 -> -38:-35, -40 -> -44; test_fill_t2 -36 / -35 -> -40 / -39, -40 / -39 / -37 -> -44 / -43 / -41; test_fill_x1 -39:-36 -> -43:-40, -40 / -41 / -42 -> -44 / -45 / -46; test_e22 -31:-27 -> -35:-31, -27 / -32 / -34 / -40 -> -31 / -36 / -38 / -44, -26 / -25 / -20 -> -30 / -29 / -24; test_e23 -19:-13 -> -23:-17, -27 -> -31, -31:-27 -> -35:-31, -34:-31 -> -38:-35, -40 -> -44; test_fill_c11, test_fill_c9, test_fill_c16, test_e21 by four each). Plan: `hand` = {net, adds, reduces, fills, at, verdict, residual, orders, adopted, px} -- the verdict `explained` / `adopted` / `unread` / `ambiguous` / `adopt_write_failed`; `at` the clock of the last log read and `residual` the reading it was made on (the memo: E22's shape, the plan's record beside the per-process `_hand_read_at`, which also carries the record across a quiet skip), the whole record carried tick to tick until a new read moves it; on the adoption tick the plan's `kind` reads `hand_adopted` and the book's `last_reason` the same; on a tick that found the hand reduce under an order of ours the plan carries `hand_order_open` (the reading, verdict `order_open`), the tick's `kind` and the book's `last_reason` read `hand_order_open`, and the cancelled row's reason is `hand_reduce`. Decision: NONE written (no order row; a cancel under a reason writes no decision word, as `market_closed` / `wrong_sign_hold` write none). Heartbeat: the census names and the `recent` entry `hand_adopted` {shares, px, orders, ledger, realized}. The reading gains `hand_adds`. The freeze's `detail` gains `hand` (the adds) only when they are nonzero. `mirror-frozen` and `book=<id>` print `hand` beside manual / registered off `last_plan->'hand'` (no migration: the plan JSON carries it); the hourly gains nothing.

THE RAILS. None new: the wait is E22's `rules.MIRROR_LOST_FILL_REREAD_S` (min_wait_env, 300.0; the environment may only LENGTHEN it; rules 1031), read through `rules` at call time -- on the SAME residual (a moved residual is a new reading: at most one read per book per tick, and a stuck residual reads once per wait); the tolerance is the freeze's own `mi.VENUE_LEDGER_TOL_SHARES` (1.0, analytics/mirror.py 89) on the explanation and on the memo's residual; `FLAT_TOL_SHARES` (1e-6) on every share equality; `le._ORPHAN_SKEW_S` (30.0) the window's skew; the venue budget (`VENUE_CALLS_PER_TICK`: a spent budget reads nothing this tick); `_LOST_FILL_MEMO_MAX` 4,000 the memo's bound. No knob, no `capped_env`, no `env_switch`, nothing in `rules` of the lane's own, no migration (061 stays the newest), no decision word. `rules.MIRROR_FROZEN_EXITS` OFF (as the operator set it at 16:5xZ today and reverted at 17:30Z): nothing here places -- a frozen book reads `frozen_exits_off` and `thaw_held: thaw_off` as before; the hand still explains and adopts (a read and a booking, never a send). NOT TOUCHED (hashed against acc672d in the tests): `_thaw`, `_thaw_verdict`, `_thaw_agrees`, `_reconcile_placing`, `_mark_lost`, `_lost_fill_adopt`, `_disagree_fill_adopt`, `_cancel_and_settle`, `_cancel_final_figure`, `_registered_shares`, `_agree_record`, `_second_disagreeing_read` (the E16 two-reads rule), `_frozen_exit`, `_trade_log_fills`, `_cancelled_log_fills`; the E5 register path (`mirror-register`'s SQL), the day stop and the loss stop are untouched. E22 and E23 keep every read of their own: the hand's read is one more, made first (their tests' read counts are re-pinned by the hand's read, told apart by its window).

FAIL CLOSED, by input. A cached walk (`t.walk_at` None: the fast tick) -> no read; the last record explains while its wait holds on the same residual, else nothing (today's path). The memo not expired on the same residual -> nothing new this tick, the last verdict carried (an `unread` / `ambiguous` carried explains nothing: the freeze as today). The budget spent -> nothing this tick. The log unreadable (raises, truncated) -> `hand_unread`, today's freeze (E16's suspect then the freeze on a live book; the freeze as it stands on a frozen one), no adoption, nothing sized on the venue. The book's open clock unreadable -> `hand_unread`, no read. The adoption record unreadable or malformed -> `hand_unread`, nothing judged, no venue call spent (the record is read first). A hand fill on a market with no book -> nothing (no book, no comparison; the walk's other slugs are not read). A row the venue did not mark (`manual` None: the own side or the indicator absent) or marked AUTOMATIC -> not a hand fill (a counterparty's MANUAL order on the other side explains nothing: the 15:53Z Zheng rows). A self-trade, an unpriced fill, a hand fill without its order id or side, one order id printed twice with disagreeing figures, the record naming more than the log, a hand net that leaves the comparison outside the tolerance either way, or no hand fill at all -> `hand_ambiguous`, nothing adopted, the freeze as today (the register is the road). A fill dated before the window -> not counted; a zero-quantity print -> nothing. The desk's `manual` rows or a register row on the slug -> the existing terms first; a register explaining the whole surplus leaves the branch un-entered and the hand never read. A hand reduce on a book with an order of ours in flight -> on the closing branch not read this tick; on the live / frozen branch NOT adopted, the order cancelled under `hand_reduce`, nothing planned on the book that tick, the reading not memoed and made again on the next fresh walk (a cancel the budget refuses stands and is tried again; a non-terminal row, nothing to cancel, waits for step O). A hand fill the venue dates after the tick's clock but inside the walk -> counted (no upper bound); one dated after the walk is not in the walk and the gap check refuses it (`hand_ambiguous`). A booking that fails -> `write_failed` (counted, the book frozen under it as `_book_delta`'s failure is), the record NOT written (one transaction), `adopt_write_failed` on the plan, nothing counted of the lane's; a sale the primitive refuses (no entry price, the row not live) -> `refused:<why>`, nothing written, `adopt_write_failed`, today's reading stands (1129's shape without the adoption is the wrong-sign hold). The reason a hand ADD is never adopted and a hand REDUCE never explained: a hand add sized as the book's would be sold back at his exit (1156's fight); a hand reduce explained instead of adopted would leave the ledger holding shares the venue does not (1075's phantom -3,966, bought back at 0.97). Nothing here sends an order: the adoption tick places nothing, the explained book plans on its ledger, the frozen exit after the explanation sizes on venue_own net of the hand and holds by name when his net is not under it.

THE RULE (`rules.his_net_drop`, `rules.exit_confirmed`, `mirror_live._exit_ref`, `_exit_snap`, the E25 block of `_tick_book`). (A) THE DROP. The REFERENCE is the reading the plan before sized on -- the row's last plan's `net` and its `at` (`_exit_ref`; a quiet skip's plan has no reading and carries the last read's as `exit_ref`, so a drop found on the read after a quiet spell is judged against it); while a hold stands (the last plan's `exit_confirm` reads `unconfirmed`) the reference is the reading BEFORE the drop (`exit_confirm.prev` / `since`), because the held plan's own `net` is the dropped reading and judged against it the next tick would read no drop at all. `rules.his_net_drop(prev_net, net, elapsed_s, short)` reads the change ON THE BOOK'S LEG (a short book's fall is its net rising toward zero) and calls it SUDDEN when the fall exceeds max(MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_SHARES 1,000, MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_PCT 0.30 x |prev_net|) AND the two plans are no more than MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_S 600 s apart (a standing hold re-judges the same drop with no window: it was judged when first seen). Book 1177's 8,473.8 (the plan before, 17:15:57) -> 3,262.8 in 37 s: change -5,211.0, drop 5,211.0, threshold max(1,000, 0.30 x 8,473.8) = 2,542.14, sudden (from 17:15:05's 8,597.6, the fixture's shape: -5,334.8 against 2,579.28 in 89 s, sudden too). A RISE reads drop 0 and is never sudden: entries -- the increase, the take on an add, the first open -- are untouched, and his buys keep today's latency. (B) THE CONFIRMATION, in `_tick_book`'s reduce branch AFTER E12b's `flow_hold` and E15's `reduce_unwitnessed` (the drop is judged on a reduce those ADMITTED, never instead of them; a plan either of them holds or caps is not this rule's): when the plan is a `reduce` or the paired flatten (`flatten_paired`) that is NOT the sign flip's -- the flip close is never guarded (section 4 of the batch-C plan; FILL_plan 120: the flip is the reopen mechanism), so a flip's flatten (`plan.sign_flip` True) fires as today with nothing of this rule's on it; a confirmed vanish `flatten_vanished` keeps its own reader, the data API's `_confirm_gone`; the operator's flatten-all is never held -- and the reading is a sudden drop, `_exit_snap` reads the per-market position of THIS tick: the figure counts only when the read was fresh this tick (`snap_market_fresh` True) AND stamped (`t.mkt_at`, the callee's `ts`, written on the fresh path of `_market_snap`) AND made no earlier than the newest fill's ingest clock less `le._ORPHAN_SKEW_S` 30 s (`mi.fills_clock`; the fills are read before the snapshot inside the tick, so this bites only under a clock skew between the ingestion host and this one) -- else `snap` None, `snap_why` `snap_unread` / `snap_unstamped` / `snap_before_fill`. `rules.exit_confirmed(net, drop, snap_net, short)`: True when, on the leg, the venue shows him at or below the new reading plus MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_TOL_PCT 0.5 of the drop (the venue has moved at least half of the way; a snapshot still at the OLD net is False); None when the snapshot is unread. Confirmed -> `exit_confirmed`, the exit fires THIS TICK with no delay (the held 393 of 1177 the tick the venue reads 3,262.8; the 17:04:57Z crossing itself, 11,393.3 -> -1,566.7, is a sign flip and is never judged). Not confirmed, or unread -> `exit_unconfirmed`: the exit is HELD this tick -- nothing new is sold; the plan before's standing exit keeps its whole E4 life exactly as under E15's hold (the plan handed to `_act` is `mi.plan` toward the plan before's target, `exit_confirm.cap`, a reducing side only: a witnessed 40-share rest at his cent is re-quoted and taken while the phantom 433 is held; nothing standing: nothing sent, `reason` `exit_unconfirmed` on the row, a resting ADD cancelled under the hold's name because the target fell); E15's `reduce_ref` stands (a held plan is not a witnessed state either). THE HELD BOOK'S NEXT READ: its reason is not `on_target`, so E6's rule reads it HOT next tick (never quiet-skipped), its quote read and its per-market read with it (`_market_snap(book=True)`, uncapped, on E10's priority lane) -- the read is paced by the tick and never budgeted away for a book -- and the tick after re-judges the same drop against the same reference: confirmed -> fires; a drop that persists past MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_MAX_TICKS 3 held ticks fires on the fourth anyway, `exit_confirm_expired`, whatever the venue reads -- fail toward following him, NEVER a permanent hold. (C) THE FLAP AVERTED: a held drop whose reading is back inside the threshold of the reference before it fired (8,473.8 against 8,597.6: drop 123.8 under 2,579.28) ends the hold, counted `exit_flap_averted`, `exit_confirm` = {prev, net, drop, verdict `averted`, ticks, since} on the plan and the two readings on the recent list -- the number this lane is judged by; the tick then plans on its own reading as today (on 1177's shape the increase the recovery asks for is refused `snapshot_stale` / `drift` as any increase is without an agreeing fresh read; nothing bought, nothing sold, 719 held). (D) NOT TOUCHED: entries; the sign flip's flatten and close (the flip is the reopen mechanism; `_maybe_close_episode` hashed); the frozen exit (E5, `_frozen_exit`, its own reading); the loss stop; the day cap; E15's witness (the drop is judged after it admits, never instead of it); E12b's hold; E24's hand adoption; `_act`, `_exit_take`, `_flatten_vanished`, `_maybe_close_episode`, `_fast_gate`, `_fast_book`, `_confirm_gone`, `_net_for`, `_drift_for` (hashed against 09b35cd in the tests). THE FAST TICK is the same rule through the same function (`_fast_book` -> `_tick_book`): an exit on the fast path sized from a drop is held the same way, on the fast tick's own per-market read.

THE NAMES. Census, ONE block of four inserted before E19's `drift_smaller_open` (keys[-13]) and `registered_no_increase` (keys[-12]), after E24's four (now keys[-21:-17]): keys[-17:-13] = `exit_unconfirmed` (a reduce / the paired flatten that is not the flip's, sized from a sudden drop, held this tick: the venue still shows the old net, or its position could not be read), `exit_confirmed` (the venue's own position shows the drop: the exit fires this tick), `exit_confirm_expired` (the drop persisted past MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_MAX_TICKS held ticks: the exit fires anyway), `exit_flap_averted` (a held drop reversed in our reading before it fired: no order). The tail pins that count from the end move by four (test_mirror_live_worker -122: -> -126: with the CENSUS literal gaining the four names in place, -123 -> -127; test_e9_review_pins -122:-118 -> -126:-122, -123 -> -127; test_e17 -54:-46 -> -58:-50; test_e20 -46 / -45 / -44 -> -50 / -49 / -48; test_e14b -45 / -44 -> -49 / -48, -38:-35 -> -42:-39, -31 / -30 / -29 / -24 -> -35 / -34 / -33 / -28; test_e14's +31 offset from `take_in_band` now `exit_unconfirmed` and +35 `drift_smaller_open`, -44 -> -48; test_e19's predecessor chain from `drift_smaller_open` now the four names then E24's four, each older link by four; test_e24's own block -17:-13 -> -21:-17, -23:-17 -> -27:-21, -31 -> -35, -35:-31 -> -39:-35; test_e23 -23:-17 -> -27:-21 and its older links by four; test_fill_t1 / t2 / x1 / c11 / c9 / c16, test_e21, test_e22 by four each). Plan: `exit_confirm` = {prev, net, drop, threshold, snap, snap_age_s, snap_why, ticks, since, prev_target, kind, verdict: confirmed | unconfirmed | expired | averted, held {kind, qty, price} and cap on a hold}; carried unchanged on a tick where the drop still stands but no exit was judged (an increase, E12b's or E15's own hold, a `market_unreadable` tick); `exit_ref` = {net, at} on a quiet skip's plan and on a `market_unreadable` plan; the row's `last_reason` / the plan's `reason` `exit_unconfirmed` when nothing of his exit rests. Decision: none (the hold places nothing; 059's list stands as section 47 left it). Heartbeat: the census names and the `recent` entries `exit_unconfirmed` {prev, net, snap, ticks, qty}, `exit_confirm_expired` {prev, net, snap, ticks, qty}, `exit_flap_averted` {prev, net, ticks, ledger}. No migration (061 the newest); no preset touched (`book=<id>` prints `exit_confirm` inside `last_plan`).

THE RAILS, each pointing the ONE way the environment may move it, which is toward MORE confirmation -- never fewer holds: `MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_SHARES` = capped_env(1000.0, floor 1.0) and `MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_PCT` = capped_env(0.30, floor 0.0) -- a LOWER threshold judges more falls sudden, the defaults are the ceilings; `MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_S` = min_wait_env(600.0) -- a LONGER window counts an older reference as this fall's; `MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_TOL_PCT` = capped_env(0.5, floor 0.0) -- a LOWER tolerance asks the venue to show more of the drop; `MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_MAX_TICKS` = int(min_wait_env(3)) -- a HIGHER count holds an unconfirmed drop longer before it fires anyway. No `env_switch`: OFF is not a state; today's behaviour -- no confirmation -- is NOT reachable from a shell (reaching it would raise a threshold, shorten the window or cut the wait: a code change with a review), and at the defaults a fall the venue shows fires on the same tick, so his real exits are today's. The defaults from the rows: the flap 5,211.0 / 61.5 % (5,334.8 / 62 % from the plan before that) is sudden; the real cuts of 75 % (1156) and 31 % (1138) are judged and confirmed the tick the venue shows them; 1177's 17:04:57Z crossing (113.8 %) is a sign flip and never judged; the 2,880 / 25.3 % first step of the 17:04:57Z cut and 1075's 23 % are under 30 % and take today's path with no hold; the 1,000-share floor keeps the rule off falls of under 100 shares of ours at ratio 0.1. THE WINDOW 600 s against the day's ticks: 1177's judged plans were 37-58 s apart (rows 148-153: 52 / 37 / 56 / 41 / 58 s), so the flap's 37 s sits well inside it; but the day's tick at 54-64 live books was 76.9 s (hourly_2030 row 426, `tick_s`), at which a quiet book's rotation (QUIET_EVERY_TICKS 9) is 9 x 76.9 = 692 s, PAST the window -- so at the busy hours a quiet book's drop found on the read after its rotation is NOT judged (today's path, no hold; the window covers the hot books, which are read every tick, and quiet books only while the tick is under 600 / 9 = 66.7 s). 0.5 asks the venue for half the drop. MAX_TICKS 3: three held ticks are 3 x 76.9 = 231 s at that tick; 1177's flap was read low on EXACTLY three judged plans (17:16:34, 17:17:30, 17:18:11; rows 151-149) and recovered on the fourth (17:19:09, row 148), so the default has NO margin on that fixture -- a phantom one read longer (or one tick faster) fires `exit_confirm_expired` on the fourth tick and sells the 393 as the day did; the census counts it and the plan names it (`snap`, `snap_why`); the operator lengthens it from a shell (min_wait_env), and a longer code default is a reviewed constant, not the environment's (the fold kept 3: the brief's value, and each added tick is one more tick of delay on a real exit the venue lags). An environment line of MIRROR_EXIT_CONFIRM_MAX_TICKS large enough is a permanent hold on that drop -- inside the house rule (a wait may be lengthened), against this lane's own "never for good": a shell decision, named here. Untouched: the exit tolerance, the take band, the rest cent, E15's witness, the E16 gates, the E18 rest life, the game cap, the day cap, the loss stop switch (docs 65), the sign flip's flatten and close (never guarded).

FAIL CLOSED, by input. His net unreadable this tick -> the tick's own path before this rule (nothing new). No reference (a book's first plan; a plan written with no reading: `market_unreadable`, the terminal skip, the grammar echo; a restart onto such a row) -> no drop judged -> today's path. A malformed hold record -> no hold: today's reference. The per-market read unreadable, stale, capped, unstamped or older than the newest fill -> `snap` None -> the exit HELD `exit_unconfirmed`, never fired on a reading nobody made -- and never held for good: past MAX_TICKS held ticks `exit_confirm_expired` fires (the snapshot unread for three ticks in a row, or read and still disagreeing: we follow our reading, as today, three ticks late). A reading and a venue figure that part on the SIGN (today's smaller-of-two reads him at 0) -> the paired flatten at target 0 on the SAME side is a drop of the whole reading, held until the venue shows the crossing -- at which the reading has crossed, the plan is a sign flip, and the flatten fires as today (never judged). E12b's or E15's hold on the same tick -> theirs first, nothing of this rule's (the record rides on unchanged while the drop stands). A hold whose plan is not a reduce next tick (an increase, a flatten that keeps its own reader) -> the record carried, the count unmoved. A `market_unreadable` tick -> the plan carries the standing hold and the reference (as the quiet skip does: `exit_ref` and the `unconfirmed` `exit_confirm` on the unreadable plan, the count unmoved); E15's own reset reference holds the next readable tick `reduce_unwitnessed`, and the next admitted reduce is judged against the reading BEFORE the drop (without the carry his next reducing row after the blip -- a 10-share buy -- let E15 admit and the whole phantom sold, 394 @0.29 in the review's probe). The terminal skip and the grammar echo still write a plan with no reading; both are pre-trade or post-close paths on which no exit is sized. The frozen exit, the confirmed vanish, the sign flip's flatten and the flatten-all never read it. Nothing here sends an order that today would not send; every hold is a plan of kind reduce / flatten_paired with qty 0 and a name.

THE TESTS (`backend/tests/test_e25_exit_confirm.py`, 31; the worker suite's long world on the E15 harness: a long book at ratio 0.1, the whole-book walk STALE so the per-market read is the venue's word and `WALK` is a snapshot that cannot be read). The rules pure: the day's figures (the flap sudden by the plan before, 8,473.8 -> 3,262.8 in 37 s: 5,211.0 against 2,542.14, and by the fixture's 8,597.6 in 89 s: 5,334.8 against 2,579.28; 123.8 not; the 2,880 step 25.3 % not; the 12,960 crossing sudden on the pure rule; 1156's 75 % and 1138's 31 % on the short axis sudden, 1075's 23 % not; a fall AT the threshold exactly -- 1,000 on 2,000 -- not past it, one millionth more sudden); a rise never a drop and the short axis the mirror image; the threshold's floor and share (a lowered floor judges, a raised share cannot be reached by the rail); the window at 600 exactly and past it, a lengthened window; every unreadable None; the confirmation on the leg within the tolerance (the old net False, the new True, below it True, half the drop at the tolerance, past it False, the short's mirror image, unread None); the rails' defaults, sources, `__all__`, the env lowering / raising each the one way. ## 68. E26 -- the chain listener sweeps the logs the socket did not deliver; the poller's full page walks to a row it has seen (2026-09-09, FILL lane 26)

THE ROWS (hard2/book_1177_1740.txt; the book aec-wta-qinzhe-eleryb-2026-09-08, BUY_LONG, ratio 0.1, row 4). The chain path has no per-tx key that halves an order: the legacy decoder SUMS one wallet's same-token legs in a receipt into one row or the selector REFUSES the receipt whole; a 2,880-only chain row means fills two and three were in other txs this listener landed no chain row for. Whether their logs reached the socket is NOT settled by the files (the review's MEDIUM-2): the same fvv row counts ONE s1 row of exactly 5,211 shares on this book, and the judged plans read a 5,211-share `bought_complement` reduction on the same token at 17:16:25Z (rows 149-151, exit_at 1788974185); the emitter proceeds only on a 'refused' receipt claim or no claim (s1_emitter.py 1637-1640), so if that row is the third fill's tx the socket DID deliver its log and the receipt path refused it -- a shape the sweep re-reads and refuses again, the poller carrying it as today. The notes' operator query on the four rows' tx_hash decides it; until it is run the lane claims no reading of the 17:04:57 order, only the mechanism. The listener swept nothing between reconnects: `backfill` ran only at (re)connect, and a quiet socket was re-subscribed only after 60 s of silence. Beside it, "POLLER page full (100)" fired on every poll of every whale with 100 lifetime trades -- 22 lines in the 7 s of hard2/wlog_6m_1734.txt, RN1 at 17:34:33.822 and 17:34:37.182 (rows 1037 / 1082, every ~3.4 s) -- because the page is his newest 100 whatever their age; it meant nothing.

THE RULE. (A) THE SWEEP (`ingestion/chain.py`). `ChainListener.ensure_sweep_task()` starts one side task (`chain.sweep`) from `run()` after every successful subscribe, beside the emitter's own; `_sweep_loop` sleeps CHAIN_SWEEP_S and calls `_sweep_once`, which reads the cursor (`_load_cursor`) and the tip (`_current_block`) and runs `_get_logs` -- the listener's OWN filter, same addresses, same topics -- over [start, min(tip - CHAIN_SWEEP_CONFIRM_BLOCKS, start + CHAIN_SWEEP_MAX_BLOCKS - 1)], where start is the block after the sweep's OWN last end (`_sweep_end`, this process's memory; the review's HIGH-1): the cursor is the block of the newest log ANY path handled, so a socket log landing after the last sweep would move it past blocks the sweep never read -- a log the socket dropped in one of them, or in the SAME block as the one it delivered, would sit behind it for good. The first sweep opens at the cursor block itself (its siblings); a lag past CHAIN_SWEEP_MAX_BLOCKS (the catch-up backfill owns that gap, as today) resumes at the cursor block; a block the socket handled is re-read for free (`_v3_seen` drops its txs before any RPC). A FILL_V3 entry naming a roster wallet whose tx is NOT in `_v3_seen` is `found` and handed to `_handle_v3` DIRECTLY -- never through `_handle_log`, so `emitter_observe` and `shadow_observe` see only what the socket delivered and a swept log is never a second decoder's input; `backfill`'s listener-wide `_shadow_replay` flag is NOT used (it would make the emitter abstain on a live socket log landing mid-sweep). A tx the socket handled is dropped on `_v3_seen` BEFORE any RPC. A found tx takes the whole existing path: the claim, one receipt, the decoders, the (tx, whale, asset, source chain/s1) pre-probe, ingest as source 'chain', the E9 wake (section 30). A legacy / v2 entry goes to `_handle_log` as `backfill` sends it. The swept end is saved as the cursor (`_save_cursor`, GREATEST; the socket's own saves stand) and remembered as `_sweep_end` only when no entry's handler raised. One lock (`_sweep_lock`) is held by the sweep and by `run()`'s catch-up `backfill`, so the two never interleave. The sweep is skipped while `_last_throttled` (counted `skipped_throttled`; the reconnect's own rule: no RPC calls while being rate-limited) and silently while the socket is not subscribed (`_subscribed`, set after the ack, cleared at the loop head, on the 60 s quiet timeout and on the error path). RPC cost: two calls per sweep when the socket is healthy (eth_blockNumber + eth_getLogs; 3,600 / 15 = 240 sweeps an hour, ~480 calls) plus one receipt per found tx. (B) THE HONEST PAGE (`ingestion/poller.py`, `poll_wallet`). After the batch pre-probe: when the page is full (100) and NO key of it is seen -- the only page that can hide an older new row behind it -- the next page is fetched with the same params plus `offset` (the reconciler's own request shape: `{user, limit 100, offset N, takerOnly false}`), at most POLL_OVERFLOW_PAGES more, stopping at the first page holding a seen key or fewer than 100 rows; every row walks the same per-row containment (`_parse_page`, the first page's loop verbatim), the same pre-probe and the same ingest loop. The WARNING moved: "POLLER page overflow for <whale>: <n> pages, no row seen -- the venue may hold older rows this poll did not reach" fires ONLY when the bound was reached with every page all-new; `_PAGE_OVERFLOW` / `page_overflow_counts()` count that and an unreadable overflow page, beside `_PAGE_FULL` / `page_full_counts()`, which stay counters and no longer log. A page with one seen key makes no second request.

THE NAMES. Census: NONE (`CENSUS_KEYS` untouched; every from-end pin stands). Heartbeat: chain_listener detail `sweep` = {runs, found, handled, failed, skipped_throttled, last_span, last_at} (`found` = txs the socket did not handle, one per tx on its first log: the number the lane is judged by; `handled` = the delta of `ingested` over the sweep, so rows the pre-probe and ingest admitted, legacy rows and a socket row landing mid-sweep included; the v3_* keys stand); poller `page_overflow` per whale on the ok beat beside `new`. Plan / decision word / refusal name: none (059's list stands as section 64 left it). Log lines: "chain sweep A..B: N tx the socket did not hand over" (info, on a found > 0), "chain sweep ... failed" / "tip or cursor unreadable" / "non-list body" (warning), "POLLER page overflow for ..." (warning, the bound alone), "POLLER overflow page at offset N for <whale> unreadable (...) -- the first page's rows stand" (warning). No migration (061 stays the newest).

THE RAILS (read through analytics/mirror_live_rules.py's three readers at module level, as workers/whale_exits.py 57 does; the import cycles nothing -- chain and poller import the rules module, which imports analytics/mirror and ingestion/shadow_v2, neither of which imports chain or poller). `CHAIN_SWEEP = env_switch("CHAIN_SWEEP", True)` -- OFF is today byte for byte: no task. `CHAIN_SWEEP_S = min_wait_env("CHAIN_SWEEP_S", 15.0)` -- longer only (5 -> 15, 60 -> 60, junk -> 15). `CHAIN_SWEEP_CONFIRM_BLOCKS = min_wait_env("CHAIN_SWEEP_CONFIRM_BLOCKS", 2.0)` -- deeper only. `CHAIN_SWEEP_MAX_BLOCKS = capped_env("CHAIN_SWEEP_MAX_BLOCKS", 2000.0, floor=1.0)` -- `backfill`'s own step; a lower span leaves an older gap to the reconnect backfill and the reconciler, as today. `POLL_OVERFLOW_PAGES = capped_env("POLL_OVERFLOW_PAGES", 2.0, floor=0.0)` -- 0 is today's one page with the count. The data-API ceiling (ratelimit.py) and the RPC backoff are untouched: (B)'s extra page is one normal-lane request only on an all-new full page.

FAIL CLOSED. (A) A getLogs that raises (HTTP >= 400, an RPC error), a non-list body, an unreadable tip (a 429 on eth_blockNumber) or a missing cursor -> `sweep.failed` + 1, the cursor unchanged, no receipt, no row: the next sweep re-reads the same span. A swept log whose receipt cannot be fetched or decoded -> today's refusal path inside `_handle_v3` ("v3 receipt fetch failed" / "v3 fill undecodable", counted on the beat, no retry; the poller carries it), the cursor advances. A handler that raises on one entry (the database gone at ingest) -> `failed` + 1, the other entries still walked, the cursor NOT advanced past the span -- a v3 tx whose handler raised is already in `_v3_seen`, so this process's re-read lands nothing for it (the poller carries it; a restart's catch-up re-reads it from the cursor); a legacy / v2 log is re-handled. A tx already handled -> no RPC, no row. A chain row landing AFTER its poll twins -> the read-side window collapses the twins and his net is the same sum (D1: the chain row is the wallet's net legs): pinned on the scratch database -- `his_fills` reads 2,880 + 4,869 + 5,211 once each with the three poll rows in dup_rows -- never a double count, and NEVER a second chain / s1 row for one (tx, wallet, asset) (the pre-probe, restart-proof: a second sweep of the same span with `_v3_seen` empty lands nothing). A span with nothing confirmed past the cursor (end < start) -> no getLogs. A v3 log naming no roster wallet -> not found, no RPC. Throttled -> `skipped_throttled`, no call; not subscribed -> nothing. (B) An overflow page that raises or is not a list -> the first page's rows stand, `page_overflow` counted, its own warning, no raise out of the cycle; an unreadable pre-probe is not an all-new page (the review's MEDIUM-1): the first page's probe raising -> one page, as today, no walk, no count; an overflow page's probe raising -> the walk ends there, its rows standing (ingest's ON CONFLICT is the gate), `page_overflow` counted, its own warning; the round-23 / 24 / 36 / 37 guards read the first page only and are 09b35cd's byte for byte (the first page's text with the page-full block excised hashes to 2af4512c434873d5 on both trees; the ingest loop from `new = 0` to 919891d91205d0d4). A page with one seen key -> no second request and no warning; the walk never reads past POLL_OVERFLOW_PAGES; a junk row on an overflow page costs one row. No rail widened.

THE TESTS (`backend/tests/test_e26_chain_sweep.py`, 26; `backend/tests/test_e26_poll_overflow.py`, 16; the review's `backend/tests/test_e26_review_pins.py`, 9: a socket log landing after the last sweep does not hide an older dropped one, the first sweep reads the cursor block's sibling, a lag past MAX resumes at the cursor block and within it continues, a failed sweep keeps its own end, nothing confirmed past the last sweep makes no call, an unreadable first-page probe is one page, an unreadable overflow-page probe ends the walk, and the fold's two -- a RAISING handler leaves `_sweep_end` unmoved as it leaves the cursor unsaved (so the next sweep re-reads the span), and a lag of exactly MAX blocks is read whole from the sweep's own end while one block more resumes at the cursor; the fakes are test_chain_v3.py's `_ts` / `_erc20` and test_poller_poll_wallet.py's `_FakePool` / `fake_polite_get`). THE ACTOR, from the code on 6c0830d (the notes carry the same reading). The `mirror_live: {...}` dict line is printed by main() alone, after tick_once returns and after the heartbeat write, when the stats carry ops or an abandon (15813-15815); a fast tick (fast_tick_once, run by _fast_run) publishes nothing but its own failure line, and a skipped or overlapping tick_once returns _new_stats() with ops 0 and prints no dict line. So the lines at 22:50:02 and 22:50:09 are two COMPLETED FULL ticks. IN ONE PROCESS NO PATH CAN PLAN THIS BOOK ON A STALE DICT: tick_once holds _TICK_LOCK from 14419 across step O (14876), the cancel re-read (14879), step B (14895), the walk (14911) and the candidate stage; fast_tick_once holds _FAST_LOCK and _TICK_LOCK for its whole run (15627-15697) on the same event loop (workers/all.py gathers every loop into one, 378 / 384), so no fast tick's booking lands between step B and the walk's lock; step O and the cancel re-read book off fresh rows (5781, 5752) BEFORE step B; a fill the walk books inside _tick_book updates the SAME dict (book.update, 4732); _woken_first lists each row once and _SQL_BOOK_COLS is a plain read of mirror_books (no join), so one tick never holds two dicts of one book; nothing outside the worker writes the ledger (live_executor 6417 / 6467 write the open INSERT and standing_row_id). A fast tick AFTER tick A could not have placed 8148 either: tick A held the lock until after 22:50:02.2 (its dict line, 22:50:02.297, follows the heartbeat write) while 8148 was inserted at 22:50:01 (row 310; placed_at is the column default now(), migration 047 line 108), and a fast tick after tick A is refused `fill_after_walk` (_last_filled, 14467 / 15336) and re-reads the row (15416). The one actor the code admits is a SECOND mirror_live PROCESS, each with its own locks: E26 (93ea82b) went live at 22:48:45Z (6c0830d's commit text), 74 s before 8147, and a deploy runs the new instance beside the old one until the old is stopped; two full ticks' dict lines 7.3 s apart, each placed_take 1 / filled_take 1 over the same 32 live books and 6 open orders, are two processes each completing one tick. The files cannot prove it (the log lines carry no instance id; no 'mirror_live up' line inside 22:49:50-22:51:00): the operator read that settles it is render-ops `deploys` for sportsassets-workers (the 93ea82b row's live time against the previous deploy's deactivated time) beside ingestion_state.workers_boot (all.py 355-368). What (A) guarantees whichever process booked second: its guarded write misses on the row's 692 (not the 785 its dict read), the row is re-read and the fill re-booked at 692 - 93 = 599, the ledger equal to the venue's, no freeze. What (B) does under that actor: it narrows the window from "step B to the walk's lock" (seconds) to "the other process's plan to its booking" (8147: 22:49:58.10 to 22:50:00, rows 367-369 / 309), and it closes nothing in one process, where the dict cannot be stale -- the test fixture's hook on _lock_for is a model of the second process, not of any actor in this one.

THE RULE. (A) THE LEDGER WRITE IS GUARDED (no switch: a guard on a write is not a knob). _SQL_BOOK_LEDGER_BUY and _SQL_BOOK_LEDGER_SELL gain `AND ledger_net = $6` / `AND ledger_net = $5`: the integer figure the booking was COMPUTED FROM (`_ledger_guard(bk)` = int(round(_book_state(bk).ledger_net)), the dict's ledger_net AS READ before this fill, rounded exactly as the write rounds; never the target, never the venue). _book_fill runs its one transaction in a loop of at most two attempts on `bk` (the dict, then the row as re-read): the mirror_orders cursor byte for byte (`_Rebook` on 0 rows as before), the standing-row statement byte for byte (le._book_mirror_buy / _book_mirror_sell, the sell's ledger argument the figure `bk` carries), the booking from `_book_state(bk)`, the guarded write -- 0 rows -> `_LedgerStale`: the transaction ROLLS BACK (the cursor advance with it: the order row keeps its booked figure), the row is re-read inside _book_fill (`_ledger_reread`: `_sql_book_read(t)` on the book's id, under the lock the caller already holds), and the booking is recomputed on the fresh state and written ONCE more with the fresh guard. The retry landed -> `ledger_stale_reread` (a `recent` entry with ledger_was / ledger_now / order_row) and the fresh booking's figures carried through the dict (book.update as before, from the FRESH booking's state). A second miss, a re-read that raises, finds no row, or reads a ledger_net that is not a number -> NOTHING booked: `ledger_stale_refused` (a `recent` entry; one log line per process naming the book, the dict's figure and the row's), the return 'refused:ledger_stale'; the order row's booked_filled stands where it was, so _finish_order's own unbooked-fill path (5372-5378 on 6c0830d: "the venue filled more than the ledger booked ... the row stays 'unknown' and is re-read until they book") carries it to the next tick's step O, which reads a fresh row and books it off that. _book_hand_reduce (E24) takes the same guard, the same one re-read and retry, the same two names. (B) THE WALK PLANS ON THE ROW AS IT STANDS, behind rules.MIRROR_WALK_REREAD. In _walk_books' _game(), after `async with _lock_for(book["id"])` and before _tick_book, `_walk_reread(t, book)` reads the row again (`_sql_book_read(t)`, the fast tick's own read) and replaces the dict's DB columns IN PLACE (`book.update(fresh)` on the same object the per-game index holds, so a sibling's room reads the fresh figure too); the walk-time keys the dict carries stay -- on 6c0830d step B's dict carries only the columns _sql_books_open returns, and every non-column key _tick_book reads (`_held`, `_fills`, `_frozen_venue`, `_frozen_on_fill`, `_drift_smaller`, `_adopted`, `_venue_dust`, `_catchup`) is written inside _tick_book or on the open path after it, so nothing of the walk's is lost. The row missing, or a row that left the walk's population (state 'closed') -> the book is SKIPPED this tick under `walk_row_gone` (never a send; the next tick's step B reads the truth); the re-read raising -> skipped under `walk_row_unread` (a read that cannot be made buys and sells nothing), logged once per process; a FROZEN row is walked as today (the frozen exit is the walk's, E5; 'gone' is the walk's population, never 'frozen'). When ledger_net, open_order_id, state, frozen_reason or venue_net differ between the dict and the row (`_WALK_REREAD_COLS`; None-safe, numbers as numbers) -> `walk_row_moved` and `_recent(book id, "walk_row_moved", ledger_was=, ledger_now=, cols=)` so the mirror-tick's recent block names it; unchanged -> no census, no log. The skipped book is marked walk-done and judged by the streak exactly as a book that raised is (the `finally`), and marked seen (t.books_seen) so the candidate stage never reads its market as bookless on the tick it was skipped -- _tick_book's first line, which a skipped book never reaches; a book whose re-read raises is not read at all that tick (no quote, no per-market read, no exit on it), where 6c0830d read it off step B's dict: the one-tick cost the fail-closed rule accepts, pinned in test_mirror_live_worker's book-memo test. OFF is 6c0830d's walk byte for byte: the dict as read at step B. Cost: one row read per live book per tick -- 33 live books at 23:39Z (tick_2339 row 270) against the 23:00 hour's tick_med 16.7 s / p90 26.3 s over 43 ticks and the 22:00 hour's 14.2 s / 22.6 s over 24 ticks (h2317_db rows 137-138); measured by nothing new -- the E10 wall block's `books_data_wall` carries it (the read is inside the `books` stage, under the per-book lock). (C) NOT TOUCHED (hashed against 6c0830d in the tests): _fast_book's own re-read, _fast_gate, _act, _entry_take, _place, _place_reserved, _exit_take, _frozen_exit, _reconcile_open, _reconcile_placing, _cancel_reread_row, _tick_book, _finish_order, _book_delta, _ioc_reread, _fast_candidate, _walk_candidate, _fast_tick, fast_tick_once, _tick; the rules module byte for byte but for the one switch and its comment (no rule of sizing, pricing or refusal moves: this lane changes WHAT ROW a plan reads and WHEN a write lands, never what the plan says); every rail, every census name in place, E25's confirmation, E27's band, E16's freeze rule, E5's register, E23's cancelled-row adoption, E24's hand; the mirror_orders cursor and _SQL_ORDER_CASH byte for byte; the standing-row statements byte for byte (le._book_mirror_sell / _book_mirror_buy hashed).

THE RULE (`rules.MIRROR_HAND_EXIT`; `mirror_live._hand_exit_mark`, `_hand_exited`, `_hand_exit_retry`, `_hand_held_candidate`, `_load_hand_exits`, `_hand_exit_memo_write`; the paragraph over `_STATE_HAND_EXIT`). (A) THE MARK. When `_hand_net` books an adopted hand reduce (`out == "booked"`, beside the `hand_adopted` emit) -- on a live book, on a frozen book (marked too, so the market stays off after a thaw), and through `_hand_closing` on a closing book -- the book is HAND-EXITED: the plan's `hand_exit` = {at, shares, px, orders, ledger_after, memo} (carried by `_write_plan` from the prior plan onto every later plan of the book, the way `catchup` is, and by the quiet skip beside `reduce_ref`; re-read from the row on every later tick and after a restart), the process flag `book["_hand_exit"]` for the rest of the tick (which the adoption tick ends at once -- the flag is belt and braces; the row and the memo are the records), and ONE durable memo -- ingestion_state `mirror_hand_exit` = {"<whale>:<condition_id>": {at, book_id, slug, shares, px}}, a dict, at most `HAND_EXIT_MEMO_MAX` (500) entries with the oldest `at` dropped past it, read ONCE per tick at the tick's start into `t.hand_exits` (`_load_hand_exits`, after the boot memos in `_tick` and after the guards in `_fast_tick`: E7's read idiom -- unreadable or not an object -> None, named once per process) and written through `_write_state` right after the booking (`_hand_exit_memo_write`: the dict this tick read plus the entry; a memo this tick could not read is read once more before the write so no other market's entry is lost; the whole read-modify-write under `_HAND_EXIT_LOCK` -- the review's HIGH-1: the walk ticks games in parallel, and two marks in one tick each copied the dict before the other's write landed, so the second write dropped the first's entry and, by the release rule below, re-opened that market). Census `hand_exit` once per adoption that marks; a failed write -> `hand_exit_write_failed` (counted every time, logged once per process), the record written with `memo` False, and the write made again on a later tick that holds the book from its row while the memo reads without its entry (`_hand_exit_retry`; landed, the carried record reads `memo` True). (B) NO ADD ON A HAND-EXITED BOOK. In `_tick_book`, judged right after the whale-level `_increases_refusal` and BEFORE `_increase_recheck`'s reads (the source admission, the edge and cell gates, the short gate: database reads, no venue call): the book is hand-exited when the process flag is set, when the memo this tick read names (whale, condition), or when the row's last plan carries `hand_exit` and the memo either could not be read this tick or does not yet carry the row's entry (its write pending) -- and, the memo readable and naming nothing while the row's record says its write had landed, the book was RELEASED and is not held. The hold takes the increase refusal's own road, by its name: `inc_refusal` `hand_held` (`hand_held_unread` when neither the memo nor the row's plan can be read), so on an add plan (`rules.leg_action(book intent, side) == "add"`: an increase, the re-plan on the fast wake, both tick paths through the one `_tick_book`) `_act` is handed no plan and the name -- NO rest, NO IOC, NO band take, a standing ADD rest cancelled under `hand_held` through `rules.rest_decision`'s named cancel (the rest placed by a tick between the hand fill and its read; E24's own cancel covers the adoption tick), the plan's `hold` = the name beside the target it still computes (`book=<id>` reads `target -2460`, `ledger -1421`, `kind increase`, `hold: hand_held`, the row's reason `hand_held`), census `hand_held` once per held plan. The hold's name governs the row over the whale-level names (a desk-wide `mode_env_off` refuses the add as well; the specific reason is printed). A REDUCE, a flatten, a cover, the sign-flip close, E25's confirmed exit and E5's frozen exit run byte for byte as today (`_act`, `_frozen_exit`, `_exit_take`, `_flatten_vanished`, `_maybe_close_episode` hashed): the desk sold; his exits still move ours. A held book's reason is not `on_target`, so E6 reads it every tick (never quiet-skipped) -- one quote read and one per-market read a tick, as an E25 hold costs. (C) THE MARKET STAYS OFF. `_tick_candidate` refuses `hand_held` on a (whale, condition) the memo names -- BEFORE the mapping, before the terminal pre-check, before any venue read (no budget spent), the memo's slug on the context so the 054 row carries it -- recorded through `_walk_candidate`'s `_note_candidate_refusal` as every refusal is (a transition-only row; `hand_held` NOT in `_CAND_NOT_RECORDED`), never `reopen_refused` (no quote read, `mark` absent: section 50 (b)); the wake reaches it through `_fast_candidate` -> `_walk_candidate`. When the memo cannot be read this tick (`t.hand_exits` None) the newest CLOSED book's plan decides (`_SQL_BOOK_FLIP`, lane 5's row, one read bounded by `CAND_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT_S`, cached on the candidate's context as `_reopen_of` caches): its `hand_exit` -> `hand_held`; that read raising, or the plan unreadable -> `hand_held_unread` for that candidate this tick (a read that cannot be made opens nothing); no closed book, or one without the mark -> the candidate as today. THE WHOLE MARKET, BOTH SIDES: a flip of his on a hand-exited market opens nothing (the desk took the play off; fail closed toward trading nothing on it) -- the owner's to widen (a per-side hold) or narrow. The memo holds for the market's life: an entry is dropped only by the bound (the oldest `at` past 500) or by the release; the terminal reading is NOT consulted at the memo read (E13's / lane 11's memos are per-process dicts the candidate walk checks after this refusal, and a terminal market opens nothing either way -- the entry is left; the cost is one dict key). (D) THE RELEASE. render-ops.yml `mirror-hand-release` (need_confirm; arg `<book_id>`, a bash regex `^[0-9]+$`; beside `mirror-register`, before it, so E5's register pin -- which slices from `mirror-register)` to `mirror-frozen)` and forbids `$ARG` past the register's `SQL=` -- stands): ONE statement (its own transaction under `psql -c`; pglast parses it as one SelectStmt) whose data-modifying CTEs remove the memo entry for the book row's `whale || ':' || condition_id` -- the entry for the book's MARKET, whichever book wrote it (an older book's id on the same market releases the current hold; the print names the entry's `book_id`) -- from the `mirror_hand_exit` dict (`s.value - b.k`, guarded `jsonb_typeof(s.value) = 'object' AND s.value ? b.k`) and the book row's `last_plan - 'hand_exit'` (guarded `x.last_plan ? 'hand_exit'`), and whose SELECT prints the entries it removed (`released: memo {...} plan {...}`, `memo_rows`, `plan_rows`) or `no hold on book N`, or `REFUSED: no mirror_books row with id N`; `updated_at` is not named (section 50: the fills-missed census reads the newest-UPDATED book on the condition); the `*)` help arm regenerated (`mirror-hand-release` after `cand-refusals`, before `mirror-register`); every read-only preset byte for byte (the file with this case block and its help token removed hashes to 6c0830d's 6969bae6aa1536c1). THE RELEASE RACE: a tick that read the row BEFORE the release still holds the record in memory and would write it back on its plan; `_write_plan` carries `hand_exit` from the prior plan only while the memo (readable this tick) names the market OR the record's own write is pending (`memo` False) -- a record whose write landed and whose key the readable memo no longer names is released and NOT carried back, so the release lands within one tick without a second run. (E) NOT TOUCHED (hashed against 6c0830d in the tests): E24's reading and arithmetic -- `_hand_net` with the ONE new call site excised hashes to dc37d19b62d8c6fd (6c0830d's), `_book_hand_reduce`, `_hand_closing`, `_hand_log_fills`, `_hand_adopted_record`; the hand ADD (`hand_explained`: explained, never sized -- unchanged); the freeze and the thaw (`_thaw`), the register, the flip close (`_maybe_close_episode`), every exit path (`_act`, `_exit_take`, `_flatten_vanished`, `_frozen_exit`), `_walk_candidate`, `_note_candidate_refusal`, `_note_reopen_refused`, `_reopen_of`, `_flip_since`, `_fast_book`, `_fast_candidate`, `_fast_gate`, `_cancel_open_for`, `_place`, `_place_reserved`, `_entry_take`, `_ioc_reread`, `_lost_fill_adopt`, `_disagree_fill_adopt`, `_quiet_skip`, `_load_cand_memo`, `_persist_cand_memo`; every rail; the rules module except the one switch line; E28's sites (`_walk_books`, `_book_fill`, `_SQL_BOOK_LEDGER_*`: E28 lands before this lane).
