THE RAILS. `MIRROR_HAND_EXIT = env_switch("MIRROR_HAND_EXIT", True)` in mirror_live_rules.py beside the other switches (env_switch 5 -> 6): OFF is 6c0830d byte for byte -- no mark, no memo read or written (`_load_hand_exits` reads nothing, `t.hand_exits` None, `_hand_exited` / `_hand_held_candidate` None), the re-add as today (the test drives 8320's shape under OFF: the 1,039 SELL_LONG rest at 0.31 the tick after the adoption); the environment may only turn it OFF (off / 0 / no / false; on / 1 / true / absent / blank / a typo read the default, as every switch here). `HAND_EXIT_MEMO_MAX = 500`, a module constant in the worker bounding the memo (a bound, not a knob: no rail reads it). No capped_env / min_wait_env moves (26 / 7, pinned in test_e19 / e23 / e24 / e27 as before). The closed-row read is bounded by `CAND_REFUSAL_WRITE_TIMEOUT_S` (5.0 s) like the refusal flush and the turn read; the memo read is one `ingestion_state` fetch per tick; the memo write one statement per mark.

FAIL CLOSED, item by item. The memo unreadable at the tick's start (the key raising, malformed, not an object) -> `t.hand_exits` None: a book's own row still holds it (the row is the primary record), a candidate falls to the closed-row read, that failing -> `hand_held_unread`, nothing opened; the write on the mark failing -> `hand_exit_write_failed`, the row's plan (`memo` False) and the process flag hold, the write made again on a later tick and landing (`memo` True); the switch OFF -> today; an adoption that did not book (`adopt_write_failed`, `refused:<why>`) -> no mark (nothing was adopted; E24's path as today); a hand ADD -> nothing new (explained, never marked); a hand reduce on a FROZEN book -> E24 as today (a frozen book adds nothing) and marked anyway, so the market stays off after a thaw; the release preset on a book with no hold -> `no hold on book N`, nothing changed; on an unknown id -> REFUSED, nothing changed; the row's last_plan unreadable -> the memo decides; both unreadable on a LIVE book -> the add is HELD `hand_held_unread` (a read that cannot be made buys nothing); the memo readable and naming nothing while the row's record says its write landed -> released (the operator's preset, or the memo key deleted by hand -- deleting the whole `mirror_hand_exit` key releases every hold whose write had landed: named here as the operator's own act); the bound dropping the oldest entry past 500 releases that market by the same reading (500 hand exits away; named, not guarded). Never a send on any unreadable input; a measurement never refuses the tick.

## 73. THE VENUE'S BODY, FROM THE CODE. The SDK's client (polymarket_us/client.py, `_handle_error_response`, lines 174-200) parses the error response's JSON into `body` (None when unparsable), reads `message` as `body.get("message") or body.get("error") or response.reason_phrase` (a dict body; the reason phrase or the text else) and raises `BadRequestError(message, response=response, body=body)` on a 400 (401 AuthenticationError, 403 PermissionDeniedError, 404 NotFoundError, 429 RateLimitError, 5xx InternalServerError, APIStatusError else); the exception (errors.py 12-70: `APIError.message` / `.body`, `APIStatusError.status_code` = response.status_code, `.response`) is what the adapter catches. The adapter's `pmus._post_only_refusal` (pmus.py 2430-2478) reads any APIStatusError with a 4xx status as `post_only_rejected` and carries in `raw`: `preview` (the order params), `status_code` (the int), `error` (str(exc.message or exc)[:500] -- the SDK's message, the venue's words), `error_type` (the exception's class name), `body` (the response JSON as parsed; a non-JSON-native object as str()[:500]); the second shape (`_post_only_cross`, 2565-2625: a 200 whose order came back ORDER_STATE_REJECTED with an EXECUTION_TYPE_REJECTED execution) carries `status_code` 200, `order_state`, `execution_type`, `post_only_cross` True, `order_id`, `reject_reason` (the SDK's `orderRejectReason`, types/orders.py:104) and `text`. So the raw DOES carry the body and the message on every 400; the worker's post_only_rejected branch (`_place_reserved`, 66144cf 13359-13391) wrote `_SQL_ORDER_STATE` and `_SQL_ORDER_REASON` (`post_only_rejected:<code>`) and never the receipt -- `_SQL_ORDER_REFUSED` with `_refusal_receipt(raw)` (1949-1983, U13's 4,000-character bound) is the refused-placement path right after it (`if not oid:`), which a post_only_rejected never reaches. THE VENUE'S DOCUMENTED 400 REASONS FOR A POST-ONLY GTC: none in the installed SDK -- a grep for post_only / postOnly / cross / reject over the package finds only types/orders.py:31 `ORDER_STATE_REJECTED`, :40 `EXECUTION_TYPE_REJECTED` and :104 `orderRejectReason: str`; no error catalogue, no crossing text, no reason list. So the nine identical 400s at a wire above the ask followed by an accepted tenth is a shape this lane cannot explain from the files or the SDK; it makes the next one READABLE: the body lands on the row and in the log, and the loop is bounded meanwhile.

THE RULE (`rules.MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF`, `rules.MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_S`; `mirror_live.POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_N`, `_post_only_receipt`, `_post_only_word`, `_post_only_log_once`, `_post_only_note`, `_post_only_held`; the paragraph over `POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_N`). (A) THE BODY IS KEPT. On the post_only_rejected branch of `_place_reserved`, after the state and reason writes as before, the rejected row's `receipt` is written from the adapter's raw through the SAME bound `_refusal_receipt` uses (U13's 4,000 characters, always valid JSON) onto the receipt merge `_SQL_ORDER_RECEIPT` (`ml-order-receipt`, the frozen-reduce excess's own statement) -- `_post_only_receipt(raw)`: ONLY the named fields `status_code`, `error_type`, `error`, `post_only_cross`, `execution_type`, `order_state`, `reject_reason`, `text`, `order_id`, and the body's HEAD (`body`: a string as it is, a JSON object dumped, at most `POST_ONLY_BODY_HEAD` = 600 characters with `body_truncated` past it) -- never the preview, a header or a key; a raw that is not a dict writes nothing (the receipt keeps NULL, as before); the write failing is logged once per process and changes nothing else. ONE WARNING per process per (book, status code) -- "book N post-only REJECTED <side> <slug> x<qty> @<wire> (<intent>): status <code>, the venue's body: {error_type, error, body, reject_reason, text}" -- `_post_only_log_once`, the set bounded at 2,000 keys. THE REASON keeps its shape `post_only_rejected:<code>` and gains one word when the body names it: `:cross` when `rules.take_arms(raw)` reads the crossing shape AND the raw's own `post_only_cross` is the bool True (`_post_only_word`) -- the adapter's second shape, built off the SDK's EXECUTION_TYPE_REJECTED; nothing in the installed SDK spells a crossing message, so no word is read off the message text and a 400's reason stays `:400` as today (the one existing pin that moves: the worker suite's 200-shape row now reads `post_only_rejected:200:cross`). The record is NOT behind the switch (a record is not a knob). `rules.take_arms`' truth table, the take arm (`_SQL_BOOK_ARM`), the 429 circuit (`_raw_rate_limit`), `_POST_ONLY_OK` / `_post_only_enabled`, `_SQL_ORDER_STATE` and `_SQL_ORDER_REASON` are untouched. (B) THE BACKOFF. `_post_only_note` counts CONSECUTIVE post_only_rejected placements per book at ONE side, wire AND status code (`_post_only_streak`, a process-local dict keyed by book id, bounded at `POST_ONLY_STREAK_MAX` = 500 books with the oldest `last_at` dropped: {n, side, wire, price, code, last_at, ledger, hold_since}) -- three IDENTICAL rejections; a different code starts a fresh count (a 429 is its own circuit and never counts toward a 400's streak; a raw with no code counts under None -- a rejection is a rejection), a different side or wire starts a fresh count. The count is RESET by an accepted placement (a rest or an IOC: the entry popped right after `_SQL_ORDER_PERSIST_ID` -- the one path every fill and every cancel of ours passes through first), by a plan whose side or wire differs (`_post_only_held` compares the plan's side and `_wire_for`'s wire), and by a ledger that moved since the last rejection (a fill, the desk's or ours: the entry carries the ledger it was counted at). When the count reaches `POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_N` = 3 (a module constant in the worker: a bound, not a knob) the book's REST is held for `rules.MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_S` (60.0 s) after the last rejection: `_post_only_held`, judged in `_tick_book` on the ADD branch alone and LAST among the holds -- after E29's hand hold, the whale-level refusals (`_increases_refusal`) and the recheck (`_increase_recheck`, `_short_open_refusal`): a hand_held book is never also post_only_backoff, and any named refusal governs the row before this one -- writes the plan's `hold: post_only_backoff` and `post_only_backoff` {since (the hold's first tick), until (the last rejection + the wait), n, code}, census `post_only_backoff` once per held plan, the `recent` entry {what post_only_backoff, book, n, code, until} once per hold; `_act` is still handed the plan and `_place_reserved` refuses the GTC add by the hold's name BEFORE any read or op (the short doors, the open-orders read, the room), returning `post_only_backoff` -- the row's last_reason. UNDER THE HOLD: the take inside E27's band and the at-level take are IOCs (an IOC is not post-only: the venue's refusal was of the REST) and fire as today through the same `_entry_take`; the accepted IOC resets the count, and the remainder's rest of that same tick is refused under the held plan (the planner's verdict governs the tick; the next tick's plan, unheld, rests it as today). Every exit -- a reduce, a flatten, a cover, the flip close, E25's confirmed exit, E5's frozen exit -- is a reduce and never reaches the add branch: byte for byte as today (`_act`, `_exit_take`, `_frozen_exit`, `_flatten_vanished`, `_maybe_close_episode` hashed). When the wait has passed the rest is tried ONCE MORE; a further rejection (n 4) restarts the wait from that rejection, so a book the venue keeps refusing costs one placement per wait instead of one per tick. A held book's reason is never on_target, so E6 reads it every tick (as an E25 or E29 hold). (C) THE SWITCH. `MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF = env_switch("MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF", True)`: OFF is 66144cf byte for byte for the backoff (`_post_only_held` None, the rest retried every tick; the count still measured); the receipt and the log are not behind it. (D) NOT TOUCHED (hashed against 66144cf in the tests): `rules.take_arms`' truth table, the take arm and its disarm, `_POST_ONLY_OK` / `_post_only_enabled`, the 429 circuit, `_SQL_ORDER_STATE` / `_SQL_ORDER_REASON` / `_SQL_ORDER_REFUSED` / `_SQL_ORDER_RECEIPT`, every exit path, E29's hold and memo, E28's sites, `_place` (the wrapper), `_act`, `_entry_take`, `_wire_for`, the adapter's two readers (`pmus._post_only_refusal`, `_post_only_cross`), the rules module but for the one switch line and the one wait line, render-ops.yml.

THE RAILS. `MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF = env_switch("MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF", True)` and `MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_S = min_wait_env("MIRROR_POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_S", 60.0)` in mirror_live_rules.py beside the other switches (env_switch 7 -> 8; min_wait_env 7 -> 8; capped_env 26 unmoved; test_e19 / e23 / e24 / e27 / e28 / e29 re-pinned): the environment may only turn the switch OFF (off / 0 / no / false; on / 1 / true / absent / blank / a typo read the default, env_switch's own rule) and only LENGTHEN the wait (30 -> 60.0, 120 -> 120.0, junk / -5 / 1e400 / absent -> 60.0, min_wait_env's own rule); both read at import and through the module at call time (a test's monkeypatch and the operator's setting apply at the next judgement). `POST_ONLY_BACKOFF_N = 3`, `POST_ONLY_STREAK_MAX = 500`, `POST_ONLY_BODY_HEAD = 600`: module constants in the worker (bounds, not knobs: no rail reads them; the worker reads no environment of its own for this lane). test_e28's 6c0830d excision gains this lane's block beside E29's (its pin stands); test_fill_c14's rules hash 7e521ec5ffa67f5f -> 68d94eb379d99563.

FAIL CLOSED, item by item. The raw unreadable (not a dict) -> the receipt keeps NULL, the reason `:None` (66144cf read `:None` on a falsy raw and RAISED on a truthy non-dict one -- `(raw or {}).get` on a list -- the book's tick failing; the lane reads `:None` on either), the count advances under code None (three of them hold the rest). The receipt write failing -> logged once per process, the row's state and reason as written, the count advanced, nothing else changes. The count dict unreadable (an entry that is not a dict) -> the entry dropped, no backoff this tick (today's behaviour). The switch OFF -> no hold, the rest retried every tick as today; the receipt and the log still written. The wait unreadable in the environment -> min_wait_env's own parse: the default 60 s; shorter than the default -> the default. A book with a hold and a fill arriving (the desk's or ours) -> the ledger moved: the count reset, the hold cleared on the next plan. An accepted placement (8576's shape) -> the count reset at once, no hold. The take inside the band or at his cent under the hold -> fires (an IOC); a reduce, a cover, a flatten, the flip close, E25's and E5's exits -> fire as today (never judged). E29's hand hold on a hand-exited book -> `hand_held`, never `post_only_backoff` (judged first; the streak stands untouched). A different status code, side or wire -> a fresh count, no hold on the mix. Never a send from any new path: the hold refuses a GTC, the record writes a row and a log line, the count is a dict; a measurement never refuses the tick.

THE TESTS (`backend/tests/test_e30_post_only_body.py`, 18, and the review's `backend/tests/test_e30_review_probes.py`, 6: the WARNING once per (book, status code); an exit rest at his cent and a frozen reduce rest going out on a book whose add streak is three deep; the word on the bool True alone; a hold on the prior plan judged afresh; an unhashable status code never raising out of the placement -- the worker suite's short world on the E5 pool). THE RULE. (A) THE TEAM-FIELD SUBJECT, nfl only, under the identity switch (`premap._c10_team_subject(his, code, names, board, on, title_team, trace) -> (his position, his title's team's position) | None`, pure; `premap.C10_TEAM_LEAGUE = "nfl"`, `premap.C10_SPREAD_LABEL = "premap_spread_team"`). Inside `_c3_pick_spread`, when the name step reads NOTHING (`mine` empty: 'seahawks' is not 'seattle seahawks') and `his['lg'] == 'nfl'` (the literal league token; cfb keeps the C4 / C6 chain byte for byte -- its asc questions name mascots and C6 certifies the school from `team_safe_name`; the venue's full names are the wrong instrument for the code rules, and `_c4_subject_by_code`, `_c4_subject_certified`, `_c6_team_subject`, `_c6_pair_by_event` are hashed against 0b24564), the new function runs INSTEAD of the C4 chain, which is never consulted on nfl. The witness, all of it the venue's own words on the venue's own rows, read with token-set equality (`pmus._yn_name_match`) or exact equality, never a prefix: (1) THE AEC ROW `aec-<code>-<a>-<b>-<date>` on the board (his slug as the venue dates it: C9's `nfl_date_read` has rewritten `global_slug` before `c3_pick` runs, so `his['date']` is the venue's) with EXACTLY two sides, each carrying side_norm, team_abbr and team_name, the two abbreviations being exactly {a, b} one each; a side's POSITION is its abbreviation's index in (a, b) -- never its listing order (the sides reversed bind the same, pinned). Row absent -> `spread:team-absent`; a side without an abbreviation, a name or a description -> `spread:team-absent`; abbreviations not exactly {a, b} (one code on both sides, a code that is neither, one side) -> `spread:team-unnamed`. THE ROW MUST AGREE WITH ITSELF: each side's description (the mascot) must be the tail of its own record's `team_name` (city + mascot: `seahawks` closes `seattle seahawks`, `49ers` closes `san francisco 49ers` -- true of all 30 records) -- else `spread:team-mascot-conflict`, a name beyond the lane's list, added because the lane's fail-closed item "a swapped aec (the sea dict on the patriots side) -> never a binding on the wrong side" has no other witness: with the dicts swapped every other read passes (his 'Seahawks' reads the side whose description is Seahawks, whose record says `ne`, and the sign table would buy 'Patriots cover -3.5' YES); the row's two fields disagreeing is refused BEFORE any position is read. (2) HIS OUTCOME (`_c3_norm(outcome)`) reads a side when `_yn_name_match(on, side_norm)` or `_yn_name_match(on, team_name)` or `on == team_abbr` (his feed's abbreviation, lower-cased, exact: 'TB' is `tb`, 'LA' is not `lar`); exactly one side, else `spread:team-outcome-unread` (none: the city 'Seattle' -- `team_safe_name` is NOT read, his feed never names it and 'los angeles c' is not a name; a prefix 'Seattle S'; a nickname 'Hawks' / 'Pats'; 'Cowboys') / `spread:team-outcome-ambiguous` (both). (3) HIS TITLE'S TEAM (the `_C3_SPREAD_TITLE_RE` group, `title_team`) the same way; exactly one side, else `spread:team-title-unread` / `spread:team-title-ambiguous`. (4) THE QUESTION'S NAMES (`names[0]`, `names[1]` from `_C3_ASC_Q_RE` on the row's own question) must each read exactly one side by `_yn_name_match` against `team_name` (or side_norm), and the SUBJECT `names[0]` must be the side at a and `names[1]` the side at b -- the grammar puts a in the subject slot (section 10.2 step 3); the venue naming b there, or a name reading both sides, is `spread:subject-conflict` (a name reading no side: `spread:team-question-unread`). (5) THE ASC SIDE WITNESS (`premap._c10_side_team(row, code, trace)`), after C3's sign table has chosen (sign_tok, side) and `want_id` and the row is in hand: the chosen row's chosen side must itself carry `team_abbr` equal to HIS team's code (the aec-certified one) -- the venue stating on the asc row which team that side backs -- else `spread:side-team-conflict`; the side stating no team -> `spread:side-team-absent` (fail closed: every NFL asc row on file states it, table 2). The second, independent witness of the side: the sign table derives it from the grammar, the row states it. (6) Then C3 byte for byte: the intent check, the line shear, the sign shear; matched_by `premap_spread_team`; the trace's `subject {certified: <the mascot at a>, code: a, via: 'team'}`, `subject_via 'team'`, `team {a: {abbr, side_norm, team_name}, b: {...}}` (both aec sides as read), `team_hits` / `title_hits` (the codes his outcome / title read), `side_team` (the chosen asc side's abbreviation), `his_team`, `his_signed` as today.
